 >>> MODEL NATURE (IMPORTANT): a PHENOMENOLOGICAL / EDUCATIONAL model. The engine is a REDUCED implementation
     of established models, CALIBRATED to the expected CLINICAL dynamics — it is NOT a predictive
     hydrodynamic model of a specific patient. simulateCanalith models a particle on an arc (overdamped
     Stokes motion + first-order cupular relaxation), WITHOUT full duct geometry of variable diameter, endolymph
     viscosity, transcupular pressure, fluid inertia, the distribution of MANY particles, or individual anatomy
     (full model: Squires/Hain/Stone [1]). It reproduces the EXPECTED clinical behavior, it does not predict the patient.
     Details → "BIBLIOGRAPHY AND MODEL CONSISTENCY" + "SIMPLIFICATIONS RELATIVE TO FULL MODELS". An EDUCATIONAL
     tool, not a diagnostic one. <<<
==========================================================================

This document describes the public and internal helper API of the engine and the adopted
conventions. Keep it updated as the engine evolves.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 CONVENTIONS AND COORDINATE SYSTEMS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Head frame:  x = right, y = up (skull), z = forward (nose).
World: "up" axis = +y; world gravity = (0,-1,0).
Sides (ear/side): "L" = left ear, "P" = right ear.
Canals (canal): "posterior", "horizontal", "anterior".

Quaternion: q = [w, x, y, z]; rotates HEAD vectors → WORLD
            (v_world = q ⊗ v_head ⊗ q*).

Nystagmus components (anatomical, patient frame), returned as {h, v, t}:
   h: + to the right / − to the left        (horizontal)
   v: + upward / − downward         (vertical)
   t: + upper poles to the right / − to the left  (torsional)

Ewald's laws used in the engine:
   I   — plane specificity: the fast-phase axis of a given canal (quickPhase)
   II  — horizontal canal: AMPULLOPETAL flow is excitatory; excitation > inhibition
   III — vertical canals: AMPULLOFUGAL flow is excitatory

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 PUBLIC API (returned from the module)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

— STAGE 0 (rule-based) ——————————————————————————————————————————————

isExcitatory(canal, flow) -> bool
   Whether the given flow direction excites the canal (Ewald's law II/III).
   flow: "ampullopetal" | "ampullofugal".
   Returns: horizontal → (flow==="ampullopetal"); vertical → (flow==="ampullofugal").

quickPhase(canal, ear) -> {h, v, t}
   Fast-phase axis for canal EXCITATION, ipsiversive to the ear (Ewald I).
   ear: "L"|"P". (ipsi = ear==="P" ? +1 : -1)
   horizontal → {h:ipsi, v:0,    t:0}      (horizontal toward the excited ear; torsion masked)
   posterior  → {h:0,    v:+0.81,t:ipsi}   (upbeat + torsion toward the ear; real v:t≈0.81:1)
   anterior   → {h:0,    v:-1,   t:0}      (PURE downbeat; geometric torsion ~0.74 MASKED — NYS_SHOW.anterior.t=false)
   NOTE — CLINICAL MASK (NYS_SHOW): quickPhase returns the UI convention for BPPV (anterior = pure downbeat,
   horizontal without torsion). The FULL geometry (anterior torsion PRESERVED, for lateralization) → NeuroVOR.qpFull()
   / Vestibular.nysMag(); used by the HINTS layer (vHIT saccade / verticalBeat / SCDS). See the "CLINICAL MASK" section.

nysMag(canal, ear) -> {h, v, t}
   Full geometric proportions of the nystagmus from REAL canal normals (CANAL_NORMALS), normalized
   to max=1. Does NOT apply the NYS_SHOW clinical mask (anterior-canal torsion PRESERVED) — cf. quickPhase
   (same geometry, masked to the BPPV convention). Source of the full geometry for NeuroVOR
   (qpFull → vHIT saccade / verticalBeat / pressureStimulus-SCDS). ear: "L"|"P".

nystagmus({canal, ear, excited=true}) -> {h, v, t}
   Fast phase for the given, excited/inhibited canal.
   Inhibition (excited=false) reverses the sign of all components.
   Future use: unilateral loss (excited:false), push-pull.

— STAGE 1 (static geometry) ————————————————————————————————————

gHead(q) -> [x, y, z]
   The gravity vector expressed in the head frame for orientation q.
   Upright-standing head (q=[1,0,0,0]) → [0,-1,0].

(qSupineYaw / qPitch — REMOVED 2026-08-05. They encoded the old pose convention: a fixed −100°
   hang for every lying pose. Clinical pose orientations are NO longer engine API — they are built by
   stepHeadQ(body, yaw, face) over the POSE_SPEC table in src/pose/maneuvers.js:
   headQ = Rz(roll)·Rx(pitch)·Ry(yaw+dyaw); every angle has its source in a sentence of the instruction.)

position({canal, side, variant, q}) -> {excited, mag, h, v, t}
   Static stimulation of the AFFECTED canal in head orientation q.
   side: "L"|"P"; variant: "canalo" (geotropic) | "cupulo" (apogeotropic) | "light" (since V12:
   light cupula, PERSISTENT geotropic — ONLY canal="horizontal", TypeError on the vertical canals;
   flips the sign of proj at the SAME evaluation point and projection zero — null point shared with "cupulo").
   Mechanism: projection of gravity onto the canal's excitation axis → flow → Ewald.
     drive = g · tangAt(G, φ_eval);  proj = G.exc · drive;  excited = proj>0 (ONE Ewald rule)
     φ_eval = restPhi(canal,side) for FREE debris (canalo; also cupulo of the VERTICAL canals — an
       explicit phenomenological choice, see the code comment) · CUPULA_DEG for HORIZONTAL-CANAL CUPULO/LIGHT.
       [2026-08-13, assessment II A1/V4: cupulolithiasis debris sits ON THE CUPULA, not at the free-debris
        rest (199.8°); the old proj<0 inversion held the Roll sign only by an antipodal coincidence
        (restPhi HC ~197° from the ampulla), while computing the null point on the WRONG side and
        reversing apo Bow&Lean against the Choung rule and the card own text. After the fix: apo Roll
        with an INTRINSIC asymmetry 0.832 (affected down) / 0.885 (healthy), null point at yaw +8..9°
        toward the AFFECTED ear (clinically 10–30°), apo B&L per the rule (bow→healthy, lean→affected —
        the arrow agrees with the label), pseudo-SN when sitting 0.052.]
     G.exc = +1 vertical canals (Ewald III: ampullofugal excites) · −1 horizontal (Ewald II).
     [2026-08-05] It used to be proj = g·GEXC[canal] (vertical) or g·uHC(side) (horizontal) — both of
     those axes are the tangent taken at φ=90°, i.e. at the very constant restPhi removed. The test
     card could therefore show a direction OPPOSITE to the dynamics of the same pose. GEXC and uHC
     have been DELETED.
   excited: bool; mag = |proj| (raw force); {h,v,t} = quickPhase with the sign of excitation.
   VALIDATED: Dix-Hallpike (posterior), Roll (geo/apo), APO Bow & Lean (consistent with the card since
   V4); CANALO Bow & Lean remains a watershed/positional-history matter (R8/R10 — plan V5).

— STAGE 2 (particle dynamics) ————————————————————————————————————————

simulateCanalith({canal, side, timeline, q0=null, phi0=null, settled=true, xi0=0, bond0=null, dt=0.05, tauP=6.5, tauC=5, gc=1.6, phiExit=null, fStat=0.04, adh=0.2, size="medium", rep=0, fatTau=2.0, fatFloor=0.06, crusArc=12, crusGrav=0.6}) -> [{t, xi, phi, exited}, ...] (+ a .final property)
   phiExit=null (default) = the MEASURED arc span per canal: ARC_SPAN {anterior 281.1 · posterior 318.8 ·
   horizontal 267.3}. (The crusFling/crusFlingRate parameters were REMOVED 2026-08-05 — see the crus chamber below.)
   VALIDATIONS (since 2026-08-13, assessment II A10): dt>0 · tauP>0 · tauC>dt/2 (Euler stability) ·
   gc>0 · fStat≥0 · adh≥0 · 0≤crusArc<phiExit−3 · crusGrav>0 — a clear RangeError instead of silent
   nonsense (gc<0 flipped the nystagmus side, crusArc>phiExit killed the test without a trace).
   since V3 also phi0 ∈ [CUPULA_DEG, phiExit] (described below); since V10 also bond0 ∈ [0,1] and finite xi0.
   Simulation of CANALITHIASIS: the otolith as a particle on the canal arc.
   [PHENOMENOLOGICAL MODEL — a proxy for the dynamics, not full hydrodynamics; see the "MODEL NATURE" header
    and "SIMPLIFICATIONS RELATIVE TO FULL MODELS".]
   timeline: array of segments [{q, tTrans, tHold, pivot}, ...]
       q      — target orientation (quaternion)
       tTrans — transition time to q [s] (slerp interpolation from the previous q)
       tHold  — hold time at q [s]
       pivot  — the step's rotation axis: "body" (hips/couch, default) | "neck" (the head alone);
                selects the lever arm of the R7 inertial term (LEVER.body 0.75 m / neck 0.12 m).
                B8 (V14): a segment also accepts optional neckPitch/neckYaw [°] (the neck angle:
                ν=pitch−trunk, Y=yaw+dyaw — computed in maneuvers.stepNeck from the pose table); with
                pivot="body" and ν≠0 the arm = Rx(ν)Ry(Y)⁻¹·[0, ARM_TRUNK, 0] + [±EAR_X, LEVER.neck, 0],
                ARM_TRUNK = LEVER.body − LEVER.neck = 0.63 m (the hips→C7 / C7→labyrinth split;
                50th-percentile anthropometry, Drillis & Contini 1966; STATUS: an assumption from
                outside the code, a "source boundary", ±10% — the sum returns to the R7 calibration
                bit-for-bit). Neck in line → EXACTLY the old arm (the literal branch). Measured
                effects: Yacovino 3→4 inertial contribution 0.295→0.222 g toward the vertex + a
                0.175 g naso-occipital component (assessment II targets hit to 0.001); Lempert
                untouched (the neck transitions occur after the debris has exited); the neck is
                interpolated with the SAME u as the pose slerp. Diagnostics WIRED in V24
                (2026-08-16): engineXi (the q=null path), bltInit, ldtPhases, actTimeline
                (pivot:"body" steps), rollShortPhases — with an INTERPOLATION PREFIX (a 0 s
                segment setting nPrev [0,0] → the first real segment interpolates the neck 0→ν;
                the convention chosen over const because it PRESERVES the B7 invariant — glued
                acts ≡ the chain, proof by probe P3: 0.0e+0 over 1432 samples). The new
                CALIBRATED CONSTANTS are below; the old "2.40" forecast was NEVER MEASURED
                (a historical record).
   phi0 — explicit starting arc angle of the debris [°]; null (default) = restPhi(canal,side): the
       gravitational minimum in the upright posture, or the cupula when that minimum lies outside the arc
       (posterior 48.8° · horizontal 199.8° · anterior 3°).
       VALIDATED since 2026-08-13 (assessment II, A9/V3): RangeError outside [CUPULA_DEG, phiExit] — no more
       silently dead simulations on NaN, debris resurrected from outside the canal (phi0>phiExit used to give
       a full liberatory transient) or teleports. A start in the CRUS ZONE (phi0 > phiExit−crusArc, vertical
       canals) = the debris STARTS IN THE CHAMBER (inCrus) instead of the old backward snap to pcrus.
   settled — the adhesion state at start (assessment II, A2/V3). true (default) = SETTLED debris: the
       adhesion gate from the resting drive; false = FRESHLY DISPLACED debris (positional history
       deposited it moments ago; the bond has not had time to form) — starts without the gate. The same
       distinction — "held by the bond vs held by gravity" — with which the engine explains the missing
       latency of the anterior canal. Without settled=false the gate quenched to 0.000 even responses
       of free amplitude |ξ|>1 (Bow & Lean: dead bands ~20% of the arc, the 205° cliff).
       THE GATE AT THE STARTING POINT (since V10, assessment II D1 — closure of artefact A2): for
       phi0≠null the resting drive is computed as driveAt(canal, side, phi0, q0-or-upright) — WHERE the
       debris lies, and in the STARTING orientation; for phi0=null LITERALLY the old restDrive(restPhi,
       upright) — the same bits, the default path untouched (proof V3: 34 scenarios × 48,144 samples,
       max difference 0; proof V10: snapshot dom=110 byte-identical PASS before extending the oracles).
       The old gate froze debris parked after a Dix (φ₀=159.5°, sitting: |driveAt|=0.142>fStat while
       |restDrive(restPhi)|≈0 → STUCK) for 2.05 s of bond consumption at someone else's point.
   xi0 — the cupular deflection at start (since V10; default 0). Carries the ξ tail of the previous run:
       chaining through {phi0, xi0, bond0, settled:true} is BIT-FOR-BIT identical to a single timeline
       (measured: max|Δ(ξ,φ)| = 0.0e+0 over the 1432 samples of the second act) — the B7 resolution
       stands on this identity (below, the FATIGABILITY section).
   bond0 — the bond state at start as a FRACTION of full adhesion ∈ [0,1] (since V10; null = 1). The
       unit is INDEPENDENT of size — the engine multiplies internally by the rescaled adh·r, which
       closes the documented footgun of chaining the raw final.bond between runs of different size.
       bond0=0 ≡ settled:false (the gate term (bond0==null || bond0>0) — continuity at zero).
   .final — the FINAL STATE {phi, xi, bond, stuck, exited, inCrus, bondFrac} as a property ON THE RESULT
       ARRAY (JSON.stringify of arrays skips it — oracles untouched). For session chaining (D1):
       pre=simulateCanalith(history); test=simulateCanalith({phi0: pre.final.phi, xi0: pre.final.xi,
       bond0: pre.final.bondFrac, settled:true, …}) — the chain is bit-for-bit identical to a single
       thread. bondFrac = the bond as a fraction [0,1] (clamped; the ONLY form for chaining); the raw
       bond (internal units adh·r) stays for compatibility — the between-size footgun is documented.
   q0 — STARTING orientation (quaternion); default null = the q of the FIRST segment (backward compatibility).
       NOTE (semantics of the 1st segment): with q0=null the first segment does NOT interpolate from the neutral position —
       its tTrans is time ALREADY IN the starting position, NOT a transition from sitting. To force a transition from a given
       orientation to the 1st q, pass q0 (e.g. [1,0,0,0] = neutral head). UI convention: the 1st segment has tTrans:0,
       and the transition is realized by the NEXT segment (maneuverTimeline: tTrans:0.8 per step). Analogously for simulateCupulolith.
   Model:
       φ — arc angle of the particle (ampulla φ=0, exit = ARC_SPAN[canal]: 267–319° per canal); lower
       bound CUPULA_DEG=3° (0.17 mm from the ampulla CENTRE — a point inside the ampulla) with a ZERO
       exit margin (the arc-end asymmetry noted in assessment II, C6 — sensitive to it: the horizontal
       emptying time with the healthy ear down and the rest-degeneracy threshold at a +9.9° pitch).
       Overdamped motion (Stokes drag, no inertia):
           dφ/dt = (g_head · tangent(φ)) / tauP
       Cupula (first-order model, long time constant):
           dξ/dt = -ξ/tauC + gc · exc · dφ/dt   (exc: +1 vertical / -1 horizontal)
   OTOLITH ADHESION (latency):
       The otolith is initially attached; the bond (adh) is broken by a sustained tangential force
       above the threshold fStat → a delayed start = LATENCY. A stronger provocation = shorter
       latency (consistent with the clinic). Once broken, the otolith no longer re-attaches in this session.
   COMMON-CRUS CHAMBER + EXPULSION INTO THE UTRICLE (VERTICAL canals: posterior+anterior; the horizontal has no crus):
       Anatomically, "reaching the end of the arc" (φ=phiExit) ≠ "falling into the utricle" — these are TWO events.
       • HORIZONTAL CANAL: no common crus → the non-ampullary end opens DIRECTLY into the utricle: after φ≥phiExit
         the otolith enters the utricle ONE-WAY (exited), at a PHYSICAL speed derived from gravity (~7–8 °/s).
       • VERTICAL CANALS (crusArc/crusGrav): after reaching the crus (φ≥phiExit−crusArc) the otolith ENTERS
         THE CRUS CHAMBER and PARKS — it leaves the sensitive canal, the cupula relaxes (ξ→0, no nystagmus), it waits for
         expulsion into the utricle.
         >>> B6-RESOLUTION (2026-08-14, V14c) — ONE LAW OF MOTION: in the chamber dφ/dt = max(0,
         dot(f, tangAt(G,φ)) − crusGrav)/tauP. The thesis "the chamber lies off the active arc" FELL
         after measurement: the [pcrus, pex] zone is the MEASURED final arc segment of the IE-Map
         atlas (the exit = the entrance to the vestibule), so tangAt applies there and IS the crus
         direction (PC at the exit: the tangent 9.3° from the "head-down" axis — the old −f[1] was
         a canal-blind approximation of this projection). crusGrav=0.6 stays without recalibration
         but changes meaning: the STANDING (yield) THRESHOLD of the wide chamber (Coulomb friction
         of the debris on the wall — the Bingham yield limit; in the narrow lumen the debris flows
         like a piston, threshold 0, adhesion is carried by fStat/adh: one physics, two geometries).
         Speed ∝ the force excess, /tauP — Stokes recovered (times ∝ r⁻²: T small/med 1.60–1.64
         vs theory 1.644).
         EMERGENT: PC expels while sitting as before (projection 0.965–0.987 > 0.6, the same steps
         as in the validation below), while AC requires the CHIN ON THE CHEST (pure sitting
         0.401 < 0.6; chin 0.81–0.88) — the closing Yacovino instruction becomes physically
         LOAD-BEARING. Expulsion times (medium, nominal): Epley 3.80 s · Semont/Bascule 3.85 s ·
         Yacovino 5.50 s (small 6.1–9.0 s, big 2.2–3.1 s); final.expelDur reports the duration
         (null = no expulsion happened); the former constant EXPEL_DUR=1.2 s (10 °/s regardless of
         force and size) is REMOVED from the motion — historical. The liberatory transient stops
         being ∝ crusArc (a convention) and starts carrying FORCE and SIZE: medium Epley 0.25 <
         Semont/Bascule 0.28 < Yacovino 0.32; small 0.10–0.16 (weak, long — consistent with
         "extremely long-latency BPPV"), big 0.70–0.83 (short, strong). Yacovino medium 0.545→0.317
         crosses the 0.5 display threshold → the step-4 card says "weak/fading nystagmus" instead
         of "strongest" (the only 2 dom rebaseline keys). Chamber speeds 1.33–5.58 °/s lie INSIDE
         the physical band of HC exits 1.27–14.61 °/s — the two exit models MERGED into one law
         (the chamber merely subtracts the standing threshold). The `expelling` latch removed
         (motion follows the force; continuity without a cliff); "eternal creep just above the
         threshold" is guarded by the HARD EXPEL_SANE=[0.5, 12] s oracle (snapshot.mjs, 72/72;
         measured envelope 1.95–9.90 s). <<<
         >>> CORRECTION 2026-08-13 (assessment II, C7) + V14 UPDATE: the "INERTIAL" expulsion route
         remains DEAD. The centripetal term a=ω×(ω×d) with the OLD lever d=[±EAR_X, L>0, 0] gave
         a[1]≤0 algebraically; with the B8 arm (z≠0) the claim becomes NUMERICAL — the measured
         maximum positive contribution across 7 maneuvers × 2 sides: ≤0.0012 g at the 0.6 threshold
         (the Semont 180°/0.8 s swing gives +1.18 g TOWARD THE VERTEX — it inhibits the expulsion).
         All expulsions are gravitational — in the final sitting holds or at the end of the sit-up
         transition; the role of the R7 inertia is real only in the drive ALONG the canal (without it
         Semont does not deliver the debris to the crus: pivot=neck → exited=false). A true inertial
         route would require the tangential term α×d, which the pose data do not carry. <<<
         Expulsion advances φ: (phiExit−crusArc)→phiExit → an ampullofugal nystagmus TRANSIENT
         (the SAME sign as the original) = liberatory/confirmatory. exited=true one-way (blocks the
         reversed phase; return to the canal / jam = out of scope, a D10 candidate).
       Backward motion in the canal lumen (dφ/dt<0, before the crus) → ξ<0 → REVERSED nystagmus.
       VALIDATION (re-measured 2026-08-13, real generated plans, both sides): Epley expulsion/liberatory
       at STEP 5 (return to sitting — end of the transition); Semont at STEP 4 (return to sitting, hold);
       Yacovino at STEP 4 (sitting, chin to chest); Bascule at STEP 5; the horizontal canal (Lempert/Gufoni geo)
       exits directly; Gufoni apo does not clear (conversion — correctly). Diagnostics (a single provoking
       position): the debris does NOT reach the crus — the position's gravitational minimum lies within the
       arc lumen (Dix: φ(120 s)=159.5, exited=false) — nystagmus crescendo→decrescendo (extinguishes), the
       debris stays in the canal.
   CONSTANTS CALIBRATED TO THE LITERATURE (posterior canal, canalithiasis) — NUMBERS IN FORCE 2026-08-16
       (V24 neck + V25 pose from the canal inclination; EXPLICIT metric: latency by the |ξ|≥0.05 threshold,
       duration to 3% of peak; protocol: dt=0.05, q0=sitting, the interpolation PREFIX + a provocation
       segment of tTrans 0.5 s with neckPitch/neckYaw from PROVOKE_POSE; MEASUREMENT tHold 60/60/90 s —
       longer than the production windows 40/40/70, because the 3% tails do not fit in the production
       window; the INTERP convention: neck 0→ν with the same u as the slerp — the numbers are NOT
       reproducible without these three declarations):
       posterior in Dix: latency 2.35 s, peak ξ 0.872 @ 10.25 s, duration (3%) 39.75 s;
       horizontal in Roll: 2.00 s / 0.784 @ 9.25 s / 39.35 s [V25 — the pose takes its flexion from
       the CANAL INCLINATION, 10.44 degrees instead of 30; the V24 record "2.30 / 0.696 @ 9.95 /
       42.75" = the path at a 30-degree flexion]; anterior in head-hang: 0.45 s (= no latency, R7) / 0.942 @ 24.95 s / 60.5 s.
       AMPLITUDE PEAKS: the POSTERIOR and ANTERIOR canals unchanged since 2026-08-13 (0.87158
       bitwise; AC 0.94188→0.94191 at the neck wiring — the only 5th-decimal change: the AC has no
       latency, its trajectory to the peak passes through a transition with the neck). The

       big 1.40 / 3.042 @ 6.95. The latency order HC < PC: after the neck wiring (V24) 2.30 vs 2.35 — a margin
       of 1 grid step dt; after deriving the pose flexion from anatomy (V25) 2.00 vs 2.35 — a margin
       of 7 steps, with the peak difference down from -20.2% to -10.1%. The conclusion is therefore
       already ROBUST against the grid, not a single-step artefact — see R3-OLD (part of the

       10.15/10.1/25.3) = HISTORICAL (the path without the neck, production tHold).
       VERDICT 2026-08-14 (3 lenses: clinics/physics-adversary/didactics):
       the AC course is EMERGENT, not an artifact — start at the cupula (restPhi 3° = attractor: the sitting
       potential minimum lies 23.6° BEYOND the exit), a 175° path to the stable 178° equilibrium in the hang,
       and the closed-form overdamped pendulum dφ/dt=(A/τP)·sin(eq−φ) reproduces the engine to within 0.40°;
       clinically consistent with peripheral pDBN (Cambi/Nuti 2013: duration 40.1±22 s; Califano 2014: AC
       attacks of 1–4 min; the Bárány <1 min criterion is met for visibility |ξ|>0.05 ≈ 57–60 s — the
       3%-of-peak criterion tail (V24: 60.5 s) lies below visual detection; the slow crescendo @25 s is
       the opposite of central CPPN, Choi 2015: immediate peak, TC 3–8 s). The former "mismatch with the test card"
       (assessment II, A6) was removed by the V8 chips ("Transient ≈1 min").
       tauC=5 s corresponds to a long cupular/canal time constant (~4–6 s).
   Returns: t [s], xi (sign = excitation/inhibition), phi [°], exited (bool).
   Tuning parameters: tauP, tauC, gc, phiExit, fStat, adh.
   PARTICLE SIZE/DENSITY (size): "small" | "medium" (default) | "big" — or a number (radius multiplier r).
       Mapping SIZE_R = {small:0.78, medium:1.0, big:1.35}. Physical scaling from r:
         tauP ∝ r⁻²  (Stokes settling velocity v ∝ r² → faster flow for a larger particle),
         gc   ∝ r³   (displaced volume/mass of endolymph → cupular deflection),
         adh  ∝ r    (detachment force of a sphere from the wall, JKR/DMT scaling).
       medium == r=1 → result IDENTICAL to the previous calibration (safe default).
       Emergently: LARGE = shorter latency, stronger/faster nystagmus, faster exit;
       SMALL = long latency, a weak and slow course and a LONGER minimal hold (see the minimal-holds
       table in VALIDATION STATUS; the app's hold is DERIVED — R4b — so small still clears) —
       consistent with the rationale of ~30 s holds for slowly settling fragments (Hain [2]) and with
       "extremely long-latency BPPV" [11]. Addresses the simplification "no effect of particle size"
       from the SIMPLIFICATIONS section.
   FATIGABILITY (rep) — a DIFFERENTIATING criterion canalithiasis↔cupulolithiasis [12].
       rep = the provocation repetition number in the same session (0 = first = full response). Mechanism:
       a compact cluster of otoconia DISPERSES on repetition (serial Dix–Hallpike) into smaller, less
       coherent fragments → weaker aggregate cupular deflection. The factor fatigueFactor(rep,{fatTau,fatFloor})
       scales gc → the AMPLITUDE ξ (and the nystagmus) decreases linearly; GEOMETRY/LATENCY UNCHANGED (the dominant,
       undisputed clinical effect is the amplitude drop). Defaults fatTau=2.0, fatFloor=0.06; rep=0 → factor 1
       → IDENTICAL result (backward compatibility). Example (posterior canal): peak ξ 0.81→0.51→0.33→0.22→0.15 for
       rep 0..4. CUPULOLITHIASIS (simulateCupulolith) has NO rep — the debris adheres to the cupula, does not disperse
       → does NOT fatigue; the difference is an emergent differentiating feature.
       B7-RESOLUTION (2026-08-14, V10): a series in the same session = a STATE CHAIN (canonical) — a
       repeated provocation is a new run with {phi0, xi0, bond0} from the previous final (bit-for-bit
       identical to a single timeline), COMPOSED with rep (dispersion within the same call). Emergently:
       the second provocation comes WITHOUT mechanical latency (the bond is broken) and weaker
       (position × dispersion); artefact B9 (threshold latency growing with rep at constant mechanics)
       disappears, because a fresh-start-with-rep is no longer the model of a repetition within the
       session. rep ALONE (a fresh start) = dispersion/ensemble averaging — semantics from outside the
       session. Between visits the bond regrows (readhesion, the domain layer) and rep returns to 0
       (re-aggregation of the clump). ANCHORS: the fatigability mechanism is mainly POSITIONAL
       (Imai 2021/2025/2026, Boselli 2014, Yang & Gao 2022 — see R10-STATUS); dispersion = a secondary
       hypothesis, the card does not state it as fact.

fatigueFactor(rep=0, {fatTau=2.0, fatFloor=0.06}) -> factor ∈ [fatFloor, 1]
   Pure fatigability factor: fatFloor + (1−fatFloor)·exp(−rep/fatTau); rep 0/negative/NaN → 1; monotonic
   decay to fatFloor; fatTau≤0 → RangeError. Exported to UI/diagnostics (serial Dix–Hallpike).

nystagmusPhase(xi, thr=0.05) -> "primary" | "reversed" | "none"
   Classification of the phase relative to the provoking direction:
   "primary" (ξ>thr, primary/liberatory), "reversed" (ξ<-thr, reversed), "none".
   NOTE (assessment II, C3): a helper API, currently NOT WIRED UP in src (no module calls it);
   the 0.05 threshold is in a different unit than the UI visibility threshold (0.10 on strength AFTER rectification).

simulateCupulolith({canal, side, timeline, q0=null, dt=0.05, tauCup=0.8, gain=1.0, size="medium"}) -> [{t, xi, target}, ...]
   q0 — STARTING orientation; semantics as in simulateCanalith (null = the q of the first segment).
   VALIDATIONS (since 2026-08-13, assessment II A10/DYN-9): tauCup>dt/2 (below it the explicit Euler
   scheme DIVERGES — 0.024 gave ξ~8e26 with no error) · gain finite AND — since V12 — gain<0 BLOCKED
   with a RangeError redirecting to simulateLightCupula (the old loophole "gain<0 = future light cupula"
   is CLOSED: the sign of gain is a DISEASE switch, not an amplitude knob — a negative value coming from
   a formula would silently flip the phenotype apo→geo on the cupulo card; the mechanism is chosen by FACADE).
   Internally both facades (cupulo/light) call the SHARED static integrator (simCupStatic) — the same
   "static target + tauCup relaxation" mechanism, differing solely in the sign of the density contrast
   (it lives in position(variant)) and the weight (CUP_WEAK / LIGHT_W).
   size scales gain ∝ r³ (a heavier otoconia cluster → stronger cupular deflection); no latency
   (tauCup unchanged). SIZE_R as in simulateCanalith. medium == r=1 → identical result.
   Simulation of CUPULOLITHIASIS: otoliths attached to the cupula → a heavy cupula deflected
   DIRECTLY by gravity (static projection), without a free particle in the canal lumen.
   Model:
       target = CUP_WEAK · gain · |proj| · (excited ? +1 : -1)
                where proj/excited from position({canal,side,variant:"cupulo",q}) (Ewald's rule;
                horizontal: apogeotropic = reversed relative to geotropic)
                CUP_WEAK=0.45 — CUPULOLITHIASIS WEAKER THAN CANALITHIASIS [1] (debris attached to the cupula
                deflects it more weakly than a bolus of free otoconia driving the endolymph column). The SAME
                coefficient scales the diagnostic nystagmus of the "cupulo" variant (nysFromGeom) — a single source of truth.
                [RECALIBRATION 0.6→0.45, 2026-08-13 (assessment II B2/V4): calibrated to the OBSERVED
                 quantity — the emergent apo/geo ratio of ξ peaks was 0.736 at 0.6 (just OUTSIDE the
                 literature band 0.4–0.7); 0.45 gives 0.538 = mid-band. For the vertical canals the band
                 is an extrapolation.]
       dξ/dt = (target - ξ) / tauCup           (fast relaxation)
   Emergent features (contrast to canalithiasis):
       - NO LATENCY (ξ follows gravity immediately; tauCup small)
       - PERSISTENCE (ξ holds the target as long as the position is maintained; no extinction)
       - SMALLER AMPLITUDE (CUP_WEAK): nystagmus less intense than in canalithiasis [1]
       - smooth reversal on position change
   Returns: t [s], xi (sign = excitation/inhibition), target (static target).
   Tuning parameters: tauCup, gain — TO BE CALIBRATED.

simulateLightCupula({canal, side, timeline, q0=null, dt=0.05, tauCup=0.8, gain=1.0, size="medium"}) -> [{t, xi, target}, ...]
   LIGHT CUPULA (assessment II, D3/V12) — persistent GEOTROPIC DCPN of the horizontal canal: a facade
   over the SAME static integrator as cupulolithiasis, with the buoyancy sign flipped in
   position(variant:"light") (ONLY canal="horizontal" — TypeError on the vertical canals) and with the
   weight LIGHT_W=0.45 (a separate calibration knob; start = CUP_WEAK, anchor: the light/canalo-geo
   ratio of ξ peaks in the Roll 0.375/0.696 = 0.538 — the middle of the 0.4–0.7 band, no separate
   literature norm for light-vs-geo SPV). gain>0 required (RangeError); the caller NEVER controls the
   sign. IDENTITY (measured): ξ_light(+g) ≡ −ξ_cupulo(+g) bit-for-bit.
   Emergent features: persistent GEOTROPIC DCPN with no latency (Roll: affected ear down +0.375 /
   healthy −0.398 — the AFFECTED side STRONGER, 2.1× from the EWALD_INHIB rectification); NULL PLANE
   SHARED with the heavy cupula (the projection zero is BLIND to the sign of gain): +8.7° yaw toward
   the AFFECTED ear (supineFlex; +6.9° supineFlat) — the model of this geometry gives an angle smaller
   than the clinic (typically ~20–30°, reported range 0–85° — Lee & Kim 2025); the mechanism is read
   from the DIRECTION of the DCPN around the zero (full heavy↔light reversal) and from TIME, not from
   the location of the zero; the sitting pseudo-SN is REVERSED relative to apo (light −0.024 toward the
   healthy ear / apo +0.024 toward the affected one — both subthreshold); around the zero a silence
   band of ~±25° (projections near the midline are small + the inhibition rectification) — clinically
   the null is a point, the mismatch is named on the card. CLINICAL STATUS (honesty of the card):
   "light cupula" is the name of a PATTERN, not a confirmed mechanism (5 hypotheses: a lighter cupula /
   heavier endolymph / light particles [never identified, maneuvers 0% — Ban 2016] / utricular
   dysfunction / perilymph-endolymph gradient); the entity sits OUTSIDE the Bárány 2015 classification;
   the 2025 series (J Neurol 272): most cases episodic, linked to vestibular migraine; Choi 2018
   (Neurology): 12% of persistent geotropic nystagmus = a cerebellar (tonsil) lesion — an
   absent/bilateral null point is a central flag. UI: a "find the null plane" mini-card on the Roll
   test card (yaw slider ±90°, two rows of eyes heavy/light from ONE angle, labels from the same
   target as the arrows); a red flag in baranyClassify HC-geo; the UI state variant stays binary
   (the phenotype/mechanism split = D4).

spvTrace(sim, canal, side) -> [{t, spvH, spvVert, spvTors}]   ·   XI_VIS   [module src/engine/spv-bridge.js]
   SPV BRIDGE (assessment II, D6/V13) — a units adapter ξ(t) → SPV(t) [°/s] on a COMMON axis of both
   engines; NO PHYSICS. A layer ABOVE the engines (bridge → {vestibular, neuro-vor}): vestibular.js
   stays free of imports, SPV_MAX has ONE source (NeuroVOR.SPV_MAX=30). CONTRACT (for the
   feature/vog-camera branch): t = seconds from the start of the provocation (grid 1:1 with sim);
   spvH/spvVert/spvTors = SPV WITH THE SIGN of the FAST-PHASE direction in the ANATOMICAL frame
   (+H toward the right ear, +Vert upbeat, +Tors upper poles toward the right; magnitude =
   slow-phase velocity — the VNG "right-beating positive" convention); the screen mirror (camRx)
   only in the render. Names spv* (the NeuroVOR dictionary), not bare h/v/t (in the repo =
   dimensionless direction components; {t,h,v,t} from the D6 spec had a key collision — corrected).
   SCALE: xiEff = ξ≥0 ? ξ : ξ·EWALD_INHIB — the Ewald II asymmetry STAYS (transduction physiology,
   the camera sees it: the reversal after the Dix ~15 °/s, not the raw ~33; Roll geo 20.9/8.0; the
   cupulo "affected-down weaker" exists only with it), the min(1,·) clamp does NOT enter (a render
   clamp, not physics): ξ>1 attainable (Semont 1.05; size big ×2.46) → a trace >30 °/s is intended —
   the trace and the animation deliberately diverge above ξ=1. ξ=1 ≡ SPV_MAX is a CALIBRATION
   CHOICE (ξ has no Hz layer; sanity: Roll geo 0.696→~21 °/s within the published 20–70 band, Dix →
   a vector peak |SPV| ~34 °/s). Vertical axis: common UNITS, not common ceilings (NeuroVOR damps
   the vertical with VERT_W=0.5 — a tonic imbalance of the pair; BPPV computes from the cupular
   deflection; VERT_W does not enter BPPV). XI_VIS = VIS_THRESH/SPV_MAX = 0.0667 — the visibility
   threshold (2 °/s) in ξ for the EXCITATORY branch (inhibitory: /EWALD_INHIB ≈ 0.148); a trace
   consumer compares |spv*| ≥ VIS_THRESH directly. The 0.05/0.10 thresholds NOT touched (different
   scales — unification is a separate decision with a rebaseline). VOG WALL (the overlay contract):
   the camera gives [{t: ms, x, y, conf}] → segmental estimates spvX/spvY in NORMALIZED image units
   (the j/s→°/s calibration deliberately out of scope until near-IR) and NO torsion — until then the
   overlay = SHAPE+TIMING, not amplitude; for PC torsion is the DOMINANT component of the model
   (quickPhase t=1.0) — the camera will see ~0.81 of the vector. Oracle pin: engine.spv (10 runs
   × {n, peak, grid5}).

dynNystagmus(canal, side, xi) -> {excited, intensity, h, v, t}
   Conversion of instantaneous ξ (from simulateCanalith) into nystagmus components.
   excited = (xi>0); direction = quickPhase with the sign of excitation.
   intensity = min(1, |xi| · (excited ? 1 : 0.45))
               — Ewald II rectification: the inhibitory response is weaker.
   [2026-07-02] NOW THE SOLE SOURCE of nystagmus direction/strength/reversal in the ENTIRE application — diagnostics
   (via nysFromGeom→position) AND maneuvers (via the view: nysFromDyn/stepXiPeak in otorepo.html, outside
   the Vestibular module). Manual direction annotations for maneuvers (headNys.ear/intensity) REMOVED;
   direction+strength+reversal (ξ<0→inhibition) follow exclusively from this function. Validated by an Ewald
   audit for all maneuvers/sides (view_doc.txt: "ENGINE AUDIT AGAINST EWALD'S LAWS").

   slerpQ (spherical interpolation with hemisphere handling) — internal.

OTHER EXPORTS (undocumented until now — assessment II, C1):
   sizeR(size) -> r  (radius multiplier; validated > 0)
   sizeUm(size) -> equivalent clump diameter [µm] (16/21/29 for small/medium/big — Stokes'
      law [15]; shown on the maneuver card)
   restPhi(canal, side) -> °  (debris rest when upright: posterior 48.8 · horizontal 199.8 · anterior 3).
      PER-CANAL CLAMP (since V7): a minimum outside the arc → VERTICAL canals: the cupula (intended —
      no stable upright rest of the AC, R7); HORIZONTAL with the minimum BEYOND THE EXIT: rest AT the
      exit (the canal empties on the first simulation step) instead of the old silent ~264° teleport
      to the cupula. At a 0° frame the horizontal branch is unreachable (eq=199.8) — it arms only
      from a pitch of ≥ +9.9° (R9).
   driveAt(canal, side, phiDeg, q, tauP=6.5) -> tangential drive [rad/s] at ANY point of the arc and
      ANY orientation (since V3; a generalisation of restDrive — restDrive = driveAt at restPhi and
      upright, identically). Will feed the drive of the positional-history presets (BLT_HISTORY, plan V5).
   constants: ARC_SPAN {anterior:281.1, posterior:318.8, horizontal:267.3} · CANAL_NORMALS · CANAL_GEOM
      · CUP_WEAK=0.45 (since V4 — calibrated to the emergent apo/geo ratio 0.538) · EWALD_INHIB=0.45.

CANAL GEOMETRY — SINGLE SOURCE [2026-08-05, branch feature/vestibular]
   Until that date the engine carried TWO independent geometries of the same canal: the measured
   CANAL_NORMALS (Wu 2021) for nystagmus magnitudes AND a separate, HAND-written 45° idealization
   (CANAL_GEOM/GEXC/uHC) for the debris dynamics. They disagreed by 15-20° on EVERY canal:
       posterior P 16.8° · L 15.5°   anterior P 20.3° · L 20.2°   horizontal P 17.4° · L 16.3°
   The engine therefore computed endolymph flow in one plane and derived eye motion about another.
   Stage 1 (morning 2026-08-05): the plane comes SOLELY from CANAL_NORMALS, with CANAL_GEOM, GEXC and uHC
   derived from it. Stage 2 (evening): GEXC and uHC DELETED — they were the tangent frozen at φ=90°
   (duplication removed; plane discrepancy = 0.00° across all 6 canals).

   Source-frame conversion (since 2026-08-05, IE-Map [13]): Slicer-RAS → head: positions (X,Z,Y);
     the normal is an AXIAL vector and the mapping has determinant −1, so its sign flips
     (comment in vestibular.js at AMPULLA_DIR). The former wuToHead helper (the Wu frame conversion)
     does NOT exist in the code — Wu 2021 remains solely an EXTERNAL control (17.1° agreement).

   AMPULLA_DIR — ampulla direction (φ=0). MEASURED 2026-08-05 (IE-Map: the projection of A−C onto the
     canal's PCA plane) — NO longer an anatomical assumption. Values (head frame; L = mirror of P):
       posterior P[-0.5174,-0.6498,0.5568] · anterior P[0.4703,-0.5693,0.6743]
       · horizontal P[-0.1794,0.1705,0.9689]
   ARC_SPIN — REMOVED 2026-08-05: the circulation sense "increasing φ = ampullofugal" FOLLOWS FROM THE
     MEASUREMENT (monotonically positive angle increments along the ordered Q0→Q4 axis for all six
     canals), so the 6-bit convention table ceased to exist.

CANAL_GEOM — DERIVED: {e1, e2, exc}; e1 = projection of the MEASURED AMPULLA_DIR onto the PCA plane,
             e2 = n × e1, exc = +1 vertical canals (Ewald III) / −1 horizontal (Ewald II).
             Live values (2026-08-13; reproducible with a node probe → Vestibular.CANAL_GEOM; L = mirror of P):
   posterior  P:{e1 (-0.517,-0.650, 0.557), e2 ( 0.588,-0.743,-0.321)}
   anterior   P:{e1 ( 0.470,-0.569, 0.674), e2 ( 0.364, 0.821, 0.439)}
   horizontal P:{e1 (-0.179, 0.170, 0.969), e2 ( 0.983, 0.061, 0.171)}
GEXC / uHC / CK — DELETED 2026-08-05. They were excitation axes computed as ±e1, i.e. the tangent
at φ=90° — a duplicate of CANAL_GEOM frozen at one point of the arc. Replaced by tangAt(G, φ),
evaluated at restPhi, i.e. at the same place the simulation actually moves from.
uHC(side) — DERIVED as +e1 of the horizontal canal (gravity TOWARD the ampulla = ampullopetal = Ewald II).

   EFFECTS (measured): posterior-canal calibration UNCHANGED (latency 1.95 s, peak 0.807 @9.25 s,
   duration 25.3 s). R3 partially and spontaneously improved: anterior latency 3.15→2.80 s, peak ξ
   0.668→0.817; horizontal 2.30→2.20 s, 0.518→0.550. All clinical directions unchanged (Dix, Roll
   geo/apo, Bow & Lean, deep head-hang), position()↔simulateCanalith consistent 6/6, maneuvers clear as
   before (gufoniApo and bascule still do not — per R2). Golden snapshot: 2 of 91 screens (see R5), the
   rest byte-identical. DOES NOT FIX R1 — R1 is the arc LENGTH, not the plane orientation (φ_eq posterior
   190.0→189.6, anterior 194.0→207.1, i.e. for the anterior canal even further past the arc end).
   [UPDATE 2026-08-13 — assessment II, C1/DYN-10] The "EFFECTS" numbers above describe the state on the
   DAY the geometry was unified (before R7/R4b) and do NOT reproduce on the current engine. The
   calibration in force for the posterior canal (since V24, the path with the neck): latency 2.35 s,
   peak 0.872 @10.25 s — see CONSTANTS CALIBRATED (full protocol there; 2.25/10.15 = the historical
   record of the neck-less path). The sentence "gufoniApo and bascule still do not [clear]" was
   true before R7 — since R7 Bascule DOES CLEAR; the only non-clearing maneuver remains Gufoni apo (a
   conversion maneuver).

         small clears everything (Semont: 252 s total → exited; Yacovino: 123 s → exited). "Slow fragments
         do not clear" therefore described a performance boundary of the model, not particle settling, and
         has been REMOVED rather than restored. <<<
     [HISTORICAL — the "cap" truncation removed by R4b 2026-08-05; holds are now DERIVED from the engine]
     TRUNCATION DEPTH (medium) — how much of the real hold the model actually simulates. It is uneven across
       maneuvers, so "does the maneuver clear" partly measures truncation aggressiveness, not canal geometry:
         semont 180 s → 36 s (20%) · gufoniGeo 120 s → 36 s (30%) · lempert 150 s → 66 s (44%)
         epley 90 s → 48 s (53%) · yacovino 60 s → 46 s (77%)
       Semont is truncated hardest and is therefore the most sensitive to changes in the constants.
     WHAT ACTUALLY SURVIVES OF THE SIZE EFFECT — THE MINIMUM PER-STEP HOLD at which the maneuver still
       clears (RE-MEASURED 2026-08-13; a uniform hold on every timed step, non-timed steps 6 s):
                    small   medium   big     plan prescribes
         epley       21 s    12 s     6 s          30 s
         semont      58 s    30 s    14 s          90 s
         yacovino    41 s    24 s    13 s          30 s   <-- THE ONLY THRESHOLD ABOVE THE NOMINAL HOLD
         lempert      9 s     5 s     3 s          30 s
         gufoniGeo    8 s     5 s     2 s          60 s
       YACOVINO + SMALL DEBRIS is still the only place where the threshold (41 s) EXCEEDS the nominal
       hold (30 s); it is rescued by sizedSeconds (holdMult 1.6 → 45 s), but the margin has shrunk to
       4 s (41 vs 45) — the most sensitive spot of the calibration (assessment II, V-DOC-11).
       Semont/small needs 58 s (fits within the prescribed 90 s). The teaching point "slow fragments
       need longer holds" [2] stands.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 USAGE EXAMPLES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The pose is built by the pose layer — qSupineYaw removed from the engine (POSE_SPEC, 2026-08-05):
import { stepHeadQ } from '../pose/maneuvers.js';
const qDix = stepHeadQ('supineHang', 45, 'up');   // RIGHT-side Dix–Hallpike

// Statically (diagnostics): nystagmus direction in a right-side Dix-Hallpike
Vestibular.position({canal:"posterior", side:"P", variant:"canalo", q: qDix});
// -> {excited:true, mag:0.935, h:0, v:0.812, t:1}          (RUN 2026-08-13)

// Dynamically: the ξ(t) course for the first Epley position (posterior right)
const tr = Vestibular.simulateCanalith({canal:"posterior", side:"P", q0:[1,0,0,0],
   timeline:[{q: qDix, tTrans:1.5, tHold:25}]});
// peak ξ 0.872 @ 10.6 s; last sample: t 26.5, ξ 0.229, φ 155.5, exited:false
Vestibular.dynNystagmus("posterior","P", tr[tr.length-1].xi);
// -> {excited:true, intensity:0.229, h:0, v:0.812, t:1}     (RUN 2026-08-13)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 INTER-LAYER DEPENDENCIES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 quickPhase  ──> nystagmus, position, dynNystagmus   (source of direction {h,v,t})
 gHead       ──> position, simulateCanalith           (gravity in the head frame)
 tangAt      ──> position, simulateCanalith          (arc tangent — one for both paths)
 restPhi     ──> position, simulateCanalith          (where the debris starts — derived from gravity)
 CANAL_GEOM  ──> simulateCanalith                     (arc + sign of excitation)
 position    ──> simulateCupulolith                    (static gravitational cupular target)
 slerpQ      ──> simulateCanalith, simulateCupulolith  (orientation interpolation)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 BIBLIOGRAPHY AND MODEL CONSISTENCY WITH THE LITERATURE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The engine is a REDUCED, phenomenological implementation of established biomechanical
BPPV models — it contains no new scientific hypotheses. Mapping:

[1] Squires TM, Weidman MS, Hain TC, Stone HA (2004). "A mathematical model
    for top-shelf vertigo: the role of sedimenting otoconia in BPPV."
    J Biomech 37(8):1137-1146.
    → The canonical canalithiasis model: an otolith settling under gravity with Stokes
      drag, transcupular pressure, cupular deflection. CONSISTENT with our
      dynamics layer (simulateCanalith), in a simplified version.
    → The MAIN mechanism of latency per [1]: no significant pressure until the otolith
      travels from the wide ampulla to the NARROW duct (pressure amplification).
      WE DO NOT MODEL THIS — see the latency annotation below.

[2] Hain TC, Squires TM, Stone HA (2005). "Clinical implications of a
    mathematical model of BPPV." Ann N Y Acad Sci 1039:384-394.
    → Clinical implications of [1]; the rationale for holding a position ~30 s in the CRP.

[3] House MG, Honrubia V (2003). "Theoretical models for the mechanisms of BPPV."
    → Both mechanisms (canalithiasis and cupulolithiasis) are physically plausible.

[4] Obrist D, Hegemann S (2008). "Fluid-particle dynamics in canalithiasis."
    J R Soc Interface 5(27):1215-1229.
    → Fluid–particle dynamics; the open question of whether otoliths adhere to the wall.

[5] Dai M, Klein A, Cohen B, Raphan T (1999). "Model-based study of the human
    cupular time constant." J Vestib Res.
    → Long cupular/canal time constant (~4–6 s). CONSISTENT with tauC=5.

[6] Schuknecht HF (1969). Cupulolithiasis (heavy cupula → immediate,
    persistent nystagmus). CONSISTENT with simulateCupulolith (no latency, persistence).

[7] Hall SF, Ruby RRF, McClure JA (1979). Canalithiasis (free fragments in the canal).
    CONSISTENT with the particle model. (cf. Brandt & Steddin 1993 — canalithiasis better
    explains latency and fatigability).

[8] Steinhausen (1933); Van Egmond, Groen, Jongkees (1949) — the torsional-pendulum
    model of the cupula. CONSISTENT with the first-order cupula.

[9] Ewald (1892) — Ewald's laws I/II/III. CONSISTENT with quickPhase / isExcitatory.

[10] Della Santina et al. (2005), JARO 6:191-206, doi:10.1007/s10162-005-0003-x — canal-plane
     orientations from 3D CT reconstruction. STATUS SINCE 2026-08-05: no longer the source of the
     geometry (that is [13]); it serves as the DIRECTIONAL CANON and as an independent control for
     R9 — lateral-canal angle to Reid's plane 19.5°±7.4 (L) and 20.4°±6.9 (R), with the authors'
     explicit note on the difference from the textbook 30°.

[13] Ahmadi S-A, Raiser TM, Rühl RM, Flanagin VL, zu Eulenburg P (2021). "IE-Map: a novel in-vivo
     atlas and template of the human inner ear". Sci Rep 11:3293, doi:10.1038/s41598-021-82716-0
     (PMID 33558581, PMC7870663, CC BY 4.0, repository github.com/pydsgz/IEMap).
     >>> THE ONLY SOURCE OF CANAL GEOMETRY IN THE ENGINE. <<< It supplies a COMPLETE set from one
     reconstruction: CANAL_NORMALS (planes from PCA of the duct centreline points), AMPULLA_DIR
     (projection of A−C), ARC_SPAN (angle A→O) and the arc direction (monotonic Q0→Q4). n=63
     subjects / 126 ears, 3T, CISS 0.5 mm iso → template 0.2 mm iso, 94 landmarks. The template is
     aligned to REID'S STANDARD PLANE (infraorbital point + external auditory meatus, bilaterally) —
     hence the atlas frame is taken as the head frame without rotation (R9). THE SOURCE'S LIMIT:
     none of the 94 landmarks is cranial, and the paper itself calls the lateral canal's coplanarity
     with Reid an ASSUMPTION.

[14] Curthoys IS, Oman CM — dimensions of the human membranous labyrinth. A CONTROL on the arc
     radii computed from [13]: ours 3.50 / 3.32 / 2.68 mm (posterior / anterior / lateral) against
     the reported 3.3 / 3.2 / 2.4 — agreeing in order and magnitude.

Sources WITHDRAWN from source status (they remain EXTERNAL CONTROLS and feed no constant):
  • Wu et al. (2021), Front Neurol 12:741948 — canal normals from MRI, n=55. Were the source of
    CANAL_NORMALS until 2026-08-05. Replaced by [13] because Wu gives no position of the ampulla
    along the loop, and stitching geometry from different papers was the source of the drifts.
    Agreement between the two sources 17.1°; lateral-canal pitch per Wu 17.4° — cited in R9 as a
    second independent number.
  • Cárdenas-Serna & Jeffery, Zenodo doi:10.5281/zenodo.4818568 (described in PMC8819049) — arc
    spans from 96 bony labyrinths (265.8 / 307.0 / 236.8°). Were the source of ARC_SPAN between
    morning and evening on 2026-08-05. Replaced by [13] for the same reason; kept as an
    order-of-magnitude control.
  • Bradshaw et al. (2010), JARO doi:10.1007/s10162-009-0195-6 — θs of the membranous labyrinth
    (271.7 / 324.7 / 249.2°), measured between vestibular boundaries and therefore systematically
    larger. Never a source — only a directional control.
  • Cherchi (2026), PMC12799655 — the real lateral-canal arc ~240° and its postero-medial segment;
    a directional control for our 267.3° and an argument against diagrams using a 180° arc.

[15] Stokes' law (v = (2/9)·Δρ·g·a²/μ) — ties the constant tauP to the radius of the otoconial
     clump. For tauP=6.5 s and a mean arc radius of 3.17 mm this gives a ≈ 10.6 µm, i.e. an
     equivalent diameter ≈ 21 µm. The size scaling tauP = tauP₀/r² (v ∝ r²) IS that law, so size
     small/medium/large = 16 / 21 / 29 µm in diameter, and those numbers are shown to the clinician.
     Single human otoconia range 1–30 µm; the size of the real AGGREGATE in a living patient is not
     known — PMC3226995 ([4] Obrist & Hegemann) says so explicitly.

[11] "Extremely Long Latency BPPV" (Res Vestib Sci, 2017) — very long latencies
     attributed to otolith ADHESION and their size. The basis for the adhesion hypothesis
     (our latency) as a minority mechanism.

[12] Dix MR, Hallpike CS (1952) — the original description of positional BPPV nystagmus and its
     FATIGABILITY (extinction/attenuation on repeated provocation). The dispersion mechanism
     of the compact debris: Brandt & Steddin (1993, [7]). The basis for the fatigueFactor (rep) factor:
     canalithiasis fatigues, cupulolithiasis (debris on the cupula) — does not.

ANNOTATION — LATENCY (a deliberate modeling choice):
  Our latency arises from otolith ADHESION (a bond broken by a sustained tangential
  force). In the literature this is an ACCEPTED but MINORITY hypothesis, invoked
  especially for long latencies (Hain/dizziness-and-balance; "Extremely Long
  Latency BPPV" 2017). The MAINSTREAM [1,2] explains latency by the ampulla→narrow-duct
  geometry (pressure amplification), which we do NOT implement. Other hypotheses:
  inertia of the otolith clot. We reproduce the correct clinical behavior
  (latency present, shorter with a stronger provocation) by an alternative route.

SIMPLIFICATIONS RELATIVE TO FULL MODELS:
  - a "tangential gravity → flow" proxy instead of the full transcupular pressure ΔPc;
  - no amplification in the narrow duct (the main latency mechanism per [1]);
  - particle size: SUPPORTED as a scaling parameter (size → tauP∝r⁻², gc∝r³, adh∝r);
    still no wall interactions and no velocity storage;
  - cupulolithiasis WEAKER than canalithiasis [1]: MODELED by a single coefficient CUP_WEAK=0.45
    (amplitude multiplier of ξ in simulateCupulolith AND of the diagnostic nystagmus of the "cupulo" variant in nysFromGeom);
    residual simplification: the coefficient is GLOBAL (not per-canal or per-geometry), a calibration value;
  - fatigability (dispersion of the compact debris on repetition) MODELED as a factor scaling
    amplitude (fatigueFactor / the rep parameter in simulateCanalith) — NOT as an explicit simulation of many
    particles with different trajectories; cupulolithiasis is deliberately without fatigability (a differentiating feature) [12].

 >>> R6 CLOSED 2026-08-05 (branch feature/vestibular). Yacovino clears: φ 3 → 133 → 262 → 281 (exit). <<<
     The R6 working hypothesis was right about the LOCATION (anterior-canal AMPULLA_DIR) but the fix was
     not a −35° rotation — it was a MEASUREMENT. The IE-Map atlas supplied a complete set from one
     reconstruction: the plane normal (PCA of the ordered duct-axis points), the ampulla centre, the
     opening, and the ARC DIRECTION (monotonic Q0→Q4 — this removed the ARC_SPIN table, 6 bits of
     convention). Arc spans 318.8/281.1/267.3.
     The "anterior-canal anomaly" dissolved with it: the anterior canal's gravitational minimum in the
     upright posture (304.7°) lies BEYOND its exit (281.1°), i.e. the anterior canal has NO stable
     resting point when upright and free debris falls out of it on its own. That is not a defect but a
     reproduction of a known fact (AC-BPPV ~1–2%). Debris persists there only pressed against the
     cupula, and that is where it starts (φ=3°).
     The earlier conclusion "head-hang comes out inhibitory at every pitch" was MY REFERENCE-POINT
     ERROR: I evaluated the drive at the resting position 304.7° rather than where the debris actually
     lies. From the cupula the head-hang drives it ampullofugally → excitation → DOWNBEAT, as in clinic.

 >>> R7 CLOSED 2026-08-05 (8dd18b5). Semont and Bascule clear; 12/12 therapeutic maneuvers. <<<
     Implemented exactly as the repair direction predicted: the particle responds to the SPECIFIC FORCE
     f = g − a. ω comes from the same interpolation that drives the pose; the tangential term (α×d) is
     omitted because with a constant rate α=0 away from segment boundaries — that under-states inertia
     rather than over-stating it.
     [Addendum 2026-08-13 (assessment II, DYN-12): for COMBINED steps (e.g. Epley s4→s5: untwist+sit-up)
      the slerp screw axis ≠ the sequence of anatomical axes — the inertia of such steps is
      order-of-magnitude in character; single-axis swings (the Semont 180°, the Lempert roll) are rendered
      faithfully. Addendum 2 (assessment II, C7): the role of f=g−a is real in the drive ALONG the canal;
      to EXPULSION from the crus inertia contributes nothing — see the correction in the crus-chamber
      description.]
     QUANTITATIVE CONFIRMATION: the vertex-ward vector hand-written into LEAN_G (+0.6) turned out to be
     THE SAME QUANTITY. For a 180° swing in 0.8 s with a 0.75 m lever, ω²L/g = 1.2 g in the same
     direction; 0.6 g corresponds to a swing in ~1.16 s. Someone once measured this physics by eye and
     wrote down the result.
     The only new input is the LEVER (anthropometry: hips 0.75 m · neck 0.12 m · interaural half 0.075 m),
     and the step's rotation AXIS is derived from POSE_SPEC (roll/trunk changed = the body moves;
     otherwise the head alone).
     ALSO DERIVED along the way: the INITIAL ADHESION STATE — see restDrive. The anterior canal is the
     only one whose rest is not a minimum: the debris lies PRESSED against the cupula with a force of
     0.131 against a 0.04 threshold, so it is held by gravity rather than by a bond and moves without
     breaking adhesion. The model therefore predicts NO LATENCY in the anterior canal (0.50 s = the
     transition time alone) — matching descriptions of AC-BPPV.

 R7-OLD. SEMONT AND BASCULE DO NOT CLEAR — THE MODEL HAS NO INERTIAL TERM [CLOSED — above].
     This is NOT a geometry or pose error; it is a structural statement. A 180° body rotation reverses
     gravity in the head frame EXACTLY (Rz(180) maps (0,−1,0) to (0,+1,0), whatever the head's relation
     to the trunk). Semont and Bascule swing the patient through 180° "without changing the head
     position" by definition, so the second position's gravitational minimum sits EXACTLY 180° from the
     first. Measured: drop 1 → 139.8°, drop 2 → 319.8°, difference 180.000°.
     A particle that settled in the first minimum therefore lands on the UNSTABLE point in the second:
     the tangential component of gravity is zero. Gravity alone will never move it — shortening the
     transition does not help (checked tTrans 0.8/0.4/0.2 s: φ stays at 140°).
     So a maneuver clinically called LIBERATORY, whose whole efficacy depends on the SPEED of the swing,
     cannot be reproduced by a purely gravitational model. Semont's previous "success" came from a
     hand-added vertex-ward component in LEAN_G (+0.6) that broke the symmetry by 27° — the pose had
     been bent to stand in for the missing physics.
     REPAIR DIRECTION (not implemented): the particle responds to the SPECIFIC FORCE f = g − a
     (equivalence principle), not to g alone. During the swing the labyrinth travels an arc whose radius
     is its distance from the rotation axis, so a is computable from the step's axis and the existing
     skeleton (SK: pelvis→head = 42+15+15 units with thigh 33 and shin 29 → about 0.8 m). This needs no
     new fitted constant — it needs a decision on whether to add an inertial term to the in-canal
     dynamics. The same term resolves the other borderline case: the anterior canal's start at the
     cupula (drive 0.012 < threshold 0.04).

 R8. THE BOW & LEAN TEST IS SUB-THRESHOLD IN THIS MODEL [2026-08-05].
     >>> RESOLVED 2026-08-13 (assessment II, V4+V5) — the decision was made and IMPLEMENTED; see
         R8-RESOLUTION below. The write-up below remains as the problem analysis; its numeric
         corrections are also in R8-RESOLUTION. <<<
     Tangential drive: bow 0.026, lean 0.022, against an adhesion-breaking threshold fStat of 0.04 and
     0.131 for the Roll test — 5× weaker than the Roll and below threshold. The simulation yields ξ=0.000.
     The reason is geometric and parameter-robust: the horizontal canal lies almost in the earth plane
     and its ampulla↔antipode axis almost in the sagittal plane. Bowing and leaning rotate gravity in
     THAT plane, so the minimum slides along an axis passing through the resting point (10° ↔ 190°,
     rest 199.8°) — the tangential component stays small at EVERY bow angle (checked 30–120°). This is
     consistent with the horizontal canal being tested by the ROLL rather than by bowing, and with Bow &
     Lean being regarded as an adjunct test of debated accuracy.
     DISCREPANCY: the sign that does come out is OPPOSITE to the Choung rule (bow toward the affected
     ear, lean toward the healthy ear) that the test card asserts in text. The margin is ~10° of resting
     position — within the individual variability of a single atlas.
     ADDENDUM 2026-08-05 (after R7, probes r8/r8b) — the picture is sharper than I first recorded:
       • CANALITHIASIS (geotropic form): the debris DOES NOT MOVE AT ALL. ξ = 0.000 at EVERY speed
         (tTrans 1.5/0.8/0.4/0.2 s) and for both rotation axes; φ stays at 199.8. R7's inertia does NOT
         rescue it — and not by accident: bowing rotates the head about the interaural axis, so the
         centrifugal force points toward the VERTEX, i.e. exactly along the horizontal canal's NORMAL.
         A force perpendicular to the ring's plane cannot move anything around the ring. That is why the
         same term rescued Semont (posterior canal, a different axis) and does nothing here.
       • CUPULOLITHIASIS (apogeotropic form): there IS a response, because the cupula is heavy and reacts
         to the DIRECTION of gravity alone, with no debris travel — but a weak one: ξ 0.100 (bow) and
         −0.087 (lean), against ~0.7 in the Roll test. Seven times smaller.
       • THE DIRECTION is opposite to the Choung rule in BOTH forms, not only the geotropic one.
         [UPDATE 2026-08-13, assessment II A1/V4: for the CUPULOLITHIC form this was an ARTEFACT of the
          evaluation point — the heavy-cupula target was computed at the free-debris rest (199.8°) instead
          of at the cupula. After moving the target to CUPULA_DEG (V4) the apo directions AGREE with the
          Choung rule and the card text (bow→healthy, lean→affected; apo Roll preserved 0.832/0.885, null
          point at yaw +8..9° toward the affected ear). The opposite-direction statement now applies ONLY
          to canalithiasis — and there the culprit is the start φ₀ (R10, the watershed), not the test
          geometry. Plan V5.]
     WHERE EXACTLY THE DISAGREEMENT SITS (probe r8c, the clinical reasoning taken apart step by step):
       AGREEMENT about the position — in the bow the canal stands VERTICAL (|g| in its plane 0.98) and its
         lowest point is the AMPULLA (φ_eq 10.0°); in the lean it is vertical too (0.94) with the lowest
         point OPPOSITE the ampulla (φ_eq 191.0°). Exactly what the clinical account says.
       THE DISAGREEMENT is about ONE thing the clinical reasoning does not state explicitly: WHERE THE
         DEBRIS LIES BEFORE THE TEST. The bow's summit (unstable point) falls at 190.0°. For EVERY start
         φ < 190° — about 70% of the arc — the bow gives AMPULLOPETAL flow and the lean AMPULLOFUGAL,
         exactly as the rule says. The engine puts the debris at 199.8°, i.e. 9.8° PAST the summit — and
         that alone flips everything.
       WHERE 199.8 COMES FROM: it is the canal's lowest point in the upright posture. The ampulla sits
         slightly ABOVE the centre of the canal plane, so the lowest point is not exactly opposite it but
         a few degrees further along.
       THE MARGIN IS SMALL BUT SIGN-ROBUST: for frame pitches 0/−5/−10/−15/−20/−30° (canal tilt 10.4–40.3°)
         the margin is 9.8/6.8/5.3/4.5/4.0/3.5° — it shrinks but NEVER changes sign. The canonical 30°
         does not flip it either. So this is NOT an R9 issue.
       WHY THAT IS A WEAK BASIS: sitting, |g| in the canal plane is only 0.18 (= sin 10.4°), i.e. the canal
         is nearly flat and its "lowest point" is barely defined — the whole non-ampullary arm lies at
         almost the same height. So the model is saying less "the Choung rule is wrong" than "the debris
         sits on the watershed and I cannot tell which way it will fall".
     THE CONSEQUENCE THAT NEEDS A DECISION: the test card currently draws a direction arrow for the
       geotropic form even though the dynamics say no nystagmus arises there. That is the same class of
       problem as the stepXiPeak fabrication removed on 2026-08-05: the screen asserts something the
       model did not compute.
     >>> HYPOTHESIS VERIFIED AGAINST THE LITERATURE 2026-08-06 (data supplied by the user). <<<
     H1 (rest a few degrees PAST the ampulla's antipode) — CONFIRMED IN THIS ATLAS, not in the population.
        An independent recomputation gave the same 199.82°, i.e. 19.82° past the antipode, margin 9.82°.
        Between-subject variability of lateral-canal orientation is ~5° (Wu 2021), and nobody has measured
        the ampulla's phase along the loop at all — so a 9.8° margin is NOT large against that variability.
     H2 (~29 µm) — RIGHT ORDER, WRONG NUMBER. The quoted 29 µm is the depth of the basin WHILE SITTING
        (28.8 µm between 180° and 199.8° at |g|_plane 0.18) — it describes why the starting point is
        ill-defined. The quantity H2 actually needs is a different one: THE THRESHOLD TO CLEAR IN THE BOW,
        where the canal stands vertical and the full radius works: R(1−cos 9.82°) = 38.5–39.3 µm. Both
        numbers are correct but describe different things — my label was misleading. WHAT STANDS: "about
        30–40 µm". Caveat: the size of the real AGGREGATE in a living patient's canal is not known (single
        otoconia 1–30 µm), so any "grain-sized" comparison must be made cautiously.
     H3 (dependence on the preceding position) — STRONGLY SUPPORTED MECHANISTICALLY, CLINICALLY UNTESTED.
        Bhandari et al. 2022 (Front Neurol, doi:10.3389/fneur.2022.881156) show by simulation that the
        debris' initial location and the ORDER of the Roll test's stages change the nystagmus direction,
        can yield a unilateral or direction-fixed response, AND THAT THE TEST ITSELF MAY REMOVE THE DEBRIS
        FROM THE CANAL — which INDEPENDENTLY reproduces our R10 (21 s with the healthy ear down).
        BUT: no study randomises the position before the BLT. Moreover, in Koju the BLT was performed
        BEFORE the Roll test and only 41% had the expected response — so a prior Roll is not the only cause.
     H4 (frequent absence of a response) — CLEARLY CONFIRMED:
        Choung 2006 (n=26) 11.5% without bow or lean · Lee 2010 (n=89) 20.2% · Kim 2016 (n=66) 45%, the BLT
        lateralised only 36/66 · Koju 2022 (n=44 geo) only 40.9% with the expected response ·
        Califano 2024 (n=100) HPT non-diagnostic in 20%, direction agreement 75% in BOTH forms.
        Nowhere any trace of a stable ~90% sensitivity that would refute the hypothesis.
        NOTE: poor sensitivity does not by itself prove a mechanism — the alternatives are the visibility
        threshold of the nystagmus, debris dispersion, entrapment against the wall, individual geometry,
        and too slow an execution.
     H5 (apo more reproducible than geo) — BADLY FRAMED, needs restating.
        I equated apogeotropy with a heavy cupula. Apogeotropy is also produced by FREE debris in the
        ampullary arm, by debris on the canal or utricular side of the cupula, and by transient
        peri-ampullary canalolithiasis; while persistent geotropic DCPN can be a light cupula. Comparing
        "geo versus apo" MIXES a mobile mechanism with an immobile one.
        Data split BY MECHANISM support the intuition: Kim — bow and lean in 63/63 cupulopathies against
        36/66 canalolithiases; Choi — both components in 85/85 cupulopathies against 48/74 canalolithiases.
        Data split BY PHENOTYPE do not: Koju 41% geo vs 27% apo; Califano no difference; Lee both
        components more often in canalolithiasis (62.9%) than in classified cupulolithiasis (41.3%).
        REVISED H5: in patients with a CONFIRMED PERSISTENT CUPULOPATHY — recognised by nystagmus duration
        and a reproducible null plane, and NOT by apogeotropy alone — the BLT should be more reproducible
        than in canalolithiasis with free debris.
     VERDICT: the core of the hypothesis has NOT been refuted and explains the poor BLT results in
        canalolithiasis well, but nobody has yet shown clinically a dependence on the immediately
        preceding position.
        Defensible version: "In lateral-canal canalolithiasis the presence, direction and amplitude of the
        nystagmus in the BLT depend on the current location of the free debris; since that location is
        changed by the preceding position and by earlier tests, the BLT should not be treated as a
        lateralising feature independent of positional history. The dependence should be much weaker in
        confirmed persistent cupulopathy."
        Decisive study: VOG, randomised preparation (affected side / healthy side / neutral), a
        standardised route to sitting, THE BLT AS THE FIRST TEST, separate reporting of bow / lean /
        absence of response / direction / SPV / latency. A PARALLEL design, not crossover — because the
        test itself moves the debris.
     Literature record: memory otorepo-bowlean-evidence.

     FOR CLINICAL DECISION (not mine): (a) the card stops drawing an arrow when the drive is below
     threshold and says "the model does not resolve this"; (b) the direction is taken from the clinical
     rule, explicitly, as a rule rather than a physics result; (c) leave as is. I am not choosing,
     because every option changes clinical content.

     NUMERIC CORRECTIONS TO THE R8 WRITE-UP (assessment II probes — they replace the old sentences):
       (1) "ξ=0.000" is NOT geometry — it is the ADHESION GATE: the bow's drive 0.026 < fStat 0.04
           (a 1.55× margin). Without adhesion a bow from 199.8° gives ξ=−0.832 WITH EXPULSION. The
           sentence "the tangential component stays small at every angle" described the PARKED drive,
           not the response — the free response reaches |ξ|=1.04. CALIBRATION CONSEQUENCE: fStat=0.04
           is PART OF THE CLINICAL CONTENT of the "does not resolve" mode — lowering it below ~0.026
           would turn that mode into a weak response opposite to the rule.

           (bow 9.82°/189.94°, lean 11.05°/191.17°). φ₀ zones (fStat:0): full Choung rule

           bound (inversion below ~10°).

           at THE DIRECTION).

 R11. THE ENGINE EQUATES PHENOTYPE WITH MECHANISM: geo≡canalolithiasis, apo≡cupulolithiasis [2026-08-06, OPEN].
     Came out of verifying H5. For the horizontal canal variantLabels asserts outright "Canalithiasis
     (geotropic)" and "Cupulolithiasis (apogeotropic)", and gufoniApo carries the code comment
     "apogeotropic HC-BPPV = CUPULOLITHIASIS: the debris starts EMBEDDED in the cupula". The literature
     does not support that equation: apogeotropy is also produced by FREE debris in the ampullary arm and
     by debris on the canal or utricular side of the cupula, while persistent geotropic DCPN can be a
     light cupula.
     THE ENGINE CONTRADICTS ITSELF: maneuverSim runs EVERY plan through simulateCanalith, i.e. the physics
     of FREE debris — including gufoniApo, which is labelled mechanism:"cupulo". And the aim of that
     maneuver is the apo→geo CONVERSION, which makes sense ONLY for free debris: debris embedded in the
     cupula has nowhere to move to.
     So the physics is fine and the LABEL is too strong — and it is the label that teaches the clinician
     something the data do not support.
     DIRECTION OF THE FIX: separate the PHENOTYPE (geotropic / apogeotropic — what is seen) from the
     MECHANISM (free debris / cupulopathy — what is inferred from duration and a reproducible null plane).
     That is a change of clinical content, so it is the user's decision, not mine.

 R10. THE ENGINE HAS NO POSITION MEMORY — the debris start is an ASSUMPTION, not a state [2026-08-05, OPEN].
     Came out of taking R8 apart. restPhi computes the resting point for the UPRIGHT posture as if that
     were a property of the anatomy. It is not — it is a property of the patient's last hour. Measured φ
     at the start of a Bow & Lean test (horizontal canal P; the bow's summit is 190°, and which side the
     debris lies on decides the direction):
       long sitting alone (the engine's assumption)   199.8°   past the summit
       supine for 5 min, then sat up                  199.8°   past
       slept on the AFFECTED side, then sat up        132.3°   BEFORE — agrees with the Choung rule
       slept on the HEALTHY side, then sat up         267.3°   past (right at the opening)
       after a Roll test of both sides, then sat up   267.3°   past (right at the opening)
       after a Dix-Hallpike, then sat up              164.9°   BEFORE — agrees with the Choung rule
     A spread of 132–267° is ALMOST THE WHOLE ARC, and two of six realistic scenarios land on the
     "clinical" side. The engine picks one of them and reports it as a result.
     A SIDE EFFECT MORE IMPORTANT THAN BOW & LEAN ITSELF: entering the Roll position THE WAY IT IS
     ACTUALLY PERFORMED (lie supine first, then turn the head) makes the debris LEAVE THE CANAL after
     21 s with the HEALTHY ear down. The R1 check ("the diagnostic does not treat") passes only because
     engineXi jumps sitting→Roll without the lying-down stage. Directionally this matches therapy
     (Vannucchi's forced prolonged position = lying on the HEALTHY side).
     CORRECTION 2026-08-06: I wrote earlier that "the timescale differs: 21 s against hours". THAT WAS A
     BAD COMPARISON — it confused a PARTICLE'S TRANSIT time with the duration of a treatment. Checked from
     the physics side:
       the model's terminal velocity v = R/tauP = 2.68 mm / 6.5 s = 0.412 mm/s;
       by Stokes' law v = (2/9)·Δρ·g·a²/μ that corresponds to a clump radius a ≈ 9.7 µm,
       i.e. a diameter of ~19 µm — mid-range for the values quoted for human otoconia (1–30 µm).
     MORE: the engine's size scaling is tauP = tauP₀/r², i.e. v ∝ r² — EXACTLY Stokes' law.
       size small/medium/large corresponds to radii 7.6 / 9.7 / 13.1 µm and speeds 0.25 / 0.41 / 0.75 mm/s.
     So tauP is NOT a free parameter — it is equivalent to a physiologically sensible clump radius.
     CONSEQUENCE FOR R10'S SCOPE: the "timescale" part FALLS AWAY. Only the lack of POSITION MEMORY remains.
     (A separate, cheap possibility that follows from this: report size in MICROMETRES instead of the
      0.78/1/1.35 multiplier — the clinician gets a number comparable with the literature rather than a
      label without a unit. IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-06: sizeUm → 16 / 21 / 29 µm, shown on the guide card.)
     WHAT TO DO: the engine already takes a timeline — what is missing is modelling THE EXAMINATION AS A
     CONTINUOUS SEQUENCE, where each test starts where the previous one left off. A design change, not
     new physics.
