 R9. LATERAL-CANAL PITCH 10.4° INSTEAD OF THE CANONICAL ~30° [2026-08-05, OPEN].
     THIS IS NOT A DATA ERROR — it is a FRAME unknown. Settled by separating relative from absolute
     quantities (probe 45):
       FRAME-INDEPENDENT, all in agreement: canals mutually orthogonal 87.8/89.8/87.5°
         (PLANE angles, mod 180 — between the signed normals in the file it comes out 92.2/92.5/89.8°,
         because the ampullofugal convention flips some of the vectors; assessment II, C5), coplanarity
         RA∥LP and RP∥LA 16.0° (real range 10–20°), arc radii 3.50/3.32/2.68 mm against Curthoys &
         Oman 3.3/3.2/2.4, mean vertical-canal angle to the sagittal 44.0° (canon 45°), left side =
         mirror of the right (0.000° — a TRANSCRIPTION CONVENTION: the L entries are reflections of the
         P entries, not an independent reconstruction of the left ear; real interaural asymmetry, of the
         order of single degrees, is not modeled — assessment II, C5).
       FRAME-DEPENDENT, the only outlier: the absolute pitch of the lateral canal.
     An atlas of an isolated temporal bone CARRIES NO information about habitual head posture — there is
     no landmark in it that ties the two. So no internal check can either confirm or refute the pitch
     (verified: orthogonality and the angle to the sagittal are INVARIANT under rotation about the
     interaural axis).
     THE SECOND SOURCE AGREES WITH THE ATLAS, NOT WITH THE CANON: Wu 2021 (MRI, n=55, population mean)
     gives 17.4°. Two independent measurements — 10.4° and 17.4° — both BELOW the canonical 30°, both in
     scan frames. The 30° canon itself comes from the caloric convention ("raising the head 30° brings
     the lateral canal vertical"), so it is an inference from an assumption about posture rather than an
     independent measurement.
     CORRECTION TO THE EARLIER ENTRY: I wrote that the pitch hypothesis was REFUTED. That rested on
     probe 39, which examined the PRE-R7 engine and mostly positive pitches. Repeated on the current
     engine (probe 46): a −20° pitch gives exactly the canonical 30.3° and BREAKS NOTHING — maneuvers
     12/12, Dix ✓, head-hang ✓, geotropic Roll with lateralization ✓. The frame pitch is therefore a FREE
     parameter today: the model cannot tell 0° from −20°. I leave it at 0° because that is the only value
     that need not be assumed — but it is a CHOICE, not a result, and should be read as one.
     [FREEDOM-INTERVAL BOUNDS — measured 2026-08-13 (assessment II, B1/GEO-1/GEO-2; the verifier
      confirmed by bisection). The "free parameter" has HARD ends: the interval is roughly
      (−28.2°, +9.9°), not a half-line. At pitch ≥ +9.9° the HORIZONTAL canal's rest degenerates
      (eq exceeds ARC_SPAN=267.3 and the restPhi clamp TELEPORTS the debris by ~264° to the cupula —
      a start in the ampullary arm = an apogeotropic phenotype in the canalo variant; |g|_plane
      drops 0.181→0.031); the threshold lies at the edge of the ±10° band that this very write-up
      calls an irremovable posture uncertainty, and an individual with a 0–3° pitch against Della
      Santina's mean of 20°±7 is not exotic (the clamp branch was SPLIT per canal — IMPLEMENTED in V7: a beyond-the-exit minimum of the
      horizontal canal → rest at the exit, the canal empties instead of teleporting; vertical canals
      unchanged). At pitch ≤ −28.2° the ANTERIOR canal gains a stable rest inside the arc →
      adhesion and latency return — the whole derived "AC-BPPV without latency" signature (R7/R3b)
      holds only for pitches > ~−28°; at −20° (the "indistinguishable" value) the margin past the
      opening is only 7.2° (down from 23.6° at 0°). The probes so far examined ONLY negative
      pitches — R8's sentence "the margin shrinks but never changes sign" was true only for the
      tested half of the axis.]
     >>> SETTLED 2026-08-05 — THE ATLAS'S SOURCE PAPER. <<<
     Ahmadi S-A et al. "IE-Map: a novel in-vivo atlas and template of the human inner ear",
     Sci Rep 2021;11:3293, doi:10.1038/s41598-021-82716-0 (PMID 33558581, PMC7870663, CC BY 4.0).
     THE FRAME IS NAMED: the template is aligned to REID'S STANDARD PLANE, defined by four landmarks —
       the infraorbital point and the external auditory meatus, bilaterally. Quote: "aligned to Reid's
       standard plane, roughly with the horizontal semicircular canal (SCC) in the axial plane". That is
       NOT a scanner frame but a defined cranial plane, and Reid/Frankfort is by definition an
       approximation of the horizontal in natural head posture. TAKING THE PITCH AS 0° IS THEREFORE
       ANATOMICALLY JUSTIFIED, not merely convenient — this corrects the earlier note "0° is a choice".
     THE SOURCE'S LIMIT REMAINS: among the atlas's 94 landmarks there is NOT ONE cranial landmark — all
       annotate inner-ear structures. The paper itself calls the lateral canal's coplanarity with Reid an
       ASSUMPTION and reports no measured deviation. A residual posture uncertainty (Reid vs true
       horizontal) of a few degrees cannot be removed from this source.
     THE 30° CANON IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, not by our atlas:
       Della Santina et al. 2005, JARO, doi:10.1007/s10162-005-0003-x (3D CT): lateral-canal angle to
         Reid's plane 19.5°±7.4 (L) and 20.4°±6.9 (R) — the paper EXPLICITLY notes the difference from
         the textbook 30°;
       Wu et al. 2021 (MRI, n=55): 17.4°;
       IE-Map (our template): 10.4°.
       Three measurements — 20°, 17°, 10° — all BELOW 30°. The canon comes from the caloric convention
       ("raising the head 30° brings the lateral canal vertical"), i.e. an inference from an assumption
       about posture rather than an independent measurement.
     WHAT REMAINS: our 10.4° sits ~1.3 SD below Della Santina's mean — LOW NORMAL, not an error. The rest
       of the gap (10.4 vs 20) may be explained by Reid landmark definitions (infraorbital point vs
       orbitale, meatus centre vs porion) and individual variation. We do NOT adjust the geometry toward
       another paper's mean — stitching sources is exactly what was removed in b5f55b1.
     EFFECT ON R3: none FOR THE FRAME PITCH. [CORRECTION 2026-08-16] But the 30° canon returns to R3 by a
       different entrance: it is THE SAME number the clinic writes down as "raise/flex the head by 30°" in
       the Roll test. The sentence "the canon comes from the caloric convention" has the status of a
       hypothesis here — one documented from secondary sources in R3-MEASURED, THE 30° QUESTION (point b/h),
       where it was also computed how much raising is really needed at this atlas's 10.4° inclination
       (10.30°) and how much the over-flexion to 30° costs. Measurement bibliography: [H17].

 R6-OLD. YACOVINO DOES NOT CLEAR THE ANTERIOR CANAL with an anatomically correct arc [CLOSED — above].
     The gravitational minimum at its deepest step (deep head-hang) is 233.8 deg while the opening is at
     266 deg. Later steps pull the debris back: chin-to-chest -> 115.8, sitting -> 53.6. No hold and no
     tauP fixes this (tested tauP 6.5->2: final phi stays 54-61 deg, the particle merely returns faster).
     EVERY other maneuver has a step whose minimum lies BEYOND the opening: Epley 320 > 307, Semont
     326 > 307, Gufoni geo 265 > 237, Lempert 345 > 237. Yacovino alone does not.
     Its former "success" was an artefact of the too-short arc: at 178 deg the 233.8 minimum lay past the
     end, so the debris was pushed out of the canal. Same mechanism as the removed R4 property.
     WORKING HYPOTHESIS: the cause is the anterior canal's AMPULLA_DIR - an ASSUMPTION, not a
     measurement. Rotating the ampulla direction by about -35 deg in the canal plane puts step 2's minimum
     at 268.8 > 266 and Yacovino starts clearing. We do NOT guess that value: fitting an assumption so
     that one maneuver passes is exactly the mechanism that produced the R1 and R4 artefacts. Awaiting a
     measurement of the ampulla position (the same Cardenas-Serna data contains the three ampulla centres).
     IMPORTANT: nystagmus direction comes from CANAL_NORMALS (nysMag/quickPhase), NOT from AMPULLA_DIR -
     correcting the ampulla shifts only where the debris sits along the arc, touching no clinical direction.

REMAINING (pending decision):

 R1. THE MODEL ARC IS SHORTER THAN THE REAL CANAL -> the provoking position does the maneuver's work.
     The arc is modelled as 180 deg (ampulla phi=0 -> utricle phi=178). In EVERY provoking position the
     arc's gravitational minimum (where the particle should come to rest) lies BEYOND the end of the arc:
        Dix-Hallpike / posterior canal .......... phi_eq ~ 190 deg   (arc end: 178 deg)
        deep head-hang / anterior canal ......... phi_eq ~ 194 deg
        roll, HEALTHY ear down / horizontal ..... phi_eq ~ 210 deg
     The particle therefore has nowhere to settle inside the canal and always runs to the crus/exit.
     Consequences (all reproduced):
       a) A diagnostic Dix-Hallpike drives the debris into the common crus after ~13 s. THE REVERSAL OF
          NYSTAGMUS ON SITTING UP - a Barany-criteria feature, and one this very document promises
          ("Backward motion in the canal lumen -> xi<0 -> REVERSED nystagmus") - is UNREACHABLE for the
          posterior canal at any realistic hold: present up to a ~12 s hold, gone from 13 s on (the app
          holds 40 s). Instead of a reversal the model produces a PRIMARY-direction transient on sitting.
       b) In the Epley the particle reaches the crus already at STEP 2 (the Dix-Hallpike position). Steps
          3 and 4 move phi 164->166->166 with xi 0.74->0.06, i.e. they are physically inert; the whole
          maneuver collapses to "provoke, then sit up". Visible effect: at step 4 (healthy side, nose down)
          xi=0.06 -> strength below the 0.10 threshold -> the app shows NO nystagmus, although clinically
          that step frequently provokes a distinct burst. (The "otolith travel" card hides this - it draws
          a synthetic 0.15->1.0 ramp, not the real phi.)
       c) A 30-second roll test with the HEALTHY ear down EMPTIES the horizontal canal (exited=true) - the
          diagnostic test cures the patient.
          [UNMASKED 2026-08-16 in V25 — item (c) stopped being theoretical. As long as the test pose
           carried a hard-coded 30-degree flexion, the Roll act with a 20 s hold did NOT empty the
           canal; the threshold sat at a 25 s hold. Once the flexion was derived from the canal
           inclination (10.44 degrees) the threshold dropped to 20 s, i.e. EXACTLY the ACT_STEPS.roll
           value — the act empties the canal in 4/4 scenarios, exiting in the "healthy ear down"
           phase at t=46.5-47.0 s against a phase end of 47.4 s (margin 0.4-0.9 s), and the control
           test falls mute. The 30-degree canon MASKED this defect: it bought 3.5-4.2 s of margin on
           the idealised arc. Two errors were cancelling each other. This is NOT an argument for
           going back to 30 degrees — it is an argument for fixing the arc.]
     (Item "d) roll asymmetry" was listed here until 2026-08-05 - MISATTRIBUTED. Moved to R3: it survives
      the arc extension without the slightest change, so it is not a consequence of R1. Do not defer it
      behind this recalibration.)
     THIS IS NOT A ONE-CONSTANT FIX: phiExit=240 restores (a) and (c) but breaks clearing for Semont,
     Yacovino and geotropic Gufoni (verified). It needs a coordinated arc+holds+constants recalibration.

 R2. [RE-MEASURED 2026-08-05 after b5f55b1] DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED, NUMBERS OBSOLETE — this is R7.
     Bascule still does not clear (φ max 137 against an exit of 318.8), but it does NOT "push the debris
     toward the ampulla": it stalls at the UNSTABLE point of the second drop. The cause named back then
     (no inertia) is the same one as in R7 and applies to Semont too — see R7. The description below is
     kept as a record of the pre-rebuild state; its numbers (φ 90→11→78→3→51) come from the old arc and
     the φ=90 start.
 R2-OLD. BASCULE NEVER CLEARS THE CANAL. maneuverSim runs EVERY plan through simulateCanalith, so Bascule (a
     liberatory maneuver for posterior-canal cupulolithiasis) is computed as free-floating debris:
     phi 90->11->78->3->51, exited=false - the debris is pushed TOWARD THE AMPULLA and ends mid-canal.
     Because the plan carries mechanism:"cupulo", the renderer falls into the CONVERSION branch (the
     Gufoni-apo one): travel stops at 0.72 of the path, exitStep=-1, and step 1 keeps the debris glued to
     the cupula. The step text, meanwhile, promises transport into the utricle. The cause is physical: the
     Bascule mechanism is INERTIAL detachment of debris from the cupula, and an overdamped, purely
     gravity-driven model contains no such phenomenon - beyond the narrow crus-expulsion proxy (crusFling).

 R3. [RE-MEASURED 2026-08-06] THE ORIGINAL FORMULATION IS WITHDRAWN; ONE QUESTION REMAINS, DEPENDENT ON R10.
     CLOSED:
       (a) the Roll asymmetry at cupular level — |ξ| affected ear down 0.70 vs healthy 0.59, the order
           already correct in the physics itself (it was 0.518 vs 0.910, i.e. inverted). The DERIVED start
           fixed this, not tuning.
       (b) the ANTERIOR canal's latency — 0.50 s, DERIVED (no adhesion for debris pressed against the
           cupula). The signature of a derivation: it is INDEPENDENT OF DEBRIS SIZE (0.50/0.50/0.45 for
           small/medium/large), which no tuned constant would give.
       (c) THE FORMULATION ITSELF — "the constants were calibrated for the posterior canal, the others
           inherit them without validation" — WITHDRAWN AS INACCURATE. Measured: no constant affecting
           amplitude differs between canals (tauP, tauC, gc, fStat, adh are shared; only the sign of exc
           differs, plus the presence of the crus chamber, which acts only at the opening). The whole
           difference is GEOMETRIC:
             posterior in Dix    |g|_plane 0.999 × sin(Δ)=0.936 → 0.935
             horizontal in Roll  |g|_plane 0.855 × sin(Δ)=0.998 → 0.853   (the angle favours the
                                                                           HORIZONTAL; |g|_plane loses it)
           i.e. 9.6% of drive, entirely from the geometry of the position, not from an inherited constant.
     WHAT REMAINS NUMERICALLY: posterior 2.20 s / 0.87 · horizontal 2.40 s / 0.70, whereas clinically the
       horizontal canal should have the SHORTEST latency and the strongest nystagmus.
     THIS MAY REQUIRE NO ENGINE FIX AT ALL. Measured sensitivity: debris with a 10% larger radius (33%
       larger volume) is enough for the horizontal canal to overtake the posterior one in BOTH quantities
       at once (latency 2.05 vs 2.20 s, peak 1.04 vs 0.87). That fits inside a variable the model ALREADY
       HAS (size), and it has a clinical rationale: the utricle sits above the horizontal canal's
       non-ampullary opening, and HC-BPPV often arises as a canal switch after treating the posterior
       canal — i.e. the debris LITERALLY moves from the posterior canal into the horizontal one. THIS IS
       NOT PROOF, only an indication that the residual difference lies within a clinical variable rather
       than a missing per-canal constant. To be checked against the literature.
       DISPLAY CAVEAT: at size "large" ξ exceeds 1 in both canals (3.04 and 2.43) and dynNystagmus clips
       intensity at 1 — so above medium the difference is INVISIBLE in the UI.
     THE ONLY OPEN QUESTION: WHAT IS the clinical 30° neck flexion in the Roll test FOR, given that in this
       geometry it costs 15% of the drive? Four candidates checked:
         1. to stand the canal vertical — NO: at 0° flexion |g|_plane = 1.000, the optimum falls at −2°
            and does NOT depend on the frame pitch (see the correction to the link with R9).
         2. to ISOLATE the horizontal canal from the vertical ones — NO: the posterior/horizontal drive
            ratio is FLAT at ~0.79 for every flexion; only the anterior canal nulls, near 20°.
         3. to equalise the two Roll positions so that the side comparison is fair — NO: the geometric
            asymmetry is 0.0% at EVERY flexion; both positions are mirror images by construction.
         4. to PROTECT THE TEST FROM BECOMING A TREATMENT — PARTLY. With the HEALTHY ear down the debris
            leaves the canal after 15.3 s (flexion 0°), 20.9 s (30°), 42.4 s (50°). The direction is right,
            but 30° buys only ~6 s — both values fit inside a normal 30–60 s hold.
       AND HERE IS THE BLOCK — BUT NOT WHERE I FIRST PUT IT. This used to read: "candidate 4 is undecidable
       as long as the emptying timescale is wrong — the model clears the horizontal canal in 21 s while
       Vannucchi's forced prolonged position works over hours". THAT WAS WITHDRAWN the same day in R10: the
       timescale is NOT wrong (I had confused a particle's transit with the duration of a treatment), and
       tauP is equivalent to Stokes' law for a ~9.7 µm clump. THE BLOCK is R10's other half — THE LACK OF
       POSITION MEMORY: whether "30° protects the test from becoming a treatment" depends on WHERE the
       debris lies AT THE START, and that is something the engine today ASSUMES rather than knows.
     CONCLUSION: R3 reduces to a single question and is BLOCKED ON R10. Per-canal calibration remains
       DISCOURAGED — there is nothing to calibrate — the difference is geometric, and the rest fits inside
       the amount of debris.

 R3-MEASURED, THE 30° QUESTION (2026-08-16, after R10 was delivered in V10/V19 and the neck wired in V24).
     THE BLOCK FELL: the engine HAS position memory, so "where the debris lies at the start" stopped being
     an assumption. The question "what is the 30° of flexion in the Roll test FOR" could finally be measured
     on a REAL distribution of starting points instead of on a single assumed φ₀.
     PROTOCOL: a pose at an arbitrary flexion = headQOf(0, −90+flex, yaw), neck neckPitch=flex (the V24
       convention + NECK_PREFIX). THREE BIT-FOR-BIT ORACLES before the measurement:
       headQOf(0,−90,·)≡stepHeadQ("supineFlat"), headQOf(0,−60,·)≡stepHeadQ("supineFlex"),
       act(30°,20 s)≡actTimeline("roll"); the P/L mirror 0.0e+0. The act as in the engine: affected 20 s →
       centre 5 s → healthy 20 s → sitting 30 s. Starting points: the 4 BLT_HISTORY scenarios
       (146.2/173.0/221.4/rest 199.8) + a sweep of the arc every 0.05°. dt=0.05, medium.
     RESULT 1 — THE FLEXION DECIDES (hold 20 s, affected-ear-first order = ACT_STEPS.roll):
       flexion  0°: the canal emptied in 4/4 scenarios (95.3% of the arc), φ→267.3 = the opening
       flexion 15°: 4/4 (58.5% of the arc)   ·   flexion 20°: 0/4 (17.0%)   ·   flexion 30°: 0/4 (1.9%)
       The threshold lies between 15° and 20°; at 30° the margin to the edge is ≈11°.
     RESULT 2 — THE CONSEQUENCE IS QUALITATIVE, NOT A MATTER OF SECONDS: at 0° the control retest (act #2 of
       the session chain, after SESSION_REST with readhesion) is SILENT — intensity 0.0000 in ALL 4 scenarios;
       at 30° the retest responds (0.400–0.412). A test at 0° flexion does the maneuver's work and DESTROYS
       ITS OWN CONTROL: there is no way to verify whether the patient recovered or the examination lied.
       That is a stronger formulation than the old "30° buys ~6 s" (that number holds up: t_req 18.4–19.0 s
       at 0° against 23.5–24.2 s at 30°).
     RESULT 3 — WHAT R10 PREVIOUSLY MADE IT IMPOSSIBLE TO SAY: the protection is INDEPENDENT OF THE START.
       The spread of t_req over the whole real range of φ₀ is 0.6 s (18.4–19.0 at 0°; 23.5–24.2 at 30°).
       The old answer read "it depends on where the debris lies, and the engine does not know that" — now it
       does: IT ALMOST DOES NOT DEPEND. That is the real gain from closing R10 in this thread.
     RESULT 4 — THE LIMITATION THAT VOIDS THE SIMPLE ANSWER: the protection is a function of THE HOLD, not of
       the flexion itself (the neutral scenario, affected-first): hold 15 s → does not empty at any flexion;
       20 s → empties at 0°, not at 30°; 25 s and 30 s → empty ALSO at 30°; 45–60 s → empty even at 45°.
       So 30° protects ONLY within a narrow hold band of ~20 s, and at a 30 s hold (which the same entry
       calls "a normal 30–60 s hold") it does not protect at all.
     A CAVEAT AGAINST MY OWN RESULT (mandatory): ACT_STEPS.roll encodes a hold of 20 s — exactly the value
       at which 30° comes out protective. A model agreeing with its own parameter is NOT proof; had the act
       been 30 s, the result would be the opposite. The protection band (18.5–23.5 s) lies in the middle of
       the spread of clinical holds, so the verdict depends on how long THE EXAMINER holds the position.
     THE COST: the peak of the affected-ear phase falls at 30° by 12.9% (afterRoll) to 20.1% (textbook); the
       weakest measured response, 0.323, is 3.2× above XI_CARD — THE CARD NEVER GOES SILENT. The cost is real
       in amplitude, but it does not cost visibility.
     A SIDE FINDING (stronger than the main one) — THE ORDER WEIGHS MORE THAN THE FLEXION: with the
       "healthy ear first" order the affected-ear phase collapses to intensity 0.008–0.086 (the debris had
       time to leave or to move) and THE LATERALIZATION POINTS TO THE WRONG SIDE in 4/4 scenarios at 0°; at
       30° textbook and afterDix are rescued, afterRoll and neutral still mislead. With "affected first" the
       indication is correct in 3/4 (the exception being textbook — an INTERNAL flip for φ₀=146°, independent
       of the emptying). An emergent confirmation of Bhandari 2022, already cited in R10.
     WHAT THE MEASUREMENT DOES NOT DECIDE: the model has no access to anything that may be the real reason
       for the clinical 30° — the patient's comfort, vertebral-artery compression in extension, the canal's
       alignment relative to the axis of rotation in a real performance, the tradition of the write-up. The
       answer reads: IN THE GEOMETRY OF THIS MODEL (one atlas, Reid's frame) 30° makes mechanical sense — it
       protects the test from becoming a treatment and it rescues the control — PROVIDED the hold does not
       exceed ~23 s. That is the model's answer, not the clinic's.
     THE SOURCE STATUS OF 30° — LITERATURE SEARCH PERFORMED 2026-08-16. VERDICT: 30° IS NOT A MEASUREMENT OF
       THE TEST'S PERFORMANCE. It is a number carried over from THE CANON OF CANAL ORIENTATION, and that
       canon has a CALORIC pedigree.
       (a) THE NUMBER IS NOT SETTLED. The literature gives, side by side, "20°", "20–30°", "30° of flexion"
           and — in Hain (dizziness-and-balance.com) — "the head slightly raised, so that the lateral canal
           is optimally oriented", with NO angle at all. A measured quantity would have one value.
       (b) THE JUSTIFICATION IS LITERALLY CALORIC: "raising the head brings the lateral canal into the
           VERTICAL plane". In the caloric test that condition is PHYSICALLY NECESSARY — Bárány's convection
           theory (1906) requires a vertical canal plane for the endolymph density difference to set it in
           motion at all. In a POSITIONAL test there is no convection; there is only gravity acting on the
           debris. The number crossed from one procedure to the other together with its justifying sentence.
       (c) THE LITERATURE CONTRADICTS ITSELF ABOUT THE PURPOSE: some sources say that 30° sets the canal
           VERTICAL, others — that it "levels the lateral canals HORIZONTALLY". These are opposite goals. A
           number whose declared meaning reverses between sources is HANDED DOWN, not derived.
       (d) THE 30° INCLINATION CANON HAS NO COVER IN MEASUREMENTS (cf. R9): [H17] Della Santina et al. 2005
           — VERIFIED AT THE SOURCE: the angle between the lateral canal's normal and the Z axis of Reid's
           frame is 19.9°±7.0 (left), i.e. the canal plane lies ~20° off Reid's horizontal; Wu et al. 2021
           (MRI, n=55) 17.4°; IE-Map (our atlas) 10.4°. AN HONESTY CAVEAT: the sentences in R9 claiming that
           Della Santina's paper "EXPLICITLY notes the difference from the textbook 30°" COULD NOT be
           confirmed at abstract level — that requires the full text; the NUMBER itself is confirmed.
       (e) THE MEASUREMENT IN OUR ENGINE — THE SAME QUANTITY THE CLINICAL JUSTIFICATION IS ABOUT. |g|_plane
           (the fraction of gravity lying IN THE PLANE of the canal) is at its maximum exactly when the canal
           stands "vertical" in the sense of that justification. Measured for the horizontal canal P:
             the NEUTRAL supine position (face up, before the turn): the maximum falls at a flexion of
               +10.30°, |g|_plane = 1.000000 — and this atlas's canal inclination is 10.44°. THE EQUALITY IS
               NOT ACCIDENTAL: to lay the canal vertical the head must be raised EXACTLY BY ITS INCLINATION.
               At +30° |g|_plane = 0.9415 (−5.9% relative to the optimum) — AN OVER-FLEXION of ~20°.
             the TEST position (head turned 90°, ear down): the optimum shifts to −1.77°, and +30° costs
               14.5%.
       (f) CONCLUSION. The clinical justification is GEOMETRICALLY EXACT — but for an anatomy inclined at 30°.
           At the MEASURED inclinations (10–20°) the raising that realizes the same goal is 10–20°, i.e. the
           LOWER edge of the clinical band "20–30°". 30° over-flexes by exactly as much as the canon
           overstates the anatomy. NOT A SINGLE paper comparing raising angles for the sensitivity of the
           Roll test was found — the "20–30°" band is a convention, not an optimum measured on patients.
       (g) EFFECT ON THE RESULT ABOVE: the measured protective effect of 30° (the test does not become a
           treatment, the control stays alive) is therefore A SIDE EFFECT OF THE OVER-FLEXION, not an
           intention — and it has to be described that way. This does not void the measurement: the effect is
           real in this geometry. It voids the narrative "30° was chosen in order to protect the test".
           Separately, a reminder of the limitation: the protection disappears at a hold ≥25 s.
       (h) WHAT WAS NOT DECIDED: the original descriptions by McClure (1985) and Pagnini/Nuti/Vannucchi
           (1989) COULD NOT be read in the original (no access) — so it is not known whether the angle
           occurred in them at all or was glued on later. The caloric pedigree of 30° rests on SECONDARY
           sources (reviews, StatPearls, teaching materials) that agree in content; Bárány's 1906 paper was
           not read. Until (a)+(h) are closed, the sentence "30° comes from the caloric convention" has the
           status of A WELL-DOCUMENTED HYPOTHESIS, not a first-hand fact.
     QUERY II (2026-08-16, after V25 shipped; 24 agents, 58 findings, 20 checked AT SOURCE by separate
       verifiers, 0 errors). WHAT CHANGES IN POINTS (a) AND (h):
       * THE ORIGINALS WERE REACHED - PARTLY. McClure 1985 (PMID 4068089): the ABSTRACT carries NO
         head-elevation angle at all - only "lateral positions" and the movement into and out of them;
         the full text (J Otolaryngol 14(1):30-35) is not freely available. Pagnini/Nuti/Vannucchi 1989
         (PMID 2734007, DOI 10.1159/000276052): the abstract also has NO angle - only "rapid rolling onto
         the side while supine"; the full text sits behind a Karger paywall (USD 45), so an angle in the
         Methods cannot be excluded. CONCLUSION: point (a) ("the number is not fixed") becomes STRONGER -
         neither source abstract carries an angle.
       * THE GAP IS CONFIRMED BY THE RESEARCHERS THEMSELVES: [H37] Hentze et al. 2025 state OUTRIGHT that
         nobody has yet studied the influence of the achieved head angles on the diagnostic outcome. That
         openly confirms the sentence in point (f) - not a single such paper was found because none exists.
       * THE FIRST EMPIRICAL SIGNAL ABOUT DIRECTION [H36]: in a randomised crossover study with IMU
         measurement, true positives had the head raised LESS (-69.0 deg) than false negatives (-63.5 deg),
         and the authors recommend exceeding the -60 deg target toward -75 deg. In our convention:
         -60 deg = 30 deg of flexion, -69 deg = 21 deg, -75 deg = 15 deg. THE DIRECTION AGREES with V25.
         BUT: the difference is NOT significant (p=0.128 / 0.661) and n=15 lateral-canal diagnoses. It is
         a signal, not proof, and may only be cited as such.
       * THE SCALE OF THE DISPUTE IN PERSPECTIVE [H37]: the angles actually achieved are -64.4+/-9.3 and
         -63.4+/-8.7 against a -60 target, and the variability of all head angles is +/-8.7-11.0 deg. The
         execution spread is therefore LARGER than the whole 30 -> 10.30 difference this dispute is about.
         Practical conclusion: the choice of angle matters FOR THE MODEL (which executes it exactly), while
         at the bedside it disappears into the examiner's hand tremor. Record it and do not overrate it.
       * THE CALORIC PEDIGREE: STATUS UNCHANGED. It still rests on secondary sources; Barany's 1906 paper
         was not read. The status "a well-documented hypothesis" STANDS.
     IMPLEMENTATION (V25, 2026-08-16, the user's decision): conclusion (f) entered the engine — the
       Roll-test pose takes its flexion from the LATERAL CANAL INCLINATION of this atlas
       (HC_TILT_DEG = 10.4398 degrees) rather than from a textbook, and the same constant feeds every
       clinical text that mentions the angle. The full protocol, the control of the derivation, the
       consequences and the NAMED COST (unmasking R1(c)) are in the section "THE ROLL-TEST POSE
       FLEXION DERIVED FROM ANATOMY (V25)". Measurement (e) of this entry was thereby CONFIRMED
       independently in production code: |g|_plane 0.999997 at a flexion equal to the inclination,
       0.9415 at 30 degrees.
     THE MEASUREMENT'S INTEGRITY: the adversarial layer FELL (3 of the 5 workshop agents, including BOTH
       critics, ended in API errors). The core was verified by independent code driving the main loop: the
       pose oracles bit-for-bit, the act's identity with actTimeline, the P/L mirror, 4 scenarios ×
       {0°,30°} × {emptying, retest, cost} + the hold-robustness table. During verification AN ERROR OF MY
       OWN was caught: the first lateralization comparison ran on the raw |ξ| instead of on the intensity
       after the Ewald-II rectification (the rectified result is the one in "the side finding"). The
       measurement has NO independent adversarial control — in a dispute over any single number it must be
       re-measured from scratch.

 R3-OLD. [PARTLY ANSWERED 2026-08-16 in V25 — NOT by tuning the constants but by the POSE: the
     horizontal canal was being tested at a 30-degree flexion that took 5.9% of the in-plane gravity
     away from it in the setup pose and 14.5% in the test pose. Once the flexion was derived from the
     canal inclination the HC latency fell 2.30->2.00 s (the gap against the PC going from 1 to 7 grid
     steps) and the peak rose 0.696->0.784 (the distance from the PC going from -20.2% to -10.1%). The
     complaint "the numbers are born at the start point phi=90" STANDS — the remaining difference is
     still geometric. Per-canal constant tuning remains DISCOURAGED. The numbers below are the record
     from BEFORE the neck wiring and the pose change.]
     THE CONSTANTS WERE CALIBRATED FOR THE POSTERIOR CANAL ONLY; the horizontal and anterior canals inherit
     them without their own validation and come out clinically backwards:
        latency:  posterior 1.95 s . HORIZONTAL 2.30 s . anterior 3.15 s
        peak xi:  posterior 0.810 . HORIZONTAL 0.518 . anterior 0.668
     Clinically horizontal-canal BPPV has the SHORTEST latency (often absent) and the STRONGEST nystagmus -
     the model gives it an intermediate latency and the weakest nystagmus. (Cupulolithiasis: latency 0.05 s
     and full persistence over 60 s for all three canals - correct.)
     ALSO BELONGS HERE (moved from R1 on 2026-08-05): THE ROLL ASYMMETRY IS INVERTED AT THE CUPULAR LEVEL -
       |xi| affected ear down 0.518 vs healthy ear down 0.910. The clinically correct ordering is rescued
       only by the Ewald-II rectification in dynNystagmus (0.518 vs 0.409) - a 27% margin protecting the
       engine's main lateralization rule.
     MECHANISM (common to all of R3) - the numbers are born at the START POINT phi=90, not at the arc end:
       drive(90 deg) = |g.e1| / tauP     (tang(90 deg) = -e1)     latency ~ adh / (drive - fStat)
         posterior  |g.e1|=0.985 -> drive 0.1515 -> lat theor. 1.79 s (measured 1.95)
         HORIZONTAL |g.e1|=0.854 -> drive 0.1314 -> lat theor. 2.19 s (measured 2.30)
         anterior   |g.e1|=0.696 -> drive 0.1071 -> lat theor. 2.98 s (measured 3.15)
       The latency ordering follows SOLELY from how well the provoking position aligns gravity with the
       ampulla direction e1 at phi=90. The anterior canal fares worst because the deep head-hang puts only
       0.72 of gravity into its plane (posterior in Dix: 1.00).
     INDEPENDENCE FROM R1 - VERIFIED: at phiExit 178 / 240 / 270 all six numbers (latency and peak xi for
       the three canals) are IDENTICAL to the digit; so is the roll asymmetry. The arc end does not appear
       in the computation above. R3 can therefore be fixed SEPARATELY and CHEAPLY - one constant per canal:
       horizontal at tauP=4.0 -> lat 1.25 s / xi 0.670; at tauP=3.0 -> lat 0.90 s / xi 0.766 (a single
       parameter orders both symptoms; pushing xi above the posterior canal also needs gc).
     ORDERING CAVEAT: R1 and R3 are not cause and effect, but they share a root - the idealized (e1,e2)
       parametrization with a fixed phi=90 start. Fixing R1 by phiExit alone does not move R3 (measured).
       Fixing R1 by RE-PARAMETRIZING the arc (different angular span, different start) will change e1 and
       phi_start, and hence - directly from the formula above - will move R3's numbers too. In that case
       calibrate R3 AFTER the geometry rebuild, or you will tune constants against geometry you are about
       to replace.


 R5. [CLOSED 2026-08-06 — clinical decision by the USER] THE LAST STEP SHOWS THE LIBERATORY NYSTAGMUS.
     The original charge — a step's label computed from the PREVIOUS step's response, with an INVERTED
     sign — no longer occurs in ANY of the 64 steps (7 maneuvers × 2 sides). Longer derived holds (R4b)
     and inertia (R7) fixed it: every step now produces a response larger than what it inherited.
     CORRECTION 2026-08-06: this entry used to say "2 steps out of 64 remain: Bascule step 5, the
       reported 0.19 is inherited". THAT IS OBSOLETE. The strict criterion — does the reported peak SIT
       AT THE START of the segment, i.e. is it inherited — now yields 0/64. Bascule step 5 produces its
       OWN 0.376 after 1.8 s, and the debris leaves the canal in that very step (t 146.0 s). The earlier
       number came from a criterion that confused the NATURAL DECAY of ξ with a sign inversion — the
       same mistake that produced 4 false hits in the probe.
     DECISION (display, not physics): the last step DOES show the fading nystagmus, because it is a REAL
       signal, and clinically a liberatory nystagmus is exactly what is seen once the debris is released.
     IMPLEMENTATION: the frontal card carries an explicit "liberatory nystagmus" badge (svg-screens.js:
       libNote, style .libnote in index.html). It lights up in the step where the PHYSICS releases the
       debris from the canal (manExitStep = the segment containing the first man.sim.exited) — i.e. from
       the SAME run that produces the number on the card, so the label cannot drift from the value.
       Steps AFTER the expulsion need no separate case: their |ξ| falls below the 0.10 threshold and no
       card is shown (Lempert 4-6, Gufoni geo 3-4). The badge also carries a tooltip explaining that this
       is a sign of SUCCESS, not a relapse.
       Distribution (identical on both sides): Epley s5 · Semont s4 · Bascule s5 · Yacovino s4 — the LAST
       step; Lempert s3 of 6 · Gufoni geo s2 of 4 — earlier, because there the debris leaves earlier;
       Gufoni apo NONE — it is a CONVERSION maneuver, the debris never leaves the canal.
       Note: for the HORIZONTAL canal the migration drawing uses the n−2 schematic (no crus model), so it
       may point at a different step than the badge. The badge deliberately follows the PHYSICS — see the
       comment at manFractions.
     The description below is kept as a record of the pre-R4b/R7 state; its numbers come from the old geometry.

 R5-OLD. stepXiPeak IS NOT A "PER-STEP" MEASURE [found 2026-08-05 while unifying the geometry].
     It takes max|ξ| over a window that STARTS at the segment boundary, so it also picks up ξ INHERITED
     from the previous step (the cupula relaxes with tauC=5 s while maneuver steps last 6-12 s — the tail
     has no time to decay). A step's nystagmus label can therefore be computed from the PREVIOUS step's
     response. EVIDENCE (Bascule step 5, the only maneuver oscillating hard enough to expose it):
       old geometry: ξ_start −0.50, the step's own response +0.59 → stepXiPeak = +0.59 ("primary")
       new geometry: ξ_start −0.75, the step's own response +0.50 → stepXiPeak = −0.75 ("REVERSED, weak")
     The PHYSICS direction is the SAME in both cases (own response positive, φ rises 4→49°); only the
     "which is larger in magnitude" tie-break flipped. This is the ONLY difference the geometry unification
     introduced into the golden snapshot (2 of 91 screens: guide/bascule/{P,L}/step4).
     SAME ROOT as the masking described in R4b: short hold + long cupular time constant = a tail posing as
     a response. FIX: compute the step peak on ξ MINUS the inherited value, or only from the moment |ξ|
     stops decreasing monotonically. It will move labels in several steps of several maneuvers → separate
     commit + per-step verification in the browser.



=== MANEUVERS AS QUATERNION TIMELINES (entry into the dynamics) ===
The maneuver plan → engine bridge. simulateCanalith uses ONLY gHead (gravity in the head frame).
Since 2026-08-05 (b5f55b1) the direction of dependency is REVERSED: a step defines a POSE (anatomical
angles taken from the clinical description) and gravity is DERIVED from it. Previously the opposite
held — a step supplied a hand-written gHead vector and the pose was reconstructed from it via qFromG
(which lost the anatomical roll and let the drawing, the physics and the instruction drift apart
independently; the drift reached 70°).
Head frame: x=right ear, y=skull(+crown), z=nose.
Functions (after xiEnvelope in the file):
- POSE_SPEC[body|face] → {roll, trunk, pitch, dyaw} in DEGREES, each number from a sentence of the
  instruction shown to the clinician:
    roll  = rotation about the body's long axis (side ±90 / prone 180 / Semont's lateral drop)
    trunk = trunk angle in the sagittal plane (sitting 0, lying −90, hip hinge +45)
    pitch = angle of the WHOLE HEAD = trunk + neck; neck = pitch − trunk (this feeds the skeleton)
    dyaw  = neck rotation contained in the pose description (added to the step's yaw)
- stepHeadQ(body,yaw,face) → Rz(roll)·Rx(pitch)·Ry(yaw+dyaw); head→world head orientation.
- stepGravity(body,yaw,face) → gHead(stepHeadQ(...)) — gravity is DERIVED from the pose.
- composeHead == stepHeadQ — drawing and physics are one function (audit #1's invariant is an identity).
- TORSO_Q[body] → Rz(roll)·Rx(trunk), derived from the same table (was written out separately).
- maneuverTimeline(plan,size) → [{q,tTrans:0.8,tHold}] with the hold DERIVED (R4b).
- maneuverSim(plan) → simulateCanalith over the timeline → φ(t) of the particle in the canal.
  [2026-07-02] Lempert STEP 1 (head turned toward the affected side, horizontal canal): yaw must be ±90° —
  at 45° ξ_peak≈0 (no provocation). The clinical instruction says "90°"; the code said 45°, fixed.
PARTICLE START: not a constant but restPhi(canal,side) = the gravitational minimum in the upright
posture, or the cupula when that minimum lies outside the arc. Posterior 48.8° · horizontal 199.8° ·
anterior 3°.
VALIDATION (re-measured 2026-08-13, real generated plans, both sides, exit = measured ARC_SPAN):
  Epley ✓✓ (49→158→230→307→319) · Yacovino ✓✓ (3→136→262→281) · Lempert ✓✓ · Gufoni geo ✓✓
  Semont ✓✓ (49→138→268→319) · Bascule ✓✓ (49→3→140→307→319) — clearing since R7 (specific force f=g−a)
  Gufoni apogeotropic ✗✗ — CORRECTLY: a CONVERSION maneuver (apo→geo), it does not clear directly.


=== ANTERIOR-CANAL TEST (deep head-hang) + a note on consistency ===
Diagnostics: the 'headhang' test (deep head-hang, supineHang/yaw0/face:up) → DOWNbeat nystagmus + torsional
toward the affected ear; treatment: Yacovino. Lateralization of the anterior canal is sometimes uncertain (torsion often weak).
RESOLVED: the anterior canal's GEXC had the WRONG SIGN (RA/LA = +e1 = ampullopetal) instead of -e1
(ampullofugal, like the posterior). Rule: the excitation axis = -e1 (the ampullofugal tangent at φ=90°).
Fix: GEXC RA:[1,0,1]→[-1,0,-1], LA:[-1,0,1]→[1,0,-1]. After it, position()↔simulateCanalith
CONSISTENT 24/24 (all canals/sides/positions). The antHeadHangNys workaround REMOVED — the deep head-hang test
and the Dix toggle now use nysFromGeom (with quickPhase anterior t:0 → pure downbeat).

=== CLINICAL CORRECTION: anterior canal = PURE DOWNBEAT (no torsion) ===
quickPhase('anterior') changed from {0,-1,ipsi} to {0,-1,0}. Reason: clinically deep head-hang produces
primarily/exclusively downbeat; the torsion is trace/variable/absent → lateralization by nystagmus is
UNRELIABLE (anterior canals near the sagittal plane). The idealized CANAL_GEOM geometry
gave vertical:torsion = 1:1 (like the posterior: normal [0.71,0,±0.71]) → overestimation of torsion. The change applies
everywhere (deep head-hang test, dial, frontal eyes, Yacovino). arrowGlyph: dir=0 → pure vertical (↓).
Deep head-hang test: the torsion-lateralization declaration removed (latNote/label/note/recommend).

=== DIFFERENTIATION IN DIX-HALLPIKE: posterior vs anterior canal (derived from the engine) ===
The dynamic probe (simulateCanalith, ξpeak≈0.81) in the Dix-Hallpike position confirms the coplanarity of LARP/RALP:
  RIGHT Dix-Hallpike (qSupineYaw +45 — historical probe-pose name; today stepHeadQ('supineHang',45,'up')):
        posterior RIGHT → ↑+torsional (v+1,t+1);  anterior LEFT → ↓downbeat (v-1,t0)
  LEFT  Dix-Hallpike (qSupineYaw -45): posterior LEFT  → ↑+torsional;            anterior RIGHT → ↓downbeat
Differentiator = the vertical direction: ↑→posterior (lower ear); ↓→anterior (the OPPOSITE ear, same plane).
(After the GEXC=-e1 fix, static position() agrees with the dynamics — nysFromGeom computes the nystagmus directly.)
UI: in the Dix test, the 'Observed nystagmus' toggle (↑torsional / ↓downbeat) — the downbeat mode switches
the animation (pure downbeat), the mechanism (anterior canal of the opposite ear), the recommendation (Yacovino) and shows
a CENTRAL RED FLAG (isolated/persistent downbeat without latency → cerebellum/craniocervical junction).

=== NYSTAGMUS MAGNITUDES FROM REAL CANAL NORMALS (instead of ideal 45°) ===
SOURCE (since 2026-08-05): the IE-Map atlas [13] — the SAME CANAL_NORMALS from which the engine's entire
  geometry derives (planes from a PCA of the duct-axis points; see the vestibular.js header and the bibliography).
  Wu S, Lin P, Zheng Y, Zhou Y, Liu Z, Yang X (2021). "Measurement of Human Semicircular Canal
  Spatial Attitude". Front Neurol 12:741948 (MRI, n=55) — the source of the normals until 2026-08-05, today
  an EXTERNAL CONTROL (17.1° agreement with IE-Map). Directional canon: Della Santina CC et al. (2005),
  JARO 6:191-206. Internal validation: RA∥LP and RP∥LA coplanarity 16.0° (plane angle).

NORMAL VECTORS (live CANAL_NORMALS, head frame x=right, y=up, z=nose; L = mirror of P):
  posterior P[0.6220, 0.1612, 0.7662] · anterior P[-0.8038, 0.0391, 0.5937]
  · horizontal P[-0.0304, 0.9834, -0.1786]

MAPPING TO MAGNITUDES (|components| of the normal in the HEAD FRAME; beat directions from the clinical
convention in quickPhase): VERTICAL ∝ |x|(interaural) · HORIZONTAL ∝ |y|(cranial) · TORSION ∝ |z|(naso-occipital).
nysMag() normalizes to max=1.

REAL PROPORTIONS (max=1, live 2026-08-13):  posterior v0.81:h0.21:t1.00 · anterior v1.00:h0.05:t0.74
  · horizontal v0.03:h1.00:t0.18
  (The previous numbers of this section — 0.93/0.41/0.44/0.78/0.29 — came from the Wu vectors and became
   stale once the source was swapped to IE-Map; the largest divergence: anterior h 0.44→0.05.
   Assessment II, GEO-3/K2.)

CLINICAL MASK (constant NYS_SHOW) — geometry gives the magnitudes, but the clinical convention decides
WHICH components we express (each can be revealed with a true flag):
  posterior.h=false  geom.≈0.21 — clinically omitted (the posterior is described as vertical-torsional).
                                   SIGN FIXED 2026-08-13 (assessment II, A4): quickPhase now ties
                                   posterior.h to −ipsi (CONTRA direction −0.21, from the signed
                                   geometry Ω=−n) — the flag is safe to reveal.
  posterior.t=true                — torsion expressed; real v:t ≈ 0.81:1 (was a rigid 1:1)
  anterior.t=TRUE    geom.≈0.74 — MASK REMOVED 2026-08-16 (V26, the user's decision). The anterior
                                   canal's torsion is now EXPRESSED: +0.7386*ipsi, i.e. the upper poles
                                   toward the AFFECTED ear. The SIGN was derived from the signed geometry
                                   by the same rule with which A4 corrected posterior.h, and confirmed
                                   clinically [H32]. The old "anterior = pure downbeat" was a clinical
                                   decision, not a result of the geometry; it has been withdrawn. The
                                   clinical reason does NOT disappear, it changes addressee: it is not
                                   the model that should lack torsion but the PATIENT who often does
                                   (42% of positional downbeats, 57% of confirmed AC-BPPV - [H31]) -
                                   and that is what the cards now say.
                                   RULE OF SIGN (calibrated 2026-08-16 on the exposed, validated
                                   components - horizontal.h, posterior.v, posterior.t, anterior.v -
                                   and reproducing ALL of them): v = -sign(W_x)*m.v, h = +sign(W_y)*m.h,
                                   t = -sign(W_z)*m.t, where W = +n for the horizontal canal (Ewald II)
                                   and W = -n for the vertical canals (Ewald III). For anterior.t the
                                   rule predicts +0.7386*ipsi - exactly what was hand-written, so the
                                   unmasking needed no sign change. Use this test on EVERY next mask.
                                   TRAP NOTED (NOT fixed - the flag is dormant): the hand-written sign of
                                   anterior.h is +ipsi while the rule gives -ipsi, exactly the mismatch A4
                                   found in posterior.h. Correct it before ever exposing that flag.
                                   A FOURTH mask, absent from this table: horizontal.v (geom. 0.03) is
                                   zeroed by a literal `v:0` in quickPhase, not by a flag.
  (superseded record) anterior.t=false - CLINICAL OVERRIDE: pure downbeat; "anterior = pure downbeat"
                                   was a clinical decision (torsion observationally a trace), NOT a
                                   result of the geometry. Held until 2026-08-16.
                                   It fell not for convenience but because the literature query showed
                                   that AC torsion IS sometimes present and CARRIES THE SIDE ([H30]
                                   downbeat+torsion in a series; [H32] upper pole toward the affected
                                   ear); its absence in some patients is a fact about the PATIENT, not
                                   about the geometry of the canal.
  horizontal.t=false geom.≈0.18 — clinically purely horizontal (the small geometric torsion omitted)
Beat signs and masks verified: posterior upbeat+ipsi torsion, anterior downbeat + ipsi torsion, horizontal toward the ear.
The only immediately visible change vs before: posterior v:t = 0.81:1 (real) instead of 1:1.

DESIGN DECISION — EXCEPTION TO THE anterior.t MASK FOR SCDS/TULLIO (2026-07-09):
  PROBLEM: the Tullio phenomenon (dehiscence of the SUPERIOR/anterior canal) produces a VERTICAL-TORSIONAL
  nystagmus in which the anterior canal's TORSION is the DISTINGUISHING SIGN (it identifies the canal plane).
  The clinical mask "anterior = pure downbeat" (anterior.t=false) — correct for BPPV — would here ZERO OUT
  this diagnostic torsion.
  CONSIDERED and REJECTED: globally revealing anterior.t (=true). It would change the rendering and geometry
  of anterior-canal BPPV → a risk of REGRESSION of the validated tests (t1–t5 + the BPPV path) and a change
  to the established clinical convention "pure downbeat".
  CHOSEN: nysMag() was EXPORTED from the Vestibular module; NeuroVOR.pressureStimulus() builds the Tullio
  vector from the FULL geometry (nysMag: anterior v1.00 : t0.74), BYPASSING the mask — LOCALLY, only for SCDS.
  The NYS_SHOW mask remains globally in force (BPPV unchanged). The anterior canal's torsion is thus
  "hidden" for BPPV rendering, but AVAILABLE via the raw geometry for the pressure path.
  → see NeuroVOR MODULE / STAGE 7 · pressureStimulus() ([H18] Minor 2000).

=== SYMPTOM LAYER — the "Vertigo/Nausea" bar + the RD card (D5/V17, 2026-08-14) ===
A PURELY VIEW-SIDE overlay on the maneuver's existing cupular-deflection trace ξ(t) (computeManSim) —
zero engine calls, zero physics changes (proof: engine/pose is bit-for-bit at rebaseline).
 VERTIGO ~ the current RAW |ξ| (no min(1,·)): 100% of the bar = |ξ|=1 — DELIBERATELY the same anchor
   as the eye-animation saturation (nysStrength: min(1,|ξ|)) — the bar saturates exactly where the eyes
   stop accelerating; a value above 1 is NOT lost (numeric readout + the .over class; big reaches 3.04 —
   without this the layer would teach that size stops mattering above medium). The visibility floor
   SYM_FLOOR=0.03 = the 3%-of-peak cut-off convention in xiEnvelope; XI_CARD=0.10 stays the threshold
   of the nystagmus CARD — the bar deliberately also shows the subthreshold residue (the symptom may
   persist after the card has gone out).
 NAUSEA = exponential saturation of the accumulated dose: 100·(1−exp(−Σ|ξ|·Δt / NAUS_I0)),
   NAUS_I0=25 ξ·s (a calibration constant). Calibration (probe 2026-08-14): a full Epley/Semont/
   Yacovino/Lempert medium → 76–78% (the standard maneuvers cluster at ~3/4 of the scale), small 51%,
   big 97%; during a medium Epley: after step 2 → 44%, after 3 → 63%, after 4 → 76% (these values are
   PINNED in golden as the steps' initial states). The cum prefix is computed once in computeManSim —
   nausea is deterministic and stateless, robust to jumping between steps.
 A DELIBERATE DECISION — NAUSEA WITHOUT EWALD II RECTIFICATION: sensory conflict does not rectify,
   inhibition nauseates just the same. Consequence: Lempert nauseates like Epley (∫|ξ|=35.9 vs 36.9)
   despite its weaker nystagmus in the ampullofugal steps (∫REC=23.4). Do NOT "fix" this to ∫REC —
   that would turn the symptom layer into a second nystagmus indicator.
 CLOCK: the step's ANIMATION axis (the same start as otolith/nystagmus), NOT the user's timer — with
   a manually extended timer the symptoms die out after the step's physics window ends, consistently
   with the eyes (do not report this as a bug). BOUNDARY: an illustrative layer, not a clinical
   measurement (the tooltip says so explicitly).
 THE "WHAT NEXT — RD" CARD on the LAST step of a CLEARING maneuver (man.exited && step n−1; expulsion
   can come earlier — the finale is the natural "what next" moment, and the liberatory step already
   has its libNote): residual dizziness (RD) in 31–61% after the nystagmus has resolved (the utricle +
   incomplete compensation), counselling cuts RD to ~13%, vestibular suppressants are HARMFUL —
   counselling + rehabilitation [H26] Özgirgin 2024; phrasing 1:1 with the NeuroVOR module (cross-engine
   consistency). gufoniApo (a conversion, exited=false) DELIBERATELY gets no card — its note orders
   a repeat Roll. Oracle scope: EXACTLY 65 guide/* keys (each = a .symrow insert; the 12 clearing
   finales additionally get the RD card), dom with no new keys.

=== DEBRIS CLOUD — the multi-particle ensemble (D9/V20, 2026-08-16) ===
ORCHESTRATION ABOVE THE ENGINE, no physics (zero edits to vestibular.js): the cloud = N=9
independent simulateCanalith simulations on ONE canonical timeline (the clinician performs ONE
maneuver — re-deriving the holds per particle would give each its own protocol and bypass the
substance of partial repositioning).
 THE GRID (ensembleSim/ENS_GRID): the midpoint quantiles of the uniform distribution on
   [0.7,1.3]·r_size — m_k = 0.7+0.6·(k+0.5)/9. SOURCE BOUNDARY: the in-vivo size distribution of
   the aggregate is UNMEASURED (see the note at H2) — the uniform band is a declaration of
   ignorance, not a measurement; do NOT calibrate it. Midpoint quantiles converge to the integral
   over the distribution (aggregate peak 1.0071/1.0085/1.0091 for N=7/9/13); a grid WITH ENDPOINTS
   overweights the extremes and drifts with N (1.061→1.035) — the old "1.085" anchor from
   assessment II is an artifact of such a coarse N=5 grid (reproduced 1.0865); the qualitative
   thesis (dispersion shifts forward and raises the peak) stands, there is NO target number. The
   grid's center (k=4) = EXACTLY 1.0 → the middle particle is BIT-EQUAL to the canonical maneuverSim.
 AGGREGATION = the arithmetic MEAN of ξ over the particles: the engine ALREADY scales gain∝r³
   inside simulateCanalith — an explicit extra r³ weight would count the mass TWICE (measured
   1.353, outside the CUP_WEAK/SPV calibration) and is FORBIDDEN. fracMass (r³) is a separate
   quantity: the fraction of MASS removed, not a signal weight.
 PARTIAL REPOSITIONING — EMERGENT from the canonical holds (zero new constants): the derivedHold
   guard TAUP_GUARD=1.1 guarantees clearing for r ≥ 1/√1.1 ≈ 0.953 — and exactly the particles with
   r<0.95 fall out of the windows. The table (medium, P, pinned in engine.ensemble): epley 8/9 (96%
   of the mass) · semont/bascule/yacovino 6/9 (84%) · lempert/gufoniGeo/zuma/kim 9/9 · gufoniApo
   0/9 (a CONVERSION — the card renders it as a conversion, not as a failure). The card teaches
   this directly: the FINEST particles remain (the slowest ones: τ ∝ r⁻², Stokes' law) — "repeat
   the positional test".
 SEPARATE AXES: rep does NOT enter the cloud (rep₂/rep₀ = fatigueFactor(2) identically to 6 digits
   for the single particle and for the aggregate — gc enters linearly, the φ trajectory is
   independent of gc) → the ensemble does NOT replace fatigueFactor (the cloud = SIZE dispersion
   within one provocation; fatigueFactor = clump disintegration BETWEEN provocations, Imai) — a
   separate calibration decision, outside V20. The canonical single simulation REMAINS the source:
   of derivedHold/timers, of manExitStep/migration, of the V10/V19 session, of symRow/RD (D5), of
   the sensitivity/EXPEL_SANE oracles. The cloud is a VIEW only (the card's numbers + the row of
   dots; drawing the cloud on the otolith path is FORBIDDEN — the manFractions ramp is schematic,
   positional realism would be fabrication).
 CANAL SWITCH = A NOTE (not orchestration): the utricle lies over the non-ampullary HC opening —
   part of HC-BPPV arises as a canal switch after treatment of the posterior canal (see R3); full
   orchestration would require a p(switch) with no source and seeding φ₀ in another canal (session
   territory) — a separate candidate. A design probe: the dynamics AFTER entry would be honest
   (HC φ₀≈250–265 at the opening, settled:false → sitting up slides the debris toward restPhi, the
   control Roll = geotropic DCPN +0.82..0.86/−0.91..−0.92, the phenotype insensitive to the depth
   of entry).
 UI: an opt-in "Debris cloud" in the #sessionbar shell (outside #app — golden untouched by
   construction), the card ONLY on the guide's final step, OUTSIDE a session (the !state.session
   gate: a session tells the story of ONE debris clump with a history). The clinical row
   DELIVERED 2026-08-16 after source verification (the user's decision): "~80% after a single
   session + rises with repetition" [H27]; the RCT context (56% vs 21%, OR 4.42) from [H29];
   the original "80–93%" REJECTED — the upper values come from REPETITIONS, not from a single
   session. Oracles: engine.ensemble (the grid + 9 maneuvers ×
   2 sides: fractions/peak/tExit per particle; a diffKeys line in --check) + the hard CLOUD ORACLE
   (throw: the center ≡ the canonical run bit-for-bit — the card cannot diverge from the eyes)

   "OFF means zero bytes"; dom 174→177). Successor candidate: a cloud session (a chain per

 WHAT IT IS: an examination mode (the third button of the #sessionbar shell, state.exam, default
   null = the bit-identical path). The patient = a LIST of lesions [{canal, side, variant, mech}]

   function of the patient, not of the leftovers of the previous lesson).
 PRIORS (VALUES SET BY CLINICAL DECISION — a source boundary; the epidemiology is ASSUMED, not
   measured): pcCanalo 0.73 · pcCupulo 0.05 · hcGeo 0.07 · hcApo 0.035 · acCanalo 0.02 ·
   multiPcHc 0.075 · bilatPc 0.02 (sum = 1, a HARD throw in the oracle — deliberately NOT at
   import: a typo should fail CI, not kill the PWA boot). The marginals agree with the app's
   labels: PC-any 0.875 (~85%), HC mono 0.105 (~10%), AC 0.02 (~1–2%), multi 7.5% (spec 5–10%),
   bilat 2% (spec 1–2%). Side 50/50 (the literature: a right-side preponderance of ~1.4:1,
   von Brevern 2007 — open to decision). OUTSIDE the pool (model boundaries, not epidemiology):
   the healthy patient (a negative test does not rule out BPPV — a false reflex), light/short

   explicit seed literal). THE DRAW ORDER = A GOLDEN CONTRACT: r1=row (weighted by p) → r2=side.
 CROSS-TEST (the crux): examPhaseNys(lesions, q, rep) — ONE DYNAMIC path for every mechanism
   (canalo→simulateCanalith, cupulo→simulateCupulolith via engineXi; the static nysFromGeom is

   0.45 while sitting, where the dynamics says 0.024=silence). A POINTWISE sum of the nystagmus

   the amplitude (the components peak at different moments). An assessment II probe: the
   additivity of SIMULTANEOUS vectors confirmed (no couplings). The kind of the sum = the

   carried by a label GENERATED from the same vector + the dial's full anat. The override in

   the cards; sequentiality and self-clearing → the continuous session). Measured cross anchors

=== THE ROLL-TEST POSE FLEXION DERIVED FROM ANATOMY (V25, 2026-08-16) ===
 WHAT: the neck-flexion angle of the Roll-test pose (POSE_SPEC "supineFlex|up") is no longer a number
   copied from a textbook. The pose: pitch = -90 + HC_FLEX_DEG (it was -60, i.e. a hard-coded "~30").
   TWO constants, both derived from the same atlas normal, deliberately SEPARATED (the first version of
   this change confused them — caught by the geometry critic):
     HC_TILT_DEG = acos(|n_y|/|n|) = 10.4398 deg — the FULL (dihedral) inclination of the canal plane.
       This is the quantity COMPARABLE WITH THE LITERATURE: Della Santina [H17] measures exactly it
       (the angle of the normal to Reid's Z axis) = 19.9, Wu 2021 = 17.4. Used for text comparisons only.
     HC_FLEX_DEG = atan2(|n_z|,|n_y|) = 10.2955 deg — the SAGITTAL FLEXION at which gravity actually
       comes to lie in the canal plane. IT SETS THE POSE. Neck flexion rotates the head about the
       INTERAURAL axis, so it only moves the (y,z) components of the normal; the x component (-0.0304 =
       a slight tilt of the canal plane in the FRONTAL plane) is UNREACHABLE for it — hence the 0.14 deg
       difference.
   At HC_FLEX_DEG the in-plane |g| is 1.000000 EXACTLY (at the 10.4398 inclination it would be 0.999997,
   at 30 it is 0.9415). Neither number is an optimiser result — there is no optimiser in the code.
   HC_TILT_TXT ("10") feeds EVERY clinical instruction mentioning this angle, so a text-vs-physics drift
   is structurally impossible (the same principle as POSE_SPEC on 2026-08-05).
 WHY: the entry R3-MEASURED, THE 30 DEGREE QUESTION (points b-f) established that the canonical "20-30"
   is NOT a measurement of the test's performance but a number carried over from the caloric convention —
   and that its declared goal ("raising the head puts the lateral canal vertical") is met EXACTLY at a
   flexion of HC_FLEX_DEG. The canon assumes a 30-degree inclination; the MEASUREMENTS give 19.9 / 17.4 /
   10.4. Since the engine computes on IE-Map geometry, the only angle consistent with its own anatomy is
   ~10 degrees. The user's decision, 2026-08-16.
 CONTROL OF THE DERIVATION: sides P and L both give 10.2955 (checked separately, not assumed from the
   mirror); the P/L mirror of the whole diagnostic path after the change is 0.0e+0 across three canals;
   the ROLL ACT oracle enforces a bit-for-bit P/L mirror for all three debris sizes.
 THE LIMIT OF THIS RATIONALE (named, because it weakens our own result): the |g|-in-plane optimum DEPENDS
   ON THE POSE. In the test pose (head turned 90, ear down) the maximum shifts to -1.77, so THERE every
   flexion costs: at 10.30 it leaves 0.978, at 30 it leaves 0.855. The clinical rationale optimises the
   SETUP pose (the one the instruction talks about: "lay the patient down, flex, then turn"), not the
   pose in which the test is read. Had the criterion been response strength in the testing position, the
   right flexion would be ~0. The ANATOMICAL angle was chosen because it realises the sentence with which
   the clinic justifies the number — not because it maximises the output. The maneuver note says so plainly.
 MEASURED CONSEQUENCES (protocol of the CALIBRATED CONSTANTS: latency at |xi| >= 0.05, duration =
   t(3% of peak) - latency, dt=0.05, INTERP prefix, measurement tHold 60/60/90 s — the very protocol that
   reproduces the old numbers digit for digit, so the comparison is honest):
   a) HORIZONTAL CANAL, diagnostic provocation: latency 2.30 -> 2.00 s, peak xi 0.69591 -> 0.78402
      @ 9.95 -> 9.25 s, duration 42.75 -> 39.35 s. The posterior and anterior canals are BITWISE
      untouched (0.87158 @10.25 and 0.94191 @24.95) — the change touches only the horizontal pose.
   b) R3-OLD PARTLY ANSWERED — not by tuning, but by the pose: the HC<PC latency gap grew from 1 grid
      step dt (2.30 vs 2.35) to 7 steps (2.00 vs 2.35), and the peak difference shrank from -20.2% to
      -10.1%. Part of the complaint "the constants were calibrated for the posterior canal only" was an
      artefact of the 30-degree over-flexion. Per-canal constant tuning remains DISCOURAGED (R3-OLD).
   c) NULL POINT: 8.7 -> 7.5 degrees toward the affected ear; the number is COMPUTED in four texts
      (nullTxt = the nullYawOf scan), not written in. The cupulo target when lying down +0.059 -> +0.057.
      The Roll apo asymmetry (raw mag) 0.832/0.885 -> 0.966/0.985. The CUP_WEAK and LIGHT_W calibration
      anchors were recomputed (0.45*0.966/0.784 = 0.554; light/geo 0.435/0.784 = 0.554; display light
      0.435 vs apo 0.196) — the POSITION IN THE 0.4-0.7 BAND IS UNCHANGED, so the justification of both
      constants still stands; only the numbers are new.
   d) LYING EQUILIBRIUM 190.97 -> 190.35; the resting drive when lying down 0.0223 -> 0.0254 (still below
      fStat 0.04, so the "the model does not resolve it" card is unchanged; four texts that quoted 0.026
      here — the BOW value, not the lying one — were corrected in passing). The afterRoll scenario's phi0
      221.40 -> 220.15. The textbook scenario's phiAfterLie 190.399 -> 189.924, i.e. it CROSSED the 190
      watershed — VERIFIED not to change any verdict (the LDT card's direction comes from phi0 = 146.19,
      and a bow chained after the LDT starts from 195.6/195.8 and beats TOWARD THE HEALTHY side either way).
   e) SHORT-ARM PHENOTYPE: rollAff plateau -0.375 -> -0.519, heal 0.459 -> 0.466 — signs and logic

      identical final phi and peaks. Lempert does not use this pose (the debris exits at t=71.55 s, the
      pose comes later); zuma uses it in step 4, but after the exit — only the instruction TEXT changes.
 PHENOMENON No. 1 — THE TEST BECOMES A TREATMENT. [THE USER'S CLINICAL RULING 2026-08-16: this is NOT an
   error. A positional test can act as a treatment and is to be NOTED, not fixed. What follows stands as a
   description of the phenomenon and its limits, not as a defect to remove. The only thing still open is
   whether the DISTRIBUTION (4/4 for medium/big) overstates the frequency — that is a property of the arc,
   not a measurement in patients.]
   THE FLEXION IS NOT WHAT DECIDES THAT (a correction after the
   review; the first version of this entry attributed the effect to the flexion and was MISLEADING).
   The measured size x hold matrix (neutral scenario, act affected->centre->healthy, end of phase 3 =
   27.4 + hold):
     SMALL debris: does not empty the canal at ANY flexion or hold (20 and 30 s, 10.30 and 30).
     MEDIUM:  hold 20 s -> empties at 10.30 (t=46.55, margin 0.85 s), NOT at 30;
              hold 30 s -> empties at BOTH (10.30: t=57.05; 30: t=61.65).
     LARGE:   ALWAYS empties, including at 30 with a 20 s hold (t=38.95, margin 8.45 s).
   Conclusion: the HOLD LENGTH and the debris SIZE decide; the flexion shifts the threshold by ~5 s of
   hold and only for the MEDIUM size. The sentence "the textbook 30 did not do that" is true ONLY for
   medium@20 s — for large debris it is always false, for small it is moot. ACT_STEPS.roll encodes a 20 s
   hold, i.e. SHORTER than the literature (typically >=30 s) — so it, not the angle, puts the act on the
   threshold. The phenomenon remains an UNMASKED R1(c) defect (the idealised arc), but ascribing a
   protective role to 30 degrees was an overreach and has been withdrawn.
   Effect on the card: the control test after the act falls mute (medium/large). DIAGNOSIS UNTOUCHED —
   both phases carry a full response, lateralisation correct in 3/4 scenarios as before the change.
 PHENOMENON No. 2 — ANTERIOR-CANAL LEAKAGE ONTO THE ROLL CARD (formerly "loss of canal selectivity") (the oracle critic's finding, VERIFIED, and the STRONGEST point
   against this change): in the exam cross matrix a patient with ANTERIOR-canal debris produces, in the
   Roll position, a response of 0.0127 at a 30-degree flexion (mute) and 0.1185 at 10.30 (VISIBLE — the
   XI_CARD threshold is 0.10). The crossing lies between 15 and 10.30 (15 -> 0.0701). So in THIS model's
   geometry 30 degrees does buy something, and it is measurable: it keeps the Roll test blind to
   anterior-canal debris. This does not invalidate the caloric pedigree of the number (30 was still not
   chosen after a sensitivity measurement), but it DOES invalidate the strong claim "30 buys nothing" —
   and that is how it must be read. Cross-canal leakage is not new in itself (HC in Dix 0.478, PC in Roll
   0.530 — pinned since V21); what is new is this single cell crossing the visibility threshold. A
   CLINICAL DECISION (the user's): whether to teach that the Roll test at the anatomical flexion can be
   positive in AC-BPPV, or to treat this as an argument for a 15-20 degree band.
   [SETTLED 2026-08-16 — THE ANATOMICAL ANGLE STAYS (the user's decision), and measuring the APPEARANCE of
    the nystagmus largely defuses the objection. In the Roll position, components in the patient frame:
      HC: h +/-1.000, v 0, t 0 — purely HORIZONTAL, DIRECTION-CHANGING between sides (0.784 affected ear
          down vs 0.297 healthy), i.e. a classic DCPN;
      AC: h 0, v -1.000, t 0 — pure DOWNBEAT, NO direction change, and practically UNILATERAL
          (0.1185 with the affected ear down vs 0.0003 with the healthy one).
    The axes are ORTHOGONAL, so the picture is not confusing: the clinician reads "positional downbeat",
    i.e. exactly the AC/central pathway, not a horizontal-canal DCPN. The card displays "nystagmus down
    (downbeat) (weak)" — verified. A NOTE ON CONVENTION: the AC's zero torsion is a CLINICAL MASK
    (NYS_SHOW.anterior.t = false, "pure downbeat"); this atlas's geometry gives a torsion of 0.739 relative
    to the vertical component. In a real patient under VOG the picture would therefore be downbeat plus
    torsion — even further from the purely horizontal HC. The HC torsion is hidden the same way (geom.
    0.182). The 15-20 degree band was REJECTED: the AC visibility threshold sits at 12.07 degrees, but the
    lower half of the band (up to 18) still fails to stop the canal from emptying, and the POSTERIOR canal's
    leakage onto the Roll card (0.53 at 10.30, still 0.40 at 30) is visible at EVERY angle — the
    "selectivity" never existed. Choosing a pose angle to obtain a desired diagnostic behaviour would be
    bending the pose to the result, the very error the 2026-08-05 pose derivation removed.]
 AN INTERNAL CONTRADICTION TO BE RESOLVED (geometry critic): two paths model the SAME examination and say
   different things — the history preset BLT_HISTORY.afterRoll ("after the Roll test, both sides") leaves
   the debris in the canal (phi0 220.15, exitedInHistory=false), while the session act ACT_STEPS.roll
   EMPTIES it (exitStep 2). Before V25 both said "it stays". The difference comes from the holds
   (BLT_HISTORY has its own) — a V26 candidate: derive the afterRoll history from actTimeline instead of
   from a separate step list.
 THE MANEUVER NOTE (DIAG.roll.poseNote — the user's decision: "note it in the maneuver's notes"): the only

   [SHORTENED 2026-08-16 at the user's request (V26a): the full version was too long for a clinical

    1618 -> 832 characters; rebaseline scope: 11 dom keys, the Roll card only. Do NOT restore the long
    version in the UI without an explicit request.]
   Rewritten after the review: it says where the number comes from, names the LIMIT (the figures belong to the setup pose; in
   the reading position the optimum is near 0), qualifies the cost by SIZE and HOLD instead of stating it
   unconditionally, and closes by saying this is a property of the model's arc, NOT clinical knowledge (in

 ORACLES — NEW (the oracle critic's blocking finding: nothing whatsoever guarded the change's main effect):
   * engine.rollact (6 keys: 2 sides x 3 sizes) — pins for exited/exitStep/tExit/phases plus FOUR hard
     throws: (1) a FRESH patient may never have a phase marked "gone", (2) the "affected ear down" phase
     must carry a response, (3) expulsion only in the last positional step (otherwise the comparison
     phase, and with it lateralisation, is lost), (4) a bit-for-bit P/L mirror.

 THE IN-PASSING FIX (the clinic critic's blocking finding): the session card took `pv.exited` — the state
   at the END of the act — and projected it back onto ALL phases. While the Roll act never emptied the
   canal this was harmless; after V25 the first test on an UNTREATED patient showed BOTH phases as "canal
   cleared — no response" captioned "This is what a control test right after successful repositioning
   looks like", i.e. the application taught that fresh HC-BPPV has a negative bilateral Roll test.
   sessionPreview gained a `gone` field (the debris was outside the canal ALREADY AT THE START of that
   phase — distinct from `exited` and from `phases[].exited`), the render switched to it, and the phase in
   WHICH the debris leaves got its own note: the response is full, but it is the LAST provocation of this
   debris. The same semantics as the SCEN_DRIVEN branch.
