=== REMOVING THE CLINICAL MASK anterior.t - THE ANTERIOR CANAL'S TORSION EXPRESSED (V26, 2026-08-16) ===
 WHAT: `NYS_SHOW.anterior.t` false -> true. The anterior canal stops being a "pure downbeat" in the
   engine: quickPhase now returns t = +0.7386*ipsi (upper poles toward the AFFECTED ear) alongside
   v = -1.000. The user's decision; it supersedes the "CONSIDERED AND REJECTED" entry of 2026-07-09.
 THE SIGN - CHECKED BEFORE UNMASKING, NOT AFTER. The mask hid not only a value but a sign, and a
   hand-written sign in this file has been WRONG before (A4 found exactly that in posterior.h). So the
   rule of sign was calibrated on the components that are EXPOSED and validated, and tested for whether
   it reproduces all of them:
     v = -sign(W_x)*m.v · h = +sign(W_y)*m.h · t = -sign(W_z)*m.t,  W = +n (Ewald II) / -n (Ewald III)
   The rule reproduces, TO THE SIGN: horizontal.h (P and L), posterior.v, posterior.t (P and L),
   anterior.v - i.e. everything currently visible and clinically validated. For anterior.t it predicts
   +0.7386*ipsi, EXACTLY what had been hand-written. The unmasking required no sign change.
   INDEPENDENT CLINICAL CONFIRMATION: [H32] Garaycochea 2022 - in AC-BPPV the upper pole of the eye
   beats toward the AFFECTED ear. Two routes, one answer.
 A TRAP FOUND IN PASSING (NOT fixed - the flag is dormant): the hand-written sign of `anterior.h` is
   +ipsi while the rule gives -ipsi - the same mismatch A4 detected in posterior.h. It is dead today
   (anterior.h=false); correct the sign before ever exposing that flag. Also noted: a FOURTH mask absent
   from the table - horizontal.v (geom. 0.03), zeroed by a literal `v:0` rather than by a flag.
 WHY THIS DOES NOT WEAKEN THE CLINICAL MESSAGE. The old cards said "AC lateralization by nystagmus is
   UNRELIABLE because it is a pure downbeat / the torsion is a trace". After unmasking that sentence
   would be HALF false (the model does have torsion), so it was rewritten so that the CONCLUSION stays
   while the premise changes: not "the model has no torsion" but "the PATIENT often has none".
     [H31] Castellucci 2020: purely vertical nystagmus, with no torsion at all, in 42.4% of positional
       downbeats and in 57.1% of confirmed AC-BPPV.
     [H32] Garaycochea 2022: the torsion is weaker than the vertical; the side is unambiguous in only
       about half of patients; a right-sided POSTERIOR cupulolithiasis (apo) looks IDENTICAL to a
       left-sided AC.
     [H33] Bertholon 2002: the torsion is weak and often clinically invisible.
   The advice "do not lateralize AC by the nystagmus" STAYS on all four cards, with a new justification.
 TEXTS CHANGED (7 sites, all of which asserted that there is no torsion): the head-hang canalo latNote ·
   the head-hang phase label · the head-hang phase note · the "Direction" row of the Barany
   classification · the recommend note for Yacovino · the antMode label and note on the Dix card · the
   nystagmus-label renderer in svg-screens (it cut the word "torsional" for the anterior canal ONLY -
   the branch is now shared by both vertical canals; the horizontal canal goes down a different branch,
   so its t=false mask is untouched).
 MEASURED CONSEQUENCES: AC quickPhase t 0.0000 -> +/-0.7386. The head-hang card: `dir` 0 -> -/+1 (the
   torsional direction stops being undefined). The SPV bridge, AC provocation: the torsional component
   0.0 -> 20.9 deg/s against a vertical 28.3 deg/s (ratio 0.739). The exam cross matrix: STRENGTHS
   UNCHANGED (0.1185 / 0.4745 / 0.9419 - the dominant axis stays vertical); only the `anat` vector
   changes. The POSTERIOR and HORIZONTAL canals are bit-for-bit.
 SCDS/TULLIO: the 2026-07-09 workaround (pressureStimulus reads nysMag, bypassing the mask) is now
   REDUNDANT - the global path yields the same 0.74 ratio. Left UNCHANGED: it works, it is validated,
   and removing it would be a change with no gain. engine.neuro is BIT-0 - verified.
 A COVERAGE GAP CLOSED IN PASSING: the whole antMode path ("downbeat in the Dix-Hallpike -> anterior
   canal of the OPPOSITE ear") had NOT A SINGLE screen in the golden - the nystagmus label and the
   lateralization note could have started lying without a byte of difference. Added
   `dom diag/dix/{P,L}/antMode` with hygiene (return to the posterior view and side P). The same class
   of gap as the Roll card under a session in V25.
 ORACLES / SCOPE: 13 changed / +2 added (antMode) / 0 removed; counts plans 18, neuro 19, pose 360,
   dom 189 -> 191. Changed: engine.spv/headhang P+L (the torsional component), engine.exam/cross,
   dom guide/yacovino/{P,L}/step1-3 (6 - the "+ torsional" label), dom diag/headhang P, L, P/cupulo,
   L/cupulo (4). BIT-0: engine.neuro (all of it), every posterior- and horizontal-canal key, pose,
   plans, sensitivity, expulsion, ensemble, GRACE, jam, rollact, the session. 3 oracles PASS.
 WHAT THIS DOES NOT SETTLE: whether 0.74 is the right VISIBILITY for the torsion. The geometry gives
   that ratio, but the literature says that in patients the torsion is weak or absent - so the model may
   OVERSTATE it as a visual signal. We do not damp it artificially (that would be reinstating the mask
   by another route); instead the cards state outright how much of it survives in a patient. A separate
   candidate: whether the AC torsion should be DRAWN more faintly than the posterior one at the same
   number - that is a question about RENDERING, not about geometry.

==========================================================================
 NeuroVOR MODULE — THE TONIC/CENTRAL LAYER (VOR · fixation · vHIT · HINTS)
 File: src/engine/neuro-vor.js  |  Module: export const NeuroVOR = (()=>{ ... })()
 Nature: a pure ES module WITHOUT DOM (offline-testable in node)
 Added: 2026-07-07  |  Extensions: central COMPENSATION (stage 6/6b/6c) 2026-07-08 ·
 NEUROANATOMY (stage 7: E1 per-canal · E2 plane vHIT · E3 3D vector · E4 pathologies) 2026-07-09 [in progress]
==========================================================================

GOAL AND PHILOSOPHY ("first-principles")
 We do not code the APPEARANCE of a pathology, but the PHYSIOLOGY: the resting activity of the labyrinths,
 Ewald's law with an inhibitory cut-off, fixation suppression (flocculus), canal VOR gain,
 the neural integrator, and the graviceptive axis (otoliths/MLF). The signs — spontaneous
 nystagmus, its dependence on fixation and gaze, the head impulse (vHIT) result,
 skew deviation — FOLLOW from the asymmetry of a few parameters. A layer INDEPENDENT of
 the BPPV dynamics (simulateCanalith); it shares only the direction conventions (quickPhase)
 and the frontal camera (mirror cam.right[0] = -1, like nysFromGeom/nysFromDyn).
 It realizes the todo item: "Other states (excited:false / push-pull): unilateral
 vestibulopathy, central nystagmus".

CONVENTIONS
 Sides: "P"=right=+x (right ear), "L"=left=-x. Afferent frequencies in Hz.
 Slow-phase velocities (SPV) and drift in °/s. Gaze angle gazeDeg: + = to the right (P).
 The "head-frame" sign h/t as in Vestibular; `dir`/`tdir` = the SCREEN sign after camera mirroring.

"PATIENT" — makePatient(overrides) -> a parameter object
   toneL, toneR   : resting labyrinth activity L/P [Hz], healthy = 90. Hypofunction lowers it. [H1]
   gainL, gainR   : horizontal-canal VOR gain L/P (0..~1.1), healthy = 1.0 — HIGH frequency (vHIT). [H4][H5]
   caloricGainL/R : LOW-frequency gain of HC L/P, healthy = 1.0 — CALORIC TEST (~0.003 Hz).      [H21]
                    A SEPARATE axis from `gain` (HF) → enables the caloric↔vHIT dissociation (stage 7/E4.5).
   fixationGain   : the flocculus's capacity for visual suppression (0..1); central → ~0.              [H3]
   integratorTau  : the neural integrator's time constant [s]; healthy ~25, central "leaky" ~1–3. [H6]
   skewTone       : vertical graviceptive asymmetry [°, signed]; + = eye P higher.          [H7]
                    SIGN vs AFFECTED SIDE: the ocular tilt reaction (OTR) in a PERIPHERAL loss
                    (and in a brainstem lesion BELOW the decussation, e.g. Wallenberg) is IPSIVERSIVE —
                    the eye on the AFFECTED side sits LOWER. A loss of ear P ⇒ skewTone NEGATIVE. A fix
                    from the neurophysiology review: previously nerveBranchLesion and the stroke preset
                    gave the opposite sign, so the engine pointed to the wrong side.
   otrTorsion     : OTR torsion [°] (upper poles toward the lower eye).                            [H7]
   sacculeL/R     : SACCULE function (0..1, healthy=1) → cVEMP; INFERIOR nerve.                       [H22]
   utricleL/R     : UTRICLE function (0..1, healthy=1) → oVEMP + co-forms SVV; SUPERIOR nerve.     [H22]
   comp           : level of central COMPENSATION c∈[0,1]; 0=acute phase, 1=full symmetry.     [H10][H11]
   pacemakerBias  : the persistent (sticky) pacemaker charge of the affected nucleus [Hz] → Bechterew's nystagmus. [H11]
   lesionEar      : the explicit affected side for compensation ("P"/"L"/null=auto from the weaker labyrinth).
   tauVS          : the VELOCITY STORAGE time constant [s] (decay after rotation); healthy ~15.     [H14]
   --- STAGE 7 (neuroanatomy): VERTICAL canals per side; healthy = like horizontal (tone R0, gain 1.0) ---
   toneAcL/R, gainAcL/R : ANTERIOR canal (anterior/"superior") L/P — SUPERIOR nerve.          [H15]
   tonePcL/R, gainPcL/R : POSTERIOR canal (posterior)          L/P — INFERIOR nerve.         [H15]
   dehiscence     : SCDS — dehiscence of the SUPERIOR canal: null | "L" | "P" (a mobile third window).   [H18]

PUBLIC API (returned from the module)

 afferent(tone, drive) -> Hz                                                                  [H1][H2]
   The afferent after RECTIFICATION: max(0, min(R_SAT, tone+drive)). Inhibitory cut-off (≥0) +
   saturation (≤R_SAT=400; until stage N3 of review II it was 300 — a healthy VOR was losing gain from as low as 262°/s). A shared primitive: rest (drive=0) and head impulse (drive≠0).

 spontaneous(p) -> {kind,h,v,t,dir,tdir,vdir,strength,spv,beatEar,lesionEar}                  [H1][H2]
   STAGE 1 — SPONTANEOUS nystagmus from tonic asymmetry (push-pull). The brain estimates head velocity
   from the difference of the horizontal-pair afferents: Ω_est ∝ (rateL − rateR). A tonic asymmetry mimics a steady
   rotation → the SLOW phase toward the weaker labyrinth, the FAST phase (the visible beat) toward the stronger one.
   spv = SPV_MAX·|toneL−toneR|/R0 (SPV_MAX=30°/s, R0=90). Horizontal-torsional nystagmus (tH = 0.5·h·asym).
   Direction FIXED regardless of gaze — a PERIPHERAL feature.
   STAGE 7 (E3): a VERTICAL-TORSIONAL component from the vertical-canal pairs (verticalBeat) is added to the horizontal —
   fields v/vdir, tV, t=tH+tV, spvV/strengthV; kind="verticalTorsional" when the vertical dominates. Vertical canals
   healthy → v=0, spvV=0 (the horizontal case unchanged). See verticalBeat (STAGE 7).
   TWO SCALE FIXES from the neurophysiology review:
     • SPV_MAX 12→30°/s. An acute UVH in darkness beats at ~20–40°/s (neuritis, the first days), so the old
       model CEILING (12°/s with a completely silent labyrinth) lay BELOW the real range. The effect was
       visible on screen: with fixationGain 0.9 an acute neuritis gave 1.1°/s in the light, i.e. less
       than the module's own VIS_THRESH=2 threshold — the pathology came out "invisible" at the bedside. Now 2.8°/s.
     • tH weighted by intensity (·asym). The VERTICAL components carry intensity (the pair imbalance /R0),
       while the horizontal one carried a unit sign — so the sum t=tH+tV mixed direction with magnitude.
       The effect: a trace horizontal nystagmus (asym≈0.01) contributed the FULL 0.5 and could REVERSE the
       resultant torsion direction (tdir, i.e. what the renderer draws) of a strong vertical-torsional nystagmus.
   `kind` is decided by comparing the VISIBLE velocities (spvV vs spv), not |v| vs |h| — h is always
   ±1, so 0.1°/s of horizontal used to "win" over 12°/s of vertical.
   NOTE ON THE NAME `lesionEar`: it is the ear with the LOWER nucleus activity — it coincides with the affected
   ear only in a loss. In an IRRITATIVE lesion (irritative Ménière) the affected ear is MORE ACTIVE, so the
   actually affected side is carried by `trueLesionEar` (from compensate ← p.lesionEar, set e.g. by meniere()).

 suppressionFactor(p, fixOn) -> [0..~1.5]                                                       [H3]
 observe(p, fixOn) -> spontaneous(p) with suppression applied {spv,spvRaw,suppressionFactor,suppressed,...}
   STAGE 2 — FIXATION SUPPRESSION. eye = VOR − fixationGain·retinal_slip. fixOn=true (light):
   peripheral (fixationGain≈0.9) fades (×0.1); central (≈0) persists; <0 = paradoxical enhancement.
   fixOn=false (Frenzel/darkness): the full slow phase. `suppressed` = clinically "suppresses with fixation".

 headImpulse(p, toSide, {headVel=200, headAmp=15}) -> {gain,deficit,saccadePresent,saccadeAmp,
                                                        saccadeToSide,inhibitedFloored,abnormal,...}  [H4][H5]
   STAGE 3 — head impulse (vHIT). At the impulse velocity the INHIBITED canal hits the inhibitory cut-off
   (0 Hz, `inhibitedFloored`) → the VOR velocity is carried SOLELY by the EXCITED canal
   (ipsilateral). gain = the excited modulation / the ideal one. deficit → a corrective SACCADE (overt when
   ≥2.5° ≈ gain<0.83), directed opposite to the impulse (`saccadeToSide`). CENTRAL: gains≈1 →
   a normal HIT (ominous). Calibration S_HZ=0.8 → cut-off ~tone/S≈112°/s < the vHIT velocity.
   The afferent's DYNAMIC RANGE (a review fix): the excitatory modulation is bounded by a PROPERTY of the
   afferent, MOD_MAX=R_SAT−R0=310 Hz (after N3; helper excited(), shared with fusionWeights), and is not computed as
   rate−tone. Previously a high resting tone by itself ate up the headroom to the ceiling: a tone ≥167 Hz with gain=1.0
   gave a PATHOLOGICAL vHIT with no gain loss whatsoever — and a tone up to 200 Hz is reachable with the slider in
   the Laboratory. That contradicted the module's own thesis: in the IRRITATIVE phase of Ménière's the gain is
   preserved, so the vHIT stays NORMAL, and that is the DIFFERENTIATING feature of this entity [H20] (the
    caloric↔vHIT dissociation itself is carried by [H21] — the Ménière criteria are symptom-based and say
    nothing about the vHIT). Saturation still
   acts where it makes physiological sense — at a very high head velocity (Ω>387°/s for gain 1.0 — outside the vHIT protocol; after N3, it was 262).
   STAGE 7 (E2): the second argument is POLYMORPHIC — 'P'/'L' (horizontal, as above, numerically unchanged) OR {canal,ear}
   (any of the 6 canals; planes HC/RALP/LARP). It also returns canal/ear/plane/coplanar/saccade{h,v,t}. See vhitPlane.

 gazeEvoked(p, gazeDeg) -> {tau,spv,beatSign,driftVel,present}                                  [H6]
 nystagmusAtGaze(p, gazeDeg, fixOn) -> the resultant nystagmus {beatHead,spv,dir,strength,components}
 directionChanging(p, fixOn, ecc=20) -> bool
   STAGE 4a — a "leaky" integrator: at gaze θ the drift toward center = θ/τ (slow phase), the fast phase
   toward the gaze. Healthy τ≈25 (negligible drift); central τ≈1–3 → a DIRECTION-CHANGING nystagmus.
   nystagmusAtGaze sums the beat velocities: SPONTANEOUS (peripheral, fixed) + GAZE-EVOKED (central, changing).
   directionChanging requires visibility ≥VIS_THRESH(2°/s) on BOTH sides and opposite directions
   (a strong spontaneous nystagmus can dominate the drift → it stays unidirectional — realistic).

 skew(p) -> {present,central,skewDeg,sign,hyperSide,torsionDeg}                                  [H7]
   STAGE 4b — skew deviation / OTR from graviceptive asymmetry (otoliths + MLF). present when |skewTone|≥1°.
   central when |skewTone|≥SKEW_CENTRAL(2.5°) — only a SIGNIFICANT skew counts as a CENTRAL feature (INFARCT);
   a small skew (e.g. the utricle of the superior nerve) present, but NOT central (avoids a false "stroke" — a review fix).
   Alternate-cover test: the uncovered eye catches fixation with a VERTICAL SACCADE = positive cover test = central.
 svv(p) -> {tiltSide, deg, abnormal, peripheralDeg, centralDeg, central}                          [H7][H22]
   STAGE 7 — SUBJECTIVE VISUAL VERTICAL (SVV). The graviceptive tilt computed from TWO tracks of the same axis:
     • PERIPHERAL — the utricle + the VERTICAL canals AC/PC; the vertical tilts TOWARD THE AFFECTED SIDE;
     • CENTRAL — skewTone (the otolith-brainstem/MLF pathway, the same one that gives skew and torsion), SVV_SKEW=1.6°/1°.
   `central` says WHICH track dominates — on that basis clinicalReadout attributes the finding as peripheral or
   as central, instead of (as before) always as peripheral. A review fix: svv() read the peripheral track
   only, so a patient with an overt OTR (skew 3°, torsion 4°) had SVV=0.0° = "normal" — whereas a skew without
   an SVV tilt clinically practically does not occur (SVV is the MOST SENSITIVE component of the OTR triad).
   MORE SENSITIVE than skew deviation: an INFERIOR neuritis gives a tilt DESPITE a normal skew (skew from the utricle=0).
   Symmetry (BVH)→0. abnormal when ≥2°. Does NOT affect the HINTS verdict (a supplementary readout).
 vemp(p) -> {cVEMP:{L,R,ampL,ampR,asym,weakEar,strongEar,bilateralWeak,organ,nerve}, oVEMP:{...}}  [H18][H22]
   STAGE 7 — VEMP: cVEMP(cervical)≈SACCULE→INFERIOR nerve (ipsi); oVEMP(ocular)≈UTRICLE→SUPERIOR nerve. Amplitude from the
   organ's function (sacculeL/R, utricleL/R) × the THIRD WINDOW enhancement (dehiscence ⇒ ×VEMP_SCDS=2.0 ipsilaterally).
   Status: ≥1.35 enhanced / ≥0.65 normal / ≥0.3 reduced / <0.3 absent. It separates the branches: INFERIOR
   neuritis→cVEMP↓+oVEMP normal; SUPERIOR→the opposite. Two review fixes:
     • bilateralWeak — a BILATERAL absence (BVH/ototoxicity) gives AR%≈0, so the mere lack of asymmetry read
       as "normal"; the same masking pitfall that the caloric handles with the bilateralWeak flag;
     • strongEar / the "enhanced" status — SCDS clinically INCREASES the amplitude (a large oVEMP n10, a low cVEMP
       threshold), and the old amplitude ceiling of 1.0 made that structurally impossible: a modelled dehiscence showed
       a "normal VEMP", i.e. the inverse of the diagnostic SCDS pair. weakEar names an ear only when its
       amplitude is ACTUALLY < 0.65 (otherwise an enhancement on one side would "reduce" the healthy other one).

 SCENARIOS, scenario(key) -> patient                                                            [H8]
   Presets: normal · neuritisR/neuritisL (PERIPHERAL: tone↓ + gain↓ of the affected canal, flocculus intact,
   integrator normal, no skew) · strokeCentral (CENTRAL: canals INTACT gain≈1 → a normal HIT,
   mild asymmetric tone, a "leaky" integrator, skew+torsion; skewTone=−3 = an ipsiversive OTR for a lesion
   on the P side, i.e. the medullary/Wallenberg variant — the commonest vascular cause of AVS). KEY: the STATIC tone (nystagmus) and the
   DYNAMIC gain (HIT) are SEPARATE parameters — that is why a stroke gives a nystagmus with a normal HIT.

 hints(p) -> {verdict, hi, vhitVertical, ny, ts, infarct, centralSigns}                          [H8][H22]
   STAGE 5 — HINTS synthesis. verdict: "normal" | "peripheral" (reassuring) | "central" (ominous).
   Central features (INFARCT): Impulse Normal (a normal HIT DESPITE a spontaneous nystagmus) ·
   Fast-phase Alternating (a direction-changing nystagmus) · Refixation on Cover Test (a positive skew) ·
   isolatedVertical (an isolated vertical-torsional nystagmus with a NORMAL vHIT of the vertical canals).
   The presence of any → "central". The reassuring triad (pathological HIT + unidirectional, fixation-suppressed
   + no skew) → "peripheral".
   TWO NYSTAGMUS AXES (a review fix). ny.horizontal / ny.vertical separate the horizontal component
   from the vertical-torsional one; ny.hasSpontaneous = the sum. Previously ONLY the horizontal counted, so a patient
   with a 9–12°/s vertical-torsional nystagmus (INFERIOR-nerve neuritis [H22]) got the verdict "normal" —
   and that ALONGSIDE the centralSigns list from clinicalReadout, which reported the same nystagmus as a red flag.
   The verdict and the sign list contradicted each other on one screen.
   The criteria are PER AXIS, because the HIT tests the HORIZONTAL canals:
     • Impulse Normal ← a HORIZONTAL nystagmus with a normal horizontal HIT;
     • isolatedVertical ← a VERTICAL nystagmus with a normal RALP/LARP vHIT (vhitVertical) — the exact
       vertical counterpart of the same reasoning. The "normal vertical vHIT" condition is NECESSARY:
       the same nystagmus WITH a vertical-canal loss is INFERIOR-nerve neuritis, i.e. PERIPHERAL.
   vhitVertical {RALP, LARP, abnormal} computed ONCE and reused by clinicalReadout (a single source).

 --- STAGE 6: CENTRAL COMPENSATION (brainstem/cerebellar neuroplasticity) — added 2026-07-08 ---

 compensate(p) -> {vnL, vnR, c, lesionEar, clampAmt, paceAmt, pacemakerBias}                    [H10][H11]
   STAGE 6 — STATIC compensation. The activity of the vestibular NUCLEI (VN) after neuroplasticity — it is this
   (not the raw labyrinth) that push-pull "sees" (spontaneous). Two-track: a CEREBELLAR CLAMP lowers the healthy
   nucleus (early, contribution exp(−c/CLAMP_TAU)) + an intrinsic PACEMAKER raises the affected nucleus (late, persistent).
   clamp+pacemaker = c·gap (restored) → symmetry at c=1 WITHOUT overshoot. c=0 and bias=0 → an exact
   NO-OP (vnL/vnR = afferent(tone,0)) — consistent with stages 1–5.
   STAGE 6b — pacemakerBias (sticky, [Hz]): a latched pacemaker charge INDEPENDENT of the current tone.
   When the labyrinth recovers function, the affected nucleus (tone returned + charged bias) becomes STRONGER →
   a RECOVERY nystagmus (Bechterew's) beating toward the EARLIER affected ear. The scalar c carries no history —
   which is why the charge is a separate, persistent parameter (flag spontaneous().bechterew).

 fusionWeights(p, headVel, toSide) -> {wCanal, wNeck, canalRel, neckRel}                        [H12][H13][H14]
   STAGE 6c — RELIABILITY-WEIGHTED FUSION (precision-weighted / complementary filter; an optimal estimator).
   The canal carries a velocity estimate with a weight = reliability (gain × the margin to saturation). At high velocity
   the canal loses reliability (saturation/inhibitory cut-off) → the estimator SWITCHES to neck
   proprioception/efference: neckRel = c·(1−canalRel). The "switch" FOLLOWS from the weights, not from a script. fw describes
   the MECHANISM (when/why the system switches to prediction).

 postRotational(p) -> {tauBase, tau, c, shortened}                                              [H10][H14]
   STAGE 6c — VELOCITY STORAGE. tau = tauVS·(1 − DVS_FRAC·c): compensation "opens the valve" of the velocity
   integrator → decay after rotation 15 s → ~5 s. A SEPARATE system from the gaze-holding integrator
   (integratorTau, stage 4a): compensation shortens velocity storage, but NOT gaze-holding (otherwise a false central).

 headImpulse — EXTENDED with DYNAMIC compensation: {covertAmp, overtAmp, covertFrac, covertSaccade,
   overtSaccade, fusion}. The total saccade = deficit·amp (the gain is IRREPARABLE), but it shifts from OVERT
   (overt, after the impulse, slip) to COVERT (covert, during the movement, prediction from fusion); the covert share = c
   (completeness of compensation): c=1 → the saccade is entirely COVERT (a bedside HIT "normal"). fusionWeights = the mechanism.
   abnormal = an INSTRUMENTAL deficit (independent of compensation); saccadePresent/overtSaccade = bedside
   overtness (vanishes with compensation). [H5][H12][H13]

 --- STAGE 7: NEUROANATOMY (added 2026-07-09) — per-canal (E1) · plane vHIT (E2) · 3D vector (E3) ·
     pathologies (E4: SCDS ✓, BVH ✓, Ménière ✓) · CALORIC test (E4.5 ✓) · clinicalReadout synthesis (E5 ✓).
     Generalizes the horizontal model → 6 CANALS. ENGINE COMPLETE — the UI phase remains (U1–U8).
     BACKWARD COMPATIBLE: vertical canals default to healthy → stages 1–8 numerically unchanged. Reuses Vestibular
     geometry (quickPhase/nysMag/CANAL_NORMALS). E5 will add PARAM_SPEC + clinicalReadout + hardening. ---

 CANAL_PARAM, NERVE_CANALS — maps: canal→param names (toneXx/gainXx); nerve→canals: superior={horizontal,
     anterior,utricle}, inferior={posterior,saccule}. Neuritis usually of the SUPERIOR nerve (longer bony canal). [H15]

 nerveBranchLesion(ear, branch, sev) -> overrides for makePatient                                 [H15]
   E1 — dropout of a nerve BRANCH (branch "superior"/"inferior", sev 0..1): tone→R0·(1−sev), gain→1−0.9·sev (N7)
   for the branch's canals; superior adds a SMALL skewTone (1.5·sev, utricle — < the central threshold) + caloricGain↓ (1−sev;
   HC=superior nerve) + utricle↓ (oVEMP); inferior adds saccule↓ (cVEMP) — does NOT touch the caloric (HC spared).
   SUPERIOR → nystagmus + pathological vHIT of the horizontal AND anterior + caloric↓ + oVEMP↓ + SVV↑, POSTERIOR/cVEMP normal;
   INFERIOR → only POSTERIOR (torsional downbeat) + cVEMP↓ + a small SVV tilt, HC/caloric/oVEMP/skew-deviation normal. [H22]

 bilateralLoss(sev) -> overrides for makePatient                                                  [H19][H23]
   E4 — BVH/ototoxicity: a SYMMETRIC drop of tone+gain of all 6 canals + caloricGain↓ bilaterally. EMERGENTLY: symmetry → NO
   spontaneous nystagmus (distinguishes it from unilateral UVH!), yet a pathological vHIT BILATERALLY (all
   planes); verdict peripheral (no false stroke). Compensation idle. Preset SCENARIOS.bvh.
   BVH THRESHOLDS (Bárány criteria [H19], confirmed [H23]): vHIT bilateral HC gain < 0.6 (SHIMP < 0.5) · caloric
   sum of warm+cold for a GIVEN ear < 6 °/s (= CAL_BILAT) · rotatory-chair gain ≤ 0.1 with phase lead ≥ 15° and
   time constant ≤ 6 s (NOT modelled; post-erratum 2023 numbers for the Bárány paper — full erratum note in the
   bibliography entry; until 2026-08-22 this read the pre-erratum "gain < 0.1 with phase lead > 68°", i.e. the 2017
   wording, which the 2022 paper also cites). IN THE ENGINE: bilateralLoss(1) → gain 0.35 (< 0.6/0.5) bilaterally; "pathological vHIT" is NOT a
   direct gain cutoff but a corrective-saccade amplitude ≥ 2.5° in headImpulse (for amp 15° ≈ gain ≤ 0.83 — more
   sensitive than 0.6, appropriate for a SINGLE canal). Only the caloric 6 °/s threshold is encoded numerically
   (CAL_BILAT → caloricBattery.bilateralWeak). SHIMP implemented (N4/N7). NOTE: the random preset bilateralLoss(r(0.6,1))
   yields gain 0.35–0.61 — the upper end (0.61) is SLIGHTLY above the Bárány 0.6 cutoff (meets BVH by caloric, not vHIT).

 meniere(ear, {phase | tone, gain}) -> overrides for makePatient                                  [H20]
   E4 — MÉNIÈRE / hydrops: PAROXYSMAL fluctuations of the affected ear's horizontal tone + a CHRONIC caloricGain↓ (opts.caloricLoss
   default 0.55) with the HF gain (vHIT) preserved = caloric/vHIT DISSOCIATION. Phases: irritative (tone 150, gain 1 →
   nystagmus TOWARD the affected ear, vHIT ~normal) · nullpoint (90 → none) · paretic (0, gain 0.5 → TOWARD the healthy ear, vHIT
   weakened) · interictal (90 → normal). A raw `tone` can be passed (smooth sweep). THE DIRECTION REVERSES
   ITSELF via push-pull (150→90→0 = P→none→L for ear P). PITFALL: irritation = nystagmus + a normal HIT →
   HINTS (for a CONTINUOUS AVS) reads "Impulse-Normal" = apparently CENTRAL; Ménière is PAROXYSMAL → HINTS does not apply
   (clinicalReadout E5 flags it). NOT added to SCENARIOS (a misleading verdict without context — for U6 with a caveat).

 qpFull(canal, ear) -> {h, v, t}                                                                 [E5-fix]
   The FULL (UNmasked) fast-phase axis of the EXCITED canal, signed (ipsi = ear==="P"?+1:−1):
   horizontal {ipsi·h, 0, 0}; anterior {0, −v, ipsi·t} (downbeat + anterior-canal torsion, which quickPhase
   MASKS for BPPV); posterior {0, +v, ipsi·t} (upbeat + torsion). Magnitudes from Vestibular.nysMag(). The PUBLIC
   source of the full geometry (vHIT saccade / verticalBeat / pressureStimulus have a consistent anterior torsion).
 canalPlane(canal,ear) -> "HC"|"RALP"|"LARP"                                                     [H16][H17]
 vhitPlane(p, plane) -> {plane, tests[2], abnormal, abnormalCanals}
   E2 — COPLANAR pairs: HC-P↔HC-L; RALP = anterior P ∥ posterior L; LARP = anterior L ∥ posterior P (RA∥LP,
   LA∥RP). vhitPlane fires both excitatory impulses of the plane. A corrective saccade per canal (headImpulse
   .saccade{h,v,t} from NeuroVOR.qpFull — the full geometry): horizontal toward the midline, anterior UP + torsion, posterior DOWN + torsion.

 verticalBeat(p) -> {v, t, mag, spvV, present}                                                   [H15][H16]
   E3 — the VERTICAL-TORSIONAL component of the spontaneous nystagmus from the vertical-canal PAIRS. imb = (ANTERIOR −
   POSTERIOR activity)/R0 per plane (LARP, RALP): the anterior more active → downbeat + torsion (LARP toward L, RALP toward P);
   the posterior → upbeat + reversed torsion. PER-PAIR summation guarantees CANCELLATION under symmetry (BVH → vector 0)
   despite different AC/PC magnitudes. Compensation shortens it the same as the horizontal (scale 1−c). Merged in spontaneous.
   (TORS_V=0.8 — a simplified torsion contribution; real geometry ~0.78–1.0.)

 pressureStimulus(p, {type, intensity, sign}) -> {present, ear, kind, h, v, t, dir, tdir, vdir, spv, strength}  [H18]
   STAGE 7 (E4) — SCDS / the TULLIO phenomenon. Dehiscence of the SUPERIOR canal (p.dehiscence = "L"/"P") = a MOBILE
   THIRD WINDOW: sound/pressure (type: sound|valsalva|hennebert|pressure) deflects the superior canal's cupula
   WITHOUT head movement → a VERTICAL-TORSIONAL nystagmus. sign: + EXCITATION (default per PRESSURE_EXC; downbeat +
   torsion TOWARD the affected ear) / − INHIBITION (suction; reversal to upbeat). intensity∈[0,1] scales the slow
   phase (spv = SPV_MAX·intensity), the direction vector normalized. AT REST SILENCE: dehiscence ≠
   hypofunction (tones/gains normal → no spontaneous nystagmus, a NORMAL canal vHIT) — the sign appears
   ONLY on stimulus. This distinguishes SCDS from neuritis (there a tonic/gain loss).
   DESIGN DECISION (anterior-canal torsion vs the NYS_SHOW mask): the anterior torsion is the
   DISTINGUISHING SIGN of SCDS, but quickPhase masks it to 0 (the "pure downbeat" convention for BPPV). So as NOT to
   violate the global mask (BPPV rendering/test regression), the Tullio vector is built from the FULL
   geometry Vestibular.nysMag (exported for this purpose), with a LOCAL bypass of the mask. The mask remains
   globally in force. (details and alternatives → the "CLINICAL MASK" section at the top of the file).

 caloric(p, {ear, temp}) -> {ear, temp, spv, beatEar, dir, fixationIndex, failsSuppression, caloricGain, Vcal, Vsp}  [H21]
   STAGE 7 (E4.5) — a single irrigation. The caloric = a LOW-frequency (~0.003 Hz) HC test, the other end
   of the characteristic relative to vHIT (~5 Hz); it scales with a SEPARATE `caloricGain` (not the HF `gain`) → that gives the DISSOCIATION.
   The stimulus = a thermal drive on the HC of the irrigated ear: WARM excites (beats TOWARD it), COLD inhibits (COWS).
   The spontaneous nystagmus (darkness) SUPERIMPOSES (→ DP). The fixation index = suppression by fixation (flocculus → central).

 caloricBattery(p) -> {RW,RC,LW,LC, rightSum,leftSum,total, CP,CPcorrected,DP, weakEar,dpSide,
                       bilateralWeak, fixationIndex,failsSuppression, vHITabnormal, caloricWeak,
                       dissociation, reverseDissociation}                                                 [H21]
   STAGE 7 (E4.5) — the bithermal battery (4 irrigations) + the Jongkees formula. CP% = canal paresis (LOCALIZES
   the weaker ear; <0 = right), DP% = directional preponderance (from the superimposed spontaneous nystagmus). CP/DP are
   RATIOS (independent of the CAL_NORMAL scale); `total`/`bilateralWeak` = absolute values. EMERGENTS:
   caloric↔vHIT dissociation (Ménière: caloricGain↓, HF gain normal) · reverseDissociation (vHIT+ / caloric− =
   a HIGH-frequency loss) · the caloric REVEALS a compensated
   loss (CP persists once the nystagmus is gone — caloricGain is peripheral, independent of `comp`) · BVH: bilateralWeak
   with CP≈0 (the ratio PITFALL) · INFERIOR nerve: caloric normal (HC=superior nerve) · stroke: caloric normal
   but failsSuppression (flocculus). CPcorrected (E5): CP from the CALORIC component |Vcal| ALONE — robust to
   MASKING by an acute spontaneous nystagmus (raw CP underestimates, e.g. −25% for a full UVH); weakEar/caloricWeak
   computed from the corrected one. Raw CP retained to show the masking effect.

 PARAM_SPEC -> [ {group, tier:"basic"|"advanced", help, params:[{key,label,min,max,step,unit,def} | {key,label,type:"select",options,def}]} ]  [E5]
   STAGE 7 (E5) — metadata of ALL patient parameters for the "mathematical patient" UI (sliders + selectors).
   7 groups: HC + caloric = "basic" (visible); AC/PC/central/compensation/SCDS = "advanced" (collapsible). Covers
   the full set of makePatient keys. Contains no logic — a pure table to generate the panel (UI phase/U2).

 clinicalReadout(p) -> {verdict, localization, findings[], peripheralSigns[], centralSigns[], ambiguities[],
                        hints, caloric, vhit, spontaneous, skew, scds}                                    [H8][H21]
   STAGE 7 (E5) — SYNTHESIS: the full clinical readout from the parameters. Reuses hints/caloricBattery/vhitPlane/observe/
   skew/pressureStimulus. `findings` = PL sentences; `localization` from the vHIT+caloric pattern (superior/inferior nerve/full/
   BVH/hydrops/central); `ambiguities` = PITFALLS: dissociation→paroxysmal Ménière (HINTS n/a), irritation misleadingly
   "central", BVH (HINTS assumes a unilateral AVS), INFERIOR neuritis (caloric blind). An EDUCATIONAL tool.
   SUPERIOR nerve LOCALIZATION (fixed 2026-07-10, revealed by the UI): the superior nerve of ear E = horizontal canal-E +
   anterior-E; anterior-P lies in the RALP plane, anterior-L in LARP (posterior-E = the inferior nerve — spared). Hence
   the "superior nerve" pattern = a pathological HC + ONE oblique (from the affected side: supObl) pathological, the other (othObl)
   normal — and not "both obliques normal" (that would only be possible with an isolated HC). A separate "horizontal
   canal" branch when both obliques are normal (isolated HC). Without this branch the most common superior neuritis fell into
   "undetermined".

EXPORTED CONSTANTS (in the public NeuroVOR object — also used by the UI, e.g. R0, VIS_THRESH): R0=90
   (rest), R_SAT=400 (saturation; N3 — it was 300, and a healthy subject read "abnormal" from 350°/s), SPV_MAX=30 (°/s at full asymmetry — an acute UVH in darkness),
   VIS_THRESH=2 (°/s — the nystagmus visibility threshold), CLAMP_TAU=0.32 (distribution of the clamp contribution in c),
   DVS_FRAC=0.67 (max. shortening of velocity storage).
INTERNAL CONSTANTS (non-exported): TORS_FRAC=0.5 (torsion share of the horizontal-torsional), MOD_MAX=310 (the
   afferent's dynamic range = R_SAT−R0; after N3), S_HZ=0.8 (afferent sensitivity Hz/°·s⁻¹), CAL_NORMAL=20 (°/s irrigation
   norm), CAL_BILAT=6 (°/s bilateral/ear threshold), CP_THRESH=25%, DP_THRESH=30%, SKEW_CENTRAL=2.5
   (° — the skew threshold for the CENTRAL flag; a small skew < the threshold = peripheral, e.g. the utricle of the superior nerve),
   SVV_SKEW=1.6 (° of SVV tilt per 1° of skew — the CENTRAL track of the graviceptive axis), VEMP_HIGH=1.35
   (the "enhanced" status threshold), VEMP_SCDS=2.0 (the VEMP enhancement by a third window).
A NOTE ON SCALE: SPV_MAX=30 > CAL_NORMAL=20, so in an ACUTE loss the spontaneous nystagmus exceeds every
   individual caloric response and the RAW CP index falls to ~0 (full masking). This is consistent with the
   clinic and exactly the reason CPcorrected exists — and it is CPcorrected, not CP, that feeds
   weakEar/caloricWeak/dissociation. The localizing indices therefore remain robust.

       the CONTAM constant) — the 0.6 here is kept deliberately, as a trace of the stage, not as a
       description of the current state.

       single parameter (a didactic gem); p.pursuitGain (null=derived) for explicit dissociations. saccadic when

       permanent): BVH 4 lines + OSCILLOPSIA (the defining complaint [H19]), a compensated UVH 0.195 (borderline),
       healthy 0. The third demonstration of the permanence of the loss (next to the caloric and vHIT) — as the
