--------------------------------------------------------------------------
 BIBLIOGRAPHY — NeuroVOR MODULE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The layer is a REDUCED, phenomenological implementation of established models of the neurophysiology
of the vestibulo-ocular reflex and peripheral/central differentiation — with no new scientific hypotheses.

CITATION CONVENTION (unified 2026-08-16, guarded by npm run zrodla:check): a number may stand alone,
but if a source designation accompanies it, the designation goes DIRECTLY AFTER the number and carries
a YEAR. The bracket closes on the number — sources without a number are added AFTER it, past a
semicolon. The gate covers src plus engine_doc and view_doc in both languages; details and declared
limits → view_doc.

=== HSN: THE VELOCITY THRESHOLD AND BIPHASIC BEHAVIOUR (V26, 2026-08-20) — [H40] Katsarkas 2000 ===
Katsarkas makes two claims that stage N4 did not reproduce. Both are now in hsn().

(b) HEAD-SHAKING VELOCITY THRESHOLD — HSN_VEL_MIN=180 deg/s, HSN_VEL_DEF=150 deg/s.
  Katsarkas (n=51, whole-body sinusoidal stimulation at 1/6 Hz, 90-220 deg/s): a bias arises at EVERY
  velocity, but its DIRECTION wanders — now toward the healthy ear, now toward the affected one — until
  shaking exceeds ~180 deg/s; only above the threshold does the slow phase lie toward the AFFECTED side
  in every patient with a bias >5 deg/s. Hence the clinical lability of the first phase's direction:
  a clinician rarely reaches 180 deg/s.
  IN THE ENGINE: new fields `velMin` and `directionReliable`; below the threshold the card states that
  the direction CANNOT BE TRUSTED, and the peripheral sign drops from "HSN toward the healthy side" to
  "HSN present — do not read the direction at this shaking velocity". The default of 150 deg/s stays
  DELIBERATELY below the threshold: that is what a typical bedside examination looks like, so the
  warning is meant to be visible by default. Velocity is a PROTOCOL, not a patient trait → the second
  argument clinicalReadout(p,{hsnPeakVel}), not a makePatient key.
  DECLARED LIMIT (measured with a probe, not assumed): `asym` is INDEPENDENT of velocity in this model —
  mod = gain·S_HZ·Ω on both sides, so Ω cancels in the ratio, and the MOD_MAX=310 saturation would
  require Ω>387 deg/s, i.e. outside the protocol (measured: spv0 identical at 90 and 220 deg/s).
  The threshold is therefore a RELIABILITY layer above the physics, not a change to the physics. The
  engine is deterministic, so it cannot show the wandering itself — and does not pretend to.

(c) BIPHASIC BEHAVIOUR FROM THE TIME-CONSTANT PAIR — HSN_PH2_K=0.35, function hsnPhase2().
  Katsarkas: the monophasic or biphasic course of HSN reflects an interaction of two time constants —
  "velocity storage" and "gaze holding". Both were ALREADY in the engine (tauVS=15 s, integratorTau=25 s),
  but hsn() used only the first. Phase 2 is now DERIVED from that pair:
      v(t) = spv0·e^(−t/τ_vs) − K·spv0·(e^(−t/τ_gh) − e^(−t/τ_vs))
  The bracket vanishes at t=0 → the PHASE-1 PEAK stays spv0 BIT FOR BIT (scope proof: the fields
  spv0/asym/tau/beatEar/dir/present/charge/cycles/peakVel are identical across all 19 golden patients).
  Closed forms: t_cross = ln(1+1/K)/(1/τ_vs−1/τ_gh); t_peak = ln((1+K)τ_gh/(K·τ_vs))/(1/τ_vs−1/τ_gh).
  A REVERSAL exists only when τ_gh > τ_vs. EMERGENT: it becomes VISIBLE (≥VIS_THRESH=2 deg/s) only once
  compensation has shortened velocity storage — an acute UVH (τ_vs 15 vs τ_gh 25) gives a phase-2 peak of
  0.14 deg/s = invisible → MONOphasic, while the same patient compensated (τ_vs 4.95) gives 2.13 deg/s →
  BIPHASIC. In the golden battery the only biphasic patient is `timeline/neuritisR/d21rec` (recovery,
  τ_vs 5.25 s → phase 2 of 2.01 deg/s, reversal after 9.0 s, peak at 19.4 s).
  With a LEAKY integrator (central, τ_gh < τ_vs) the component carries the SIGN OF PHASE 1 — there is no
  reversal and phase 1 simply decays more slowly.
  STATUS: this is a MODEL PREDICTION. Katsarkas gives the MECHANISM (two time constants), not these
  visibility thresholds nor the value of K — HSN_PH2_K=0.35 is a calibration of this engine, chosen so
  that phase 2 lands in the clinically described band (weaker and longer than phase 1), not a number
  taken from the paper.

ORACLE: `npm run hsn:check` (tools/hsn-check.mjs, 55 cases) — a NEW gate, because the golden snapshot
  pins hsn() ONLY at the default 150 deg/s (the harness calls `NV.hsn(p)` with no options), so neither
  the threshold (179 vs 180 deg/s) nor the mono↔biphasic switch was guarded by anything: deleting
  `directionReliable` or inverting the τ_gh>τ_vs condition would have left all 27 gates green. The gate
  checks both edges of the threshold, recomputes t_cross and t_peak FROM the definitions, forces the
  emergent (the reversal grows with compensation) and guards the PL/EN split in the card sentence.
GOLDEN AUDIT (scope proof, not an impression): 216 changed leaves = 190 ADDED fields
  (velMin/directionReliable/tauGaze/biphasic/phase2 × 19 patients × hsn and clinicalReadout.hsn)
  + 8 `findings` sentences + 8 `peripheralSigns` + 1 new sentence (biphasic in d21rec) + 8 DOM screens
  that display those sentences. ZERO changes to spv0/asym/tau/beatEar/dir/present/charge/cycles/peakVel
  or to verdict and localization — verified field by field across all 19 patients.


=== OSCILLOPSIA REQUIRES BILATERALITY + REWRITING THE DVA SENTENCE (V27, 2026-08-20) ===
Three changes in the DVA layer, all forced by the sources, none of which adds a free parameter.

(a) A NUMBER FOR GUINAND - [H41] Guinand 2012 instead of a bare "(Guinand 2012)".
  A bare-name citation was AMBIGUOUS: the Maastricht group published two 2012 papers on oscillopsia, and
  in their own literature this one is referred to as "Guinand et al. 2012b". Settled in favour of
  PMID 22431876 by two disjoint proofs: (1) GROUP COMPOSITION - 37 bilateral / 11 unilateral / 57 healthy,
  exactly the three-tier axis of the comment, whereas the competing paper (PMID 22844867, quality of life)
  has ONE arm of n=39 with no controls; (2) that second paper DOES NOT MEASURE ACUITY AT ALL - its
  instruments are SF-36, DHI, FES-I and a questionnaire, zero logMAR. The candidate field is closed:
  PubMed lists only four Guinand papers for 2012, the other two being taste disorders after tonsillectomy
  and an implant surgical technique. A bare-name citation was INVISIBLE to the zrodla:check gate - that is
  its declared blind spot.

(b) REWRITING THE DVA SENTENCE - two independent textual defects, both removed.
  - "the defining BVH complaint" -> a description of the MECHANISM. Criterion A of [H19] Strupp 2017 makes
    oscillopsia ONE OF TWO alternatives alongside the mandatory unsteadiness anchor, and the document
    itself reports 30-40% of patients complaining of it. The card should say what the patient feels and
    why, not announce a diagnosis from a different time axis.
  - "compensation does NOT repair this (HF gain is permanent)" -> "central compensation ALONE does not
    repair this (gain in the PASSIVE impulse stays low) - gaze-stabilisation exercises are a separate
    route, outside this model." Reason: the sentence hangs on dv.abnormal, so it fired for a CHRONIC
    COMPENSATED UVH - i.e. in the population of recommendation 2 in [H42] Hall 2022 (evidence quality I,
    strength STRONG "should offer"). A clinician read it as "there is nothing to rehabilitate". The
    PHYSICAL claim stays, because it is true of the model: comp is a strict no-op for dva() (measured:
    comp 0 and comp 1 give an identical logMAR of 0.2721, bit for bit; even timeline(...,{recovery:true})
    after 365 days moves it by 0.006). Two WORDS were at fault: "compensation" without a qualifier (in
    [H42] it is an UMBRELLA term that includes training) and "permanent" as a physiological claim.
    "Passive" is defensible at the source - [H43] Schubert 2008: gain during ACTIVE rotation rose in every
    patient, while during the PASSIVE impulse it did not improve significantly, and headImpulse() models
    exactly the passive impulse. The word "permanent" was stronger than the evidence (Lacour 2020 - a rise
    in passive gain too when rehabilitation began within 2 weeks). There are NO references in the sentence:
    by convention the findings layer does not cite (0 of 25 calls).

(c) OSCILLOPSIA - A BILATERALITY GATE. oscillopsia = (gP<0.6 && gL<0.6) && logMARLoss>=0.3, BVP_CUT=0.6.
  THE DEFECT THAT PROMPTED IT (measured, not deduced): a patient with a dead right ear and a healthy left
  one (gain 0.158/0.842) produced a logMAR of exactly 0.300 and received the label "OSCILLOPSIA (the
  defining BVH complaint)", while the same clinicalReadout reported localization "SUPERIOR nerve on the
  right" and verdict "peripheral" - a contradiction inside one paragraph, in a teaching tool. Symmetrically
  in the other direction: bilateralLoss(0.5) (gain 0.542/0.542 - an unambiguously BILATERAL deficit) got no
  flag at all. The threshold erred in both directions at the edges.
  THE AXIS OF THE SYMPTOM IS BILATERALITY, NOT MAGNITUDE: [H45] Karabulut 2023 (meta-analysis) - 22% in
  chronic unilateral hypofunction versus 50-70% in bilateral loss, "one vestibular organ might often be
  sufficient to enable gaze stabilization"; [H41] Guinand 2012 - 81% versus 9%. The domain of the threshold
  is therefore narrowed by criterion C of [H19] Strupp 2017 (horizontal vHIT below 0.6 BILATERALLY).
  Bilaterality is computed from BOTH gains SEPARATELY, NEVER from the mean - a mean confuses "one ear at
  zero plus one at unity" with "both at half".
  WHAT THIS CHANGE DOES NOT DO: the 0.3 threshold STAYS and does NOT come from Guinand. That paper states
  explicitly that acuity loss does not correlate with the SEVERITY of oscillopsia (replicated by Hermann
  2018, van Dooren 2019, Anson 2018) - but that is a claim about GRADING, whereas the oscillopsia field is
  a BINARY classifier. The dichotomous claim was tested by [H44] Geisinger 2024 and CONFIRMED: reporters
  lost 4.5+/-0.79 lines versus 2.6+/-0.56 (p<0.01), with NO difference in gain in any of the 6 canals.
  So gain is not a predictor, DVA is. The constants 0.6 / 0.2 / 0.3 were NOT touched - there is nothing to
  calibrate them with (the paper is paywalled and the abstract gives no per-group logMAR means).
  EMERGENT: bilaterality comes FOR FREE out of the binaural velocity estimate in headImpulse (W_IN=1/3,
  Ewald II with inhibitory clipping) - a dead labyrinth on one side still leaves the other ear at about
  0.84, because the healthy ear carries the estimate; only a genuinely bilateral deficit pushes BOTH
  readings below 0.6. Physics introduced in N7 for another purpose turns out to be a bilaterality detector.
  The constant 0.6 is not new in this model: the engine declared it TWICE (in the comment at GAIN_CUT and
  at abnormal in headImpulse) and never enforced it - V27 closes it in a third place.
  A BY-PRODUCT WORTH NAMING: both gains below 0.6 imply a logMAR above 0.24, so the conjunction with the
  0.3 threshold leaves a band of 0.24-0.30 in which the patient IS bilateral yet receives no flag
  (bilateralLoss(0.5) = 0.275). This reproduces the fact that only a fraction of BVP patients complain -
  a class of patient the engine previously could not generate.
  A SAFETY PROPERTY: the new predicate is a STRICT SUBSET of the old one (a conjunction), so it can only
  REMOVE a label, never add one. abnormal is untouched, so the set of patients for whom the DVA sentence
  appears at all does not change.
  DECLARED LIMITS: (1) STIMULUS MISMATCH - [H41] measured acuity during WALKING (active, predictable
  movement involving otoliths and proprioception), while the engine computes from a PASSIVE impulse; 0.6
  and 0.1 logMAR/line are TEACHING PROXIES, not a calibration. (2) A DELIBERATELY ACCEPTED FALSE NEGATIVE -
  about 22% of chronic UVH patients report oscillopsia [H45] and the gate will not flag them. (3) ORIGIN is
  not distinguished: a central patient with a low bilateral gain is flagged just the same (measured: gain
  0.112/0.112 with fixationGain 0 -> the flag fires), although [H44] suggests a requirement of peripheral
  origin; conversely one must not claim that central causes do not produce oscillopsia - downbeat nystagmus
  is a classic cause. (4) SACCADIC STRATEGY is outside the model: Ramaioli 2014 showed a patient reading at
  93% despite a gain of 0.59, because covert saccades occurred in 60% of movements; gainApparent is today
  PRODUCED AND UNCONSUMED in the engine - an open lead for a separate stage.

SCOPE PROOF (two layers deliberately separated, to make visible that the substantive fix was FREE):
  - PHYSICS (the bilaterality gate): 0 changed, 0 added, 0 removed keys. Verified field by field:
    meanGain/logMARLoss/linesLost/abnormal/oscillopsia identical across 190 comparisons (19 patients x
    2 paths x 5 fields). Exactly two bilateral patients keep the flag: scenario/bvh and bilateral.
  - TEXT (the sentence rewrite): 14 changed leaves - 8 findings sentences in the engine layer and 6 dom
    screens. This is a change of TEXT, not of behaviour; in dom['lab/skutek'] the sentence stands on both
    sides of the diff, so the golden shows it as a disappearance and an appearance at once.
ORACLE: npm run dva:check (tools/dva-check.mjs) - a NEW gate. Before V27 NO tools/*-check.mjs asserted on
  dva/logMARLoss/oscillopsia or on the text of the DVA sentence, and the physics fix itself is INVISIBLE to
  snapshot:check (0 changes in the golden) - so without this gate it would be entirely unguarded. It holds
  the invariant "the flag NEVER fires when either ear has a gain of at least 0.6".

=== THE DIAGNOSTIC CRITERIA FOR VESTIBULAR MIGRAINE — CLOSING THE GRACE CARD'S DANGLING POINTER (V28, 2026-08-20) — [H46] Lempert 2022 ===
The GRACE card (D8/V22) pointed, in the s-EVS column of the "Test of choice" row, to "vestibular-
migraine criteria". Those criteria WERE NOWHERE IN THE PROGRAM — and that is a measurement, not an
impression: before this change the whole repo (excluding dist/, node_modules/ and android/) gave
"ICHD" 0 hits, "photophob" 0, "phonophob" 0, "at least 5" 0, "5 minutes" 0. The only "criteria" in
the application were those for BPPV per Bárány/ICVD 2015 — a different entity. The clinician was
handed the instruction "apply the criteria" that the application could not honour.

WHY NO ORACLE CAUGHT IT. That sentence carried NO source number at all, and the zrodla:check gate
compares a number against the marker standing next to it — "a citation without a number" is its
explicitly declared boundary. The golden saw the sentence byte for byte, but the golden guards
WORDING, not whether a promise is covered. The hole was therefore structurally invisible to all
27 gates at once.

WHAT WAS ADDED.
 (a) The [H46] entry in both bibliographies — a consensus document of the Committee for
     Classification of Vestibular Disorders of the Bárány Society and the Migraine Classification
     Subcommittee of the IHS. The 2012 criteria WERE LEFT UNCHANGED in it; the literature review is
     new, particularly in the comments section, so citing them to this paper means citing 2012
     content. The entry carries the
     full set: four criteria of the definite form, THREE criteria of the probable form, the list of
     qualifying vestibular symptoms, the intensity definitions and the duration distribution with
     the counting rule for attacks lasting seconds.
 (b) vmCriteriaCard() — a new <details> card on the HINTS screen, directly under the GRACE card;
     the same pattern as hintsGrace (the hintsVmCrit state field, no re-render). The pointer from
     the GRACE card now leads to a card standing immediately beneath it. The card COMPUTES NOTHING
     and makes no diagnosis.
 (c) The CPN card in diagnostics: the sentence "the most common benign mimic" was an epidemiological
     claim WITH NO SOURCE. It is replaced by what the source actually says — [H46] Lempert 2022 —
     together with an explicit note on the boundary (point 1 below).
 (d) The comment on the vmInterictal preset in neuro-vor.js — a correction against the source,
     point 3 below.

THREE SOURCE BOUNDARIES, MEASURED IN THE FULL TEXT, NOT ASSUMED.
 1. The paper DOES NOT USE the term "central positional nystagmus" and does not split the attack
    nystagmus into central and peripheral. The CPN card therefore does not cite it for the word
    "central" — it cites it for what actually stands there: vestibular migraine may be PURELY
    POSITIONAL and then mimics BPPV, and that a benign mimic is diagnosed AFTER a central cause has
    been ruled out, not instead of it. TWO CORRECTIONS AFTER THE CLINICAL REVIEW, both in the same
    place: (i) the superlative "most common" is gone, because the source does not carry it — and so
    is the frequency grading altogether, because "a common mimic of persistent downbeat" is still
    uncovered epidemiology, now with a citation number beside it, which is worse than an openly
    unsourced claim; (ii) THE DIFFERENTIATING DETAIL (persistence, constant velocity, the 30°/s
    threshold) WAS TAKEN OFF THIS CARD and moved to the criteria card. The first V28 version put it
    on the "Management" line of a red-flag card, right after "do NOT reposition, refer for MRI" —
    and there the features "persistent, slow" read as AN ESCAPE HATCH from referring, all the more
    so because they are the very features by which the card DEFINES central nystagmus a dozen lines
    above, and because nobody measures a 30°/s threshold at the bedside without VOG. The card now
    carries the discipline idiom this application already has in jamCard: a diagnosis of EXCLUSION,
    the default path remains MRI.
 2. THE ICHD-3 ITEM NUMBER IS NOT IN THIS PAPER. It only says the entity was included in the
    ICHD-3 APPENDIX as the first step for new diagnoses; the number A1.6.6 comes from ICHD-3 itself
    and would need its own source, so it is not in the application. ICHD-3 does not know the
    probable form at all.
 3. SECTION 4.5 DOES NOT SAY THE INTERICTAL EXAMINATION IS NORMAL. It says findings CAN BE
    pathological during or shortly after an episode, and that between attacks they are NOT
    SUFFICIENTLY SPECIFIC to serve as a criterion — abnormalities are ALLOWED, merely stripped of
    diagnostic power. The comment on the vmInterictal preset claimed "the examination COMPLETELY
    NORMAL between attacks"; that claim is now marked as a DIDACTIC SIMPLIFICATION of the preset,
    not a thesis of that paper. The GRACE card's row on interictal examination IS LEFT UNTOUCHED,
    because it is a faithful digest of [H24] Edlow 2023 — the two sources speak here at different
    levels of detail, and substituting one for the other would falsify both. The instrumental rule
    the source REALLY gives works only toward EXCLUSION: PROFOUND ABNORMALITIES in the symptom-free
    interval — SEVERE hearing loss or complete unilateral or bilateral vestibular loss — usually
    indicate ANOTHER cause ("profound" qualifies the ABNORMALITIES in the source; the hearing loss
    is "severe").

THE CONTRAST WORTH NAMING. When the criteria for Menière's disease are met — especially with
audiometrically documented unilateral hearing loss — Menière's disease is diagnosed by the
criteria of [H20] Lopez-Escamez 2015, even in the presence of migraine symptoms; BOTH diagnoses are
made only with TWO DIFFERENT TYPES OF ATTACKS. And the other way round: migraine symptoms during a
vertigo attack prove nothing, because in patients with migraine caloric testing itself OFTEN
triggers a migraine attack within a day. This is the counter-rule against the naive classifier
"there are migraine symptoms, so this is vestibular migraine" — which is why the criteria card
carries it

WHAT THIS CHANGE DOES NOT DO. The engine STILL DOES NOT MODEL vestibular migraine and that
declaration stands — not one parameter and not one branch of physics was added; the vmInterictal
preset still has an empty params:{}. The criteria are PARAPHRASED, because the source licence is
CC BY-NC 4.0 and not CC BY: a verbatim reprint in a product distributed for a fee would be a
separate decision, not a side effect.

PROOF OF SCOPE (measured before the rebaseline, not estimated after it): snapshot:check lit up 26
keys and NOT ONE of them lies in the physics layer — 24 are dom/hints/preset/*, dom/hints/scenario/*
and dom/hints/nerve/* (the static GRACE card hangs on EVERY simulator screen, so a new card beneath
it moves them all), and 2 are dom/diag/dix/P/central and dom/diag/dix/L/central (the CPN card). The
engine, pose and shell layers: 0 changed keys. That is the entire content of the sentence "the
substantive correction was free for the physics" — measurable, not declared.
ORACLES: zrodla:check with a raised OCZEKIWANE constant (a new entry, new citations in the code and
in both documentation mirrors); the remaining 26 gates unchanged, including hints:dom and
hints:check, which touch neither the GRACE card nor the new one, because they work on the clinical
HINTS examination screens rather than on the simulator.
=== THE CRITERIA CARD WIRED INTO THE s-EVS TRIAGE NODE (V28c, 2026-08-20) ===
V28 placed the criteria card on the HINTS SIMULATOR screen, because that is where the GRACE card's
pointer came from. Clinically that is one screen too far, and it can be shown from the repo itself
rather than from taste: the leaf `wyzwalacz==="samoistny"` in triage-model.js returns
`kategoria:'sEVS'`, `sciezka:null`, names vestibular migraine, Menière's disease and vertebrobasilar
TIA, honestly says "the engine does not model them" — and ends there, offering NOTHING to do. It is
the ONLY leaf of the tree with an empty pathway. What is more, its own note reads "HINTS is not
appropriate either: it applies to a continuous vestibular syndrome with nystagmus, not to attacks" —
so the criteria stood on the very screen that this same node tells the clinician not to go to.

WHAT WAS DONE: `renderTriage` renders `vmCriteriaCard(true)` under the result card when — and only
when — `gotowe && w.kategoria==="sEVS"`. Above the card stands one sentence tying it to the node's
content: that these are the criteria of the FIRST of the three entities listed, and that the
exclusion block carries the other two.

WHY EXPANDED. The `true` argument forces it open. A collapsed <details> on a screen the clinician
reaches once in the workflow would repeat exactly the flaw of the original placement: the content
with the highest decision value would stay behind a click nobody makes.

WHY THIS DOES NOT TILT THE DIFFERENTIAL TOWARD MIGRAINE — a predictable objection, worth answering
outright. The card is not a digest about one entity: its EXCLUSION block (the content of criterion D)
carries the Menière rule together with the two-different-types-of-attacks condition, and the full set
of vertebrobasilar TIA red flags including vertebral artery dissection. Those are EXACTLY the other
two items from the same sentence of the model. The card therefore serves the node's whole three-way
split, not one third of it — which is why it stands here in full rather than as an extract.

ONE SOURCE, NO SECOND LITERAL: it is the same function as on the HINTS screen. A divergence between
the two places is structurally impossible rather than guarded by vigilance — the same convention as
"the label and the arrow from one ξ" (V5) and "act ≡ card bit for bit" (V19).

THE MODEL STAYS PURE. `triage-model.js` knows nothing of this card; the change is in the renderer
only. Proof, not declaration: `triage:check` (62 cases + 2176 combinations checked by invariants) is
UNTOUCHED by this change and green.

PROOF OF SCOPE: snapshot:check lit up EXACTLY ONE key — `dom/triage/sEVS`. Zero keys in the engine,
pose and shell layers; zero other triage keys. It was also verified IN REVERSE, in the browser, that
the card is ABSENT from the remaining triage outcomes: tEVS, the red flag, and the "in progress"
state — the `kategoria==="sEVS"` gate works both ways. `zrodla:check` unchanged (152): the card is
called a second time, but its strings sit on ONE source line and were already counted.

A BOUNDARY LEFT DELIBERATELY: the state field is named `hintsVmCrit` although it now serves two
screens. The name is historical; it was not changed together with the wiring, so that the diff
carries one thing at a time.

=== ICVD COVERAGE MAP — ALL 19 BÁRÁNY SOCIETY CONSENSUS DOCUMENTS (E0, 2026-08-21) ===
This chapter is a STATE OF COVERAGE, not a plan. The staged plan lives in `przesiew_icvd.md` on the
branch `claude/icvd-diagnostic-criteria-203f6a`; the source material (full texts and extractions)
sits outside the repository.

WHY IT EXISTS. Until 2026-08-21 the program applied ICVD criteria without holding them in its
knowledge base. MEASURED across the whole repository: 469 places make a statement about a disorder
or about a diagnostic criterion, of which 386 (82%) carry NO source number; 10 of the 16 ICVD
entities did not occur in the program at all. The sharpest case: the BPPV criteria, on which the
core function `baranyClassify` stands, had a documented provenance of "otoneurology memory"
(view_doc), and the pointer from the code named an engine_doc section that did not exist. Stage E0
closes the BIBLIOGRAPHIC layer of that hole: it assigns numbers and changes not one line of code.

FOURTEEN DOCUMENTS NOW HAVE A NUMBER AND ARE EITHER IN USE OR AWAITING USE.
NOTE — THIS LIST STATED SOMETHING UNTRUE ABOUT THE PROGRAM and was corrected on 2026-08-22. Three
entities were called ABSENT while all three had already entered src/ in stages D-CZAS and D-ORTO —
as a SIDE EFFECT of those stages, not as a scope decision. This is exactly the kind of staleness the
D6 ledger exists to prevent: a measurement that invalidated itself and nobody recounted it. On every
change to src/, CHECK whether this list still tells the truth.
WHEN WRITING HERE: no source number MAY begin a line. The first version of this note wrapped so that
a number fell in column 0, the bibliography parser read it as an ENTRY DEFINITION and gate ZR2 fired
at once. That is the same trap gated in V28b.
 · vestibular symptoms (dictionary) ....... [H47] — used indirectly via [H46]; never named
 · BPPV ................................... [H48] — CORE OF THE APP, until now unnumbered (E1)
 · vestibular paroxysmia .................. [H49] — one mention, in the migraine exclusions; since
   2026-08-22 WITH A NUMBER and with the time window per criterion B (it was understated to „seconds")
 · PPPD ................................... [H50] — PRESENT in src/ since stage D-CZAS: the CVS node
   names it with a number and an entry condition. Scope (whether to model it) is still decision D6
 · nystagmus classification ............... [H51] — CPN flags stand unsourced in two files (E2)
 · hemodynamic orthostatic dizziness ...... [H52] — PRESENT in src/ since stage D-ORTO: the sixth
   triage question carries the discriminating tool from note 1 of that paper
 · presbyvestibulopathy ................... [H53] — PRESENT in src/ since stage D-CZAS (CVS node);
   the gain-threshold dispute was SETTLED in E3a — the bands of [H19] and [H53] abut, they do not compete
 · mal de débarquement .................... [H54] — ABSENT; the axis that separates it from [H50]
 · vestibular migraine of childhood ....... [H55] — ABSENT; a second criteria set beside [H46]
 · SCDS ................................... [H56] — entity MODELLED, but on [H18], not on ICVD
 · motion sickness ........................ [H57] — ABSENT
 · vascular vertigo and dizziness ......... [H58] — red flags rest on [H24] and [H8], not criteria
 · AUVP / vestibular neuritis ............. [H59] — entity MODELLED widely, with no document
 · cervical dizziness (position paper) .... [H60] — ABSENT; the paper REFUSES to give criteria
 · ICVD review 2025 ....................... [H61] — states what does not exist yet

THREE DOCUMENTS ALREADY HAD NUMBERS: bilateral vestibulopathy [H19], Menière's disease [H20],
vestibular migraine [H46]. NOTE: [H19] and [H20] CARRY A DESCRIPTION OF THE ENTITY BUT NOT THE
CONTENT OF ITS CRITERIA — measured for [H20]: "30 dB" 0 hits, "20 min" 0, "12 h" 0. Completing
them is stage E4, not E0.

THE NINETEENTH DOCUMENT — the original vestibular migraine criteria (Lempert 2012) — GETS NO NUMBER
(user's decision, 2026-08-21). The criteria are cited from [H46], their current carrier; the 2012
version served as EVIDENCE, and the comparison is recorded in the [H46] entry.

WHAT THIS MAP DOES NOT SETTLE — AND WHAT WAS SETTLED ON 2026-08-22 (stage E6, user decision).
A bibliography entry does NOT mean the entity enters the ENGINE. The sentence that stood here
before — "OTOREPO is an application about BPPV and acute dizziness, not an atlas of otoneurology"
— is NO LONGER TRUE from this stage and has been replaced. The program is both, but in TWO
DISJOINT SCOPES, and the whole content of the decision lies in that disjointness:
  · THE ACUTE PATHWAY — where GRACE-3 and HINTS apply — stays GENERAL. Diagnoses are NOT
    multiplied there; the initial triage still selects an EXAMINATION PATHWAY, not an entity.
  · THE OTONEUROLOGY ATLAS (the sixth area of the app) carries ALL 18 consensus documents with
    their criteria, thresholds and source boundaries. It computes nothing and makes no diagnosis.
The scope register stopped being prose and became a FIELD the gate counts: every atlas entry
carries `wSilniku` from the closed set {modelled, criteria-without-a-model, out-of-scope} together
with MEASURED evidence. State on 2026-08-22: 7 modelled, 7 criteria without a model, 4 deliberately
outside the engine scope. "Deliberately out of scope" remains as good a decision as "included" —
but it is now RECORDED IN THE STRUCTURE, not in a paragraph that will age by itself. Exactly that
kind of ageing hit this list on 2026-08-22 and required a separate repair.
LINKING: triage nodes that have no pathway now lead to the atlas (spontaneous attacks, the chronic
syndrome, orthostatic dizziness). AT A RED FLAG THERE IS NO LINK, AND THAT IS A DECISION: the goal
there is action, and reading material dilutes urgency.

SEVENTY-FIVE CONFLICTS between the ICVD documents themselves (34 hard, 35 tensions, 6 successions)
await the user's decisions; eight of them block the writing of further chapters. Until they are
taken, NO disputed threshold enters the engine — in particular the gain thresholds carried by
entries [H19], [H53] and [H59].

=== BPPV CRITERIA — Bárány/ICVD 2015 (E1, 2026-08-21) — [H48] von Brevern 2015 ===
THIS IS THE SECTION THAT THREE POINTERS NAMED AND THAT DID NOT EXIST. The comment beside
`baranyClassify` admitted as much ("the old pointer 'engine_doc: KRYTERIA BARANY' named a section
that does not exist"), `barany-check.mjs` pointed here, and view_doc gave "otoneurology memory" as
the source of these criteria. The criteria are PARAPHRASED — the paper is all-rights-reserved
(see the [H48] entry).

HOW THE CLASSIFICATION IS BUILT. Section 2 of the paper = ESTABLISHED syndromes, section 3 =
EMERGING and controversial ones. In theory each of the three canals may be affected by
canalolithiasis or cupulolithiasis, giving six combinations per ear; the paper states that all of
them have been documented by eye-movement recordings and included in the classification EXCEPT
CUPULOLITHIASIS OF THE ANTERIOR CANAL. That is the only combination outside the catalogue — which
is why the card labels it "outside the ICVD classification" rather than "emerging" (fix in E1).

CRITERION D (in 3.2 it is C) IS WORDED IDENTICALLY ACROSS EIGHT SETS: "not attributable to another
disorder" — history and physical and neurological examination do not suggest another vestibular
disorder OR such a disorder was considered and ruled out by appropriate investigations OR it is
present as a comorbid condition THAT CAN BE CLEARLY DIFFERENTIATED.

--- SECTION 2: ESTABLISHED SYNDROMES ---
2.1 POSTERIOR CANAL CANALOLITHIASIS (pc-BPPV)
  A. recurrent attacks of POSITIONAL vertigo or dizziness, provoked by lying down or turning over
     in the supine position;
  B. attack duration < 1 min;
  C. positional nystagmus elicited AFTER A LATENCY OF ONE OR A FEW SECONDS by the Dix-Hallpike or
     the side-lying (diagnostic Semont) manoeuvre — a COMBINATION of torsional nystagmus with the
     upper pole of the eyes beating toward the LOWER ear AND vertical nystagmus beating UPWARD
     (toward the forehead), typically lasting < 1 minute;
  D. as above.
2.2 HORIZONTAL CANAL CANALOLITHIASIS (hc-BPPV)
  A. as 2.1;  B. attack duration < 1 min;
  C. positional nystagmus elicited AFTER A SHORT LATENCY OR WITH NO LATENCY on the supine roll test,
     beating HORIZONTALLY TOWARD THE LOWER ear with the head turned to EITHER side (GEOTROPIC
     direction-changing nystagmus), lasting < 1 min;   D. as above.
2.3 HORIZONTAL CANAL CUPULOLITHIASIS (hc-BPPV-cu) — NOTE: THREE CRITERIA, NOT FOUR
  A. as 2.1;
  B. positional nystagmus elicited after a brief or no latency on the supine roll test, beating
     horizontally TOWARD THE UPPER ear with the head turned to either side (APOGEOTROPIC
     direction-changing nystagmus), lasting > 1 MINUTE;
  C. not attributable to another disorder.
  THERE IS NO ATTACK-DURATION CRITERION HERE AT ALL — and this is not an omission of the paraphrase
  but the structure of the paper: in cupulolithiasis it is the NYSTAGMUS that is timed, not the
  attack. Note 1 explains why: an attack usually lasts < 1 min because the patient TURNS THE HEAD
  BACK to a position in which the vertigo stops; if the provoking position is held it lasts longer,
  and because of the spatial orientation of the affected cupula the patient may have PERSISTING
  vertigo or dizziness OF LOWER INTENSITY EVEN WHEN UPRIGHT.
  A MANDATORY EXCLUSION: apogeotropic positional nystagmus ALSO occurs as a sign of CENTRAL
  vestibular dysfunction, so excluding CNS disease is MANDATORY.
2.4 PROBABLE BPPV, SPONTANEOUSLY RESOLVED
  A. as 2.1;  B. attack duration < 1 min;
  C. NO observable nystagmus and NO vertigo on ANY positional manoeuvre;
  D. not attributable to another disorder.
  THE ENGINE DOES NOT KNOW THIS ENTITY. A negative positional test is a "model boundary" for us,
  while in the paper it can be a DIAGNOSIS. That is not a flaw of the physics — the engine simulates
  debris, and here the debris is gone — but the negative-result card could mention the possibility.
  To be settled outside E1.

--- SECTION 3: EMERGING AND CONTROVERSIAL SYNDROMES ---
3.1 ANTERIOR CANAL CANALOLITHIASIS (ac-BPPV)
  A. as 2.1;  B. attack duration < 1 min;
  C. positional nystagmus elicited IMMEDIATELY OR after a latency of one or a few seconds by the
     Dix-Hallpike manoeuvre (ON ONE OR BOTH SIDES) or in the supine straight head-hanging position,
     beating PREDOMINANTLY VERTICALLY DOWNWARD and lasting < 1 min;   D. as above.
  A SPLIT THE ENGINE DOES NOT HAVE: 3.1.1 DEFINITE anterior canalolithiasis — diagnosable on the
  basis of IMMEDIATE resolution of the positional nystagmus after therapeutic manoeuvres;
  3.1.2 PROBABLE — diagnosable ONLY after exclusion of CNS disease, when the nystagmus is REFRACTORY
  to therapeutic manoeuvres. This is response-to-treatment used as a discriminator — the only such
  instance in the paper.
3.2 POSTERIOR CANAL CUPULOLITHIASIS (pc-BPPV-cu) — THREE CRITERIA
  A. as 2.1;
  B. positional nystagmus elicited after a brief or no latency by a "HALF DIX-HALLPIKE MANOEUVRE",
     beating torsionally with the upper pole of the eye toward the lower ear AND vertically upward
     (toward the forehead), lasting > 1 MINUTE;
  C. not attributable to another disorder.
  THE TEST POSITION IS A "HALF" DIX-HALLPIKE, NOT THE FULL ONE — the engine provokes this form with
  the full manoeuvre. The difference is real (a different hang angle) but does NOT change the
  direction of the nystagmus or the mechanism; recorded as a naming/procedural divergence, not as a
  physics error.
3.3 MULTIPLE CANAL LITHIASIS (mc-BPPV)
  A. as 2.1;  B. attack duration < 1 min;
  C. positional nystagmus COMPATIBLE WITH CANALOLITHIASIS OF MORE THAN ONE CANAL during the
     Dix-Hallpike manoeuvre AND the supine roll test;   D. as above.
  THE ENGINE MODELS ONE CANAL AT A TIME — it does not know this entity. The paper stresses that
  positional manoeuvres for the vertical AND the horizontal canals must be performed in EVERY
  patient with positional vertigo, precisely because several canals may be involved.
3.4 POSSIBLE BPPV
  A. attacks of positional vertigo MISSING ONE of the criteria of a disorder coded above;
  B. not attributable to another disorder.
  THIS IS WHERE patients with BPPV without nystagmus are coded (a controversial matter, see the
  comment to 2.1), along with cases showing atypical nystagmus.

--- THE NOTES CARRY HALF THE CLINICAL CONTENT ---
Without its notes this paper's criteria are incomplete. The ones that matter for the engine:
 · THE LATENCY may in RARE cases reach 40 SECONDS (note 8 to 2.1). The engine's chip says "1–5 s" —
   that is typicality, not a bound; the paper sets NO upper bound of 5 s.
 · THE NYSTAGMUS DURATION in 2.1 usually DOES NOT EXCEED 40 SECONDS before it fades spontaneously —
   while criterion C says "typically < 1 minute". Two different numbers for one entity, both from
   the source.
 · A crescendo-decrescendo ENVELOPE (rapid rise, slower decay) — the engine reproduces it
   emergently from debris physics, not from an annotation.
 · REVERSAL: after the initial nystagmus stops, a REVERSED nystagmus of lesser intensity may appear;
   on sitting up reversal is COMMON, and FATIGABILITY on repeated testing is a COMMON phenomenon.
 · THE APPEARANCE DEPENDS ON GAZE: torsion is more prominent in the LOWER eye, the vertical
   component in the UPPER eye; with fixation retained the nystagmus looks predominantly TORSIONAL
   (fixation suppresses torsion less than the vertical component). In HEAD coordinates the movement
   stays in the canal's plane regardless of eye position.
 · TRIGGERS BEYOND THE BED: not only lying down but also tilting the head backward or forward, and
   for the horizontal canal RAPID LATERAL HEAD ROTATIONS WHILE ERECT.
 · ATTACK DURATION: patients OVERESTIMATE the length of a single attack; mild residual symptoms may
   last minutes or hours, and attacks may be triggered in series. Vertigo should nonetheless not
   generally exceed 1 minute — attacks CONSISTENTLY longer should prompt consideration of an
   alternative or additional diagnosis.

--- TWO DIVERGENCES OF THE ENGINE FROM THE CRITERIA, NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN (decisions D4 and D5) ---
1. THE ANTERIOR CANAL: criterion 3.1 C demands nystagmus lasting < 1 min, and the engine measures
   61.25 s. THE USER'S DECISION: the physics stays, the divergence is NAMED. The reason: the
   anterior canal attack in this engine is EMERGENT (the debris starts pressed against the cupula)
   rather than tuned to a number; a difference of 1.25 s lies within the uncertainty of any clinical
   measurement and does not change the diagnosis. The trial chip says "Transient (≈1 min)" and tells
   THE TRUTH ABOUT ITS OWN MODEL — the barany:check gate compares the measurement against the chip's
   wording exactly so that the chip cannot lie. Restoring a shared "< 60 s" there would trip the gate
   immediately.
2. ROLL TEST GEOMETRY: the paper gives a head elevation of "about 30°", the engine computes 10.3°
   (HC_FLEX_DEG). THE USER'S DECISION: 10.3° stays, with an explicit note. The reason: the engine's
   angle is derived from CANAL ANATOMY (the IE-Map atlas, Reid's plane), and the engine's own
   measurement puts the optimum at +10.30° with a 5.9–14.5% loss at 30°. The pedigree of the 30°
   canon is CALORIC (convection requires the canal vertical; in a positional test there is no
   convection), and the literature is self-contradictory about its purpose. THE DELIBERATE COST OF
   THIS DECISION: at 30° a mechanism of pseudo-spontaneous nystagmus operates that does not operate
   at 10.3° — the engine will therefore not reproduce it, and we must not pretend otherwise.

--- SOURCE BOUNDARIES, MEASURED IN THE FULL TEXT ---
 · CANALITH JAM and PERSISTENT GEOTROPIC DCPN: the paper DESCRIBES both in its introduction but
   gives them NO code and NO criteria. Canalith jam is a POSTULATED locking of the cupula; persistent
   geotropic DCPN is described as a picture compatible with NEITHER mobile debris NOR debris on the
   cupula, tentatively linked to a change in the density of cupula or endolymph — with the explicit
   caveat that "this remains speculative". The engine marks them "outside the ICVD classification"
   and is to keep doing so.
 · THE ENGINE MODELS THREE ENTITIES OUTSIDE THE CATALOGUE (light cupula, short arm, canalith jam)
   and ONE combination explicitly excluded from it (anterior canal cupulolithiasis). All four are
   labelled — that is a condition of the card's honesty, not an ornament.
 · FOUR CODES THE ENGINE DOES NOT HAVE AT ALL: 2.4, 3.3, 3.4, and the 3.1.1/3.1.2 split.

A LIMIT OF THE PROOF, MEASURED DURING E1 AND WORTH RECORDING: the anterior-canal label fix is NOT
COVERED BY THE GOLDEN SET. Asked directly, the golden holds NOT ONE key with the anterior canal in
its cupulolithiasis form (searched: "headhang", "anterior", "przedni" — 0 hits among the keys), and
the `dom/diag/dix/*/antMode` keys that did light up in E1 lit up for a DIFFERENT reason: the [H48]
number added to the card's note, which is shared by all subtypes. The fix was therefore verified
DIRECTLY on the `baranyClassify` function (anterior+cupulo → "outside the ICVD classification", the
other six combinations bit-for-bit unchanged), not by an oracle. For whoever returns here: this is a
place for a new golden case, not for trust.


=== MÉNIÈRE'S DISEASE CRITERIA — a five-society consensus 2015 (E4a, 2026-08-21) — [H20] Lopez-Escamez 2015 ===
THE [H20] Lopez-Escamez 2015 ENTRY HAD BEEN IN THE BIBLIOGRAPHY FOR A LONG TIME AND CARRIED NONE OF
THE CRITERIA. That is a measurement, not an impression. In `engine_doc.txt` before this change:
"35 dB" 0 hits, "co najmniej dwa" 0, "audiogram" 0, "Tumarkin" 0; "30 dB", "20 min" and "12 h" one
hit each — and all three fall in the same sentence of the E0 chapter that RECORDS the absence, not
in any statement of the criteria. Across all of `src/`: "30 dB" 0, "35 dB" 0, "20 min" 0, "12 h" 0,
"Tumarkin" 0. The contrast is sharp: the NAME of the disease occurs in `src/` 62 TIMES across five
files — the `sEVS` leaf of the triage tree, the `meniereP`/`meniereL` presets of the HINTS simulator,
the `clinicalReadout` localization, the laboratory sliders and the GRACE card — while the program
could not say HOW THE DISEASE IS DIAGNOSED. The criteria are PARAPHRASED: the publisher's footer in the
PDF reads "IOS Press and the authors. All rights reserved", permitting printing/downloading for
INDIVIDUAL USE — so, as with [H48] von Brevern 2015, a verbatim reprint in a distributed product
would be a separate decision, not a side effect.

WHOSE DOCUMENT THIS IS. It is not a Bárány Society paper alone. The criteria were formulated
JOINTLY by the Classification Committee of the Bárány Society, the Japan Society for Equilibrium
Research, the European Academy of Otology and Neurotology (EAONO), the Equilibrium Committee of the
American Academy of Otolaryngology-Head and Neck Surgery (AAO-HNS) and the Korean Balance Society,
ITERATIVELY over four years (2010-14). THE LIMIT OF THAT SENTENCE, BECAUSE IT IS EASY TO OVERSTEP:
the paper says ONLY that the AAO-HNS Equilibrium Committee CO-AUTHORED these criteria. It does NOT
say that the AAO-HNS adopted them, that it points to them, or that "the Bárány criteria" and "the
AAO-HNS criteria" mean THE SAME THING after 2015 — no such sentence is in the text and none may be
cited from here. What the paper does say about the AAO-HNS runs the other way: its own guidelines of
1972/1985/1995 stand in Appendix 2 as a HISTORICAL REPRINT (see below), outside the 2015
classification.

TWO CATEGORIES, NOT FOUR. The 2015 classification knows EXACTLY two forms: DEFINITE and PROBABLE.
Measured: "possible" 1 hit, "certain" 2 — and ALL three lie in the appendices, i.e. in the HISTORICAL
criteria. This paper has NO "possible" and no "certain (histopathologically confirmed)" category. Nor
does it stage: "staging" 0 hits, "functional level" 0.

--- DEFINITE MD — FOUR CRITERIA, A CONJUNCTION ---
  A. TWO OR MORE SPONTANEOUS episodes of vertigo, each lasting FROM 20 MINUTES TO 12 HOURS;
  B. AUDIOMETRICALLY DOCUMENTED low- to medium-frequency sensorineural hearing loss in ONE ear,
     defining the affected ear, on AT LEAST ONE OCCASION — before, during OR after one of the
     episodes of vertigo;
  C. FLUCTUATING aural symptoms (hearing, tinnitus or fullness) in the AFFECTED ear;
  D. not BETTER ACCOUNTED FOR by another vestibular diagnosis.

THE AUDIOMETRIC THRESHOLDS — TO THE DIGIT, BECAUSE THAT IS WHERE CRITERION B'S FORCE LIES (note 4).
 · UNILATERAL: pure-tone thresholds for BONE-CONDUCTED sound WORSE in the affected ear than in the
   contralateral ear by AT LEAST 30 dB HL — at EACH of TWO CONTIGUOUS frequencies BELOW 2000 Hz.
   This is an INTERAURAL DIFFERENCE threshold, not an absolute one.
 · BILATERAL: when the low-frequency loss is bilateral the difference stops working — then the
   ABSOLUTE bone-conduction thresholds must be AT LEAST 35 dB HL at each of two contiguous
   frequencies below 2000 Hz.
 · A BOUNDARY OF THE SOURCE INSIDE CRITERION B — WHICH IS WHY "TO THE DIGIT" COVERS HALF THE RANGE.
   Criterion B demands "low- to MEDIUM-frequency" hearing loss, yet note (4) defines thresholds ONLY
   for "Low-frequency SNHL" and ties both numbers to frequencies BELOW 2000 Hz. Measured:
   "medium-frequency" 1 hit (the abstract), "mediumfrequency" 1 (criterion B itself; the missing
   hyphen is a PDF line-break artefact), NOT ONE in note 4; "contiguous" 2, both at the low-frequency
   thresholds. THE PAPER GIVES NO THRESHOLD FOR THE "MEDIUM" PART. This is precisely the place where
   a knowledge base most easily writes a number from memory — it must not.
 · A REINFORCEMENT, NOT A REQUIREMENT: if multiple audiograms are available, demonstrating RECOVERY
   of the low-frequency loss at some point in time FURTHER SUPPORTS the diagnosis — the paper offers
   this as support, not as a condition.
 · Bilateral SYNCHRONOUS loss (symmetric or asymmetric) does occur in some patients, but should raise
   concern for autoimmune inner ear disease; when progressing slowly over years it may favour migraine
   as an alternate explanation of the vertigo episodes OR a comorbid condition. Bilateral
   low-frequency loss is also seen in early non-syndromic progressive deafness DFNA6/14 (WFS1 gene) —
   but vertigo attacks have NOT been associated with that group of mutations.
 · After several episodes the loss also involves the middle and high frequencies, leading to PAN-TONAL
   hearing loss — so the "low- to medium-frequency" picture is an EARLY picture.

--- PROBABLE MD — AND A REDACTIONAL FACT THAT MUST NOT BE QUIETLY FIXED ---
  A. TWO OR MORE episodes of vertigo OR DIZZINESS, each lasting FROM 20 MINUTES TO 24 HOURS;
  B. FLUCTUATING aural symptoms (hearing, tinnitus or fullness) in the affected ear;
  D. not BETTER ACCOUNTED FOR by another vestibular diagnosis.

THE PRINTED LIST FOR PROBABLE MD HAS NO ITEM C. It runs A → B → D. Item B carries the CONTENT of
definite criterion C (aural symptoms), while the letter D stays D — so the gap is IN THE MIDDLE, not
at the end, and this is neither a clean renumbering nor a clean preservation of letters. That no ITEM
was lost is visible from the paper's abstract: it describes probable MD as episodic vestibular
symptoms (vertigo or dizziness) associated with fluctuating aural symptoms over a period from 20
minutes to 24 hours — two positive elements plus an exclusion, exactly as many as the list holds.
A LETTER went missing, not a criterion. RENUMBERING TO A/B/C WOULD BE OUR EDITING, NOT THE SOURCE'S
CONTENT — which is why the letters stay faithful here: A, B, D.

THREE DIFFERENCES FROM THE DEFINITE FORM — plus one word that disappears.
 1. THE QUALIFYING SYMPTOM: vertigo OR DIZZINESS (definite: vertigo only).
 2. THE UPPER TIME BOUND: 24 HOURS instead of 12. The lower bound of 20 minutes is the same in both.
 3. NO AUDIOMETRIC CRITERION OF ANY KIND — probable MD REQUIRES NO AUDIOGRAM. The whole of definite
    criterion B falls away, together with the 30/35 dB HL thresholds.
 (·) THE WORD "SPONTANEOUS" ALSO DISAPPEARS: definite MD demands "spontaneous episodes", probable MD
    speaks of episodes without that adjective. Formally this is not a separate criterion but a
    requirement removed from inside criterion A — yet it genuinely loosens it.
 Two further, smaller differences, also measured and worth recording: note (1) of the probable form
 REQUIRES the aural symptoms to be reported DURING the episode (the wording of definite criterion C
 imposes no such constraint) and ALLOWS conductive or mixed hearing loss in the first years; and its
 differential list is SHORTER — note (2) omits vestibular paroxysmia and recurrent unilateral
 vestibulopathy, both of which note (9) of the definite form lists, and it says they "do not explain
 the CLINICAL SYNDROME", where note (9) says they "do not explain the DIAGNOSIS". The paper does not
 comment on whether these differences are deliberate.

AN INTERNAL TENSION IN THE PAPER — TWO PLACES, RECORDED AS FACT, NOT AS AN ACCUSATION.
 · Note (1) of the DEFINITE form states plainly that episodic dizziness and unsteadiness are NOT
   considered criteria defining this disease — while criterion A of the PROBABLE form admits
   dizziness. The paper does not comment on the discrepancy and DOES NOT DEFINE DIZZINESS: it defines
   vertigo only, by reference to the symptom glossary [H47] Bisdorff 2009.
 · Note (4) says the affected ear is determined by the hearing loss of criterion B — yet probable MD
   has no audiometric criterion B, although its item B speaks of the "AFFECTED ear". HOW TO DETERMINE
   THE AFFECTED EAR IN PROBABLE MD, THE PAPER DOES NOT SAY. That is a boundary of the source, not a
   blank to be filled from otoneurological memory.

--- THE NOTES THAT CARRY CLINICAL CONTENT (definite form, 1-9) ---
 · EPISODE DURATION IS DEFINED OPERATIONALLY (note 3): it is THE TIME THE PATIENT HAS TO BE AT REST
   AND CANNOT MOVE. Not "how long they felt unwell" — and that is the only reason the 20 min - 12 h
   window can be elicited from a history at all. Episodes SHORTER than 20 minutes or LONGER than
   12 hours do occur, but NEITHER is a common finding and each should prompt consideration of other
   disorders. The paper adds that the duration can be hard to define because RESIDUAL symptoms persist
   after an episode.
 · BRIEF EPISODES TRIGGERED BY POSITIONAL CHANGES OF THE HEAD SHOULD SUGGEST OTHER CAUSES, such as
   BPPV (note 3) — the source itself points from here back to OTOREPO's core and to [H48] von Brevern
   2015. When short episodes do occur, they are usually SPONTANEOUS.
 · THE TULLIO PHENOMENON AND PRESSURE SENSITIVITY DO NOT EXCLUDE THIS DISEASE (note 2): some patients
   have episodes of vertigo lasting seconds to minutes, triggered by high-intensity low-frequency
   sound and by pressure changes. The paper places these LATER in the disease and explains them
   tentatively by advanced hydrops bringing the membranous labyrinth close to the stapes footplate.
   THIS TOUCHES THE ENGINE'S SCDS BRANCH DIRECTLY: Tullio is NOT pathognomonic of a third window here,
   and Table 1 lists third-window syndromes among the mimics. Checking specificity belongs to
   [H56] Ward 2021 and [H18] Minor 2000, not to this paper.
 · DELAYED MÉNIÈRE'S DISEASE (note 5): sensorineural hearing loss may ANTEDATE the vertigo episodes by
   months or years. This variant has been called "delayed hydrops", but the paper states plainly that
   THE PREFERRED TERM IS "DELAYED MD", because hydrops is a PATHOLOGICAL finding, not a clinical
   diagnosis. The reverse order occurs too: episodic vertigo may precede the hearing loss by weeks or
   months, but tinnitus or aural fullness is usually associated with the FIRST episode of vertigo.
 · A TEMPORAL ASSOCIATION WITHIN 24 HOURS (note 6): patients not infrequently observe the link between
   hearing and the episode themselves — generally as a CHANGE IN HEARING WITHIN 24 HOURS of the
   episode. In the first few years the loss fluctuates SPONTANEOUSLY; after repeated attacks it may
   progress and become PERMANENT, and the vertigo episodes then cease to be related to the aural
   symptoms.
 · TUMARKIN ATTACKS (note 7): a sudden loss of vestibulo-spinal reflexes producing SUDDEN FALLS, less
   often lateropulsion, lasting SECONDS or — rarely — a few minutes (vestibular drop attacks, otolithic
   crises, Tumarkin's otolithic crises). OTOREPO has no such axis today.
 · TINNITUS AND FULLNESS (note 8): an increase of tinnitus or aural fullness in the affected ear is
   OFTEN associated with the episode in the first years; tinnitus may become PERSISTENT once the
   hearing loss is permanent.
 · THE DIFFERENTIAL (note 9): TIA, vestibular migraine, vestibular paroxysmia, recurrent unilateral
   vestibulopathy and other vestibular disorders. MRI MAY BE REQUIRED to exclude vestibular schwannoma
   or endolymphatic sac tumour — MRI is never a criterion OF DIAGNOSIS here, always a tool OF
   EXCLUSION. Table 1 lists 15 mimicking entities.

FAMILIAL MÉNIÈRE'S DISEASE (comment 4.3) — THIS IS NOT A SEPARATE DIAGNOSTIC CATEGORY.
The paper does not create a third form beside definite and probable. It gives an OPERATIONAL
DEFINITION: familial MD should be CONSIDERED if AT LEAST ONE other relative — FIRST OR SECOND
degree — fulfils ALL the criteria of definite OR probable MD. It is seen in 8-9% of sporadic cases in
populations of European descent; most described families show autosomal dominant inheritance, but
familial MD is genetically heterogeneous (mitochondrial and recessive patterns also occur). A family
history of sensorineural hearing loss, migraine or recurrent vertigo in several members JUSTIFIES a
familial investigation. In other words: "familial" describes the patient's CONTEXT, whose diagnosis
was still made by the same four (or three) criteria.

THE APPENDICES ARE HISTORICAL REPRINTS — THE EASIEST TRAP IN THE WHOLE FILE.
Appendix 1 reprints the Japan Society for Equilibrium Research criteria of 1974 (three conditions;
"definite" = 1-3, "suspicious or uncertain" = 1 and 3 OR 2 and 3), and Appendix 2 the AAO-HNS scale of
1995 (certain / definite / probable / possible). NEITHER SET BELONGS TO THE 2015 CLASSIFICATION. One
PDF therefore holds THREE different category grids, two of them obsolete — which is where the whole
family of errors like "possible Ménière's disease per Bárány" or "CNS exclusion is a Bárány criterion"
comes from. Both phrases come from the appendices.

--- WHAT THE ENGINE MODELS OF THIS: NOTHING — AND THAT IS NOT A FLAW BUT A DIFFERENT AXIS ---
The engine computes `meniere(ear, opts)` in `src/engine/neuro-vor.js` (MENIERE_PHASE at line 151, the
function at 157-168) as A MODEL OF LABYRINTHINE TONE DURING AN ATTACK: four phases — `irritative`
(tone 150, gain 1.0), `nullpoint` (tone R0, gain 1.0), `paretic` (tone 0, gain 0.5), `interictal`
(tone R0, gain 1.0) — plus `caloricLoss` defaulting to 0.55 and an EXPLICIT `lesionEar`. Reversal of
the nystagmus direction and the caloric↔vHIT dissociation fall out of that emergently, and the
`meniereP`/`meniereL` preset in `src/app/actions.js` (lines 93-94) shows the irritative phase.

NONE OF THOSE AXES IS CARRIED BY [H20] Lopez-Escamez 2015. Measured in the full text: "irritative"
0 hits, "paretic" 0, "recovery nystagmus" 0, "caloric" 0, "vHIT" 0, "head impulse" 0, "VEMP" 0. The
word "nystagmus" occurs twice and NEVER in the criteria — once in the introduction, in a list of ICVD
work in progress, once in the reprinted Japanese criteria of 1974.

THE DIFFERENCE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT QUANTITATIVE, and must be named as plainly as the vestibular
migraine chapter says "the engine does not model this entity". THE ENGINE MODELS THE NYSTAGMUS OF THE
ATTACK. THE CRITERIA ARE MADE FROM HISTORY AND AUDIOMETRY. The two sets have NO INTERSECTION: the
criteria require NO test of vestibular function at all, and the engine holds NONE of the quantities
the criteria demand. The practical consequence runs both ways:
 1. This paper CANNOT serve as validation for the phase model of the nystagmus. The [H20] entry is
    COMPOSITE — Lopez-Escamez 2015 AND McClure 1981, "Recovery nystagmus in Ménière's disease" — and
    its summary line reads "the paroxysmal nystagmus phases (irritation→paresis→recovery)". The zeros
    measured above for those three words mean ONLY THIS: THAT PART DOES NOT COME FROM THE 2015 PAPER.
    That it comes from McCLURE 1981 FOLLOWS FROM THE SHAPE OF THE ENTRY (it holds two papers, and the
    second carries this in its title), NOT FROM A MEASUREMENT: McClure's text is NOT part of this
    review, so nothing may be cited from it here. This is exactly the shape of pointer under which
    one most easily attributes to one
    paper a sentence belonging to the other — cite `[H20]` beside the phases and you cite McClure;
    cite `[H20]` beside the criteria and you cite Lopez-Escamez. The number is shared, the papers are
    not.
 2. CRITERION B IS INEXPRESSIBLE IN TODAY'S MODEL, and hard so. The hearing axis in the engine is
    `hearingL`/`hearingR` — a DIMENSIONLESS 0..1 quantity (`src/engine/neuro-vor.js` lines 1130-1131),
    read through a finger-rub threshold. Criterion B demands BONE-CONDUCTION thresholds in dB HL, at
    NAMED contiguous frequencies below 2000 Hz, compared interaurally or absolutely. The model has no
    frequency axis, no dB scale and no bone conduction — so criterion B is not "not yet implemented"
    but UNREPRESENTABLE without a new axis. A criteria CARD is therefore entirely feasible (it
    computes nothing); DERIVING the diagnosis from engine parameters IS NOT.
 3. The engine's sentences about a "Ménière pattern" (the caloric↔vHIT dissociation: `neuro-vor.js`
    914, 1306, 1327-1330; the sliders in `src/app/lab-model.js` 90-91) have NEITHER SUPPORT NOR
    CONTRADICTION in this paper — the criteria are simply silent on that axis. If they are ever to
    carry a number, it must be the number of ANOTHER paper.

--- SOURCE BOUNDARIES, MEASURED IN THE FULL TEXT ---
 · THIS PAPER CONTAINS NO TREATMENT — NOT ONE SENTENCE. "betahistine" 0 hits, "diuretic" 0,
   "gentamicin" 0, "rehabilitation" 0, "prophyla" 0. "treatment" 2 hits and "therapy" 2 — and every
   one of them is HISTORICAL CONTEXT OR A TITLE: a mention that the AAO-HNS guidelines of 1972/1985/
   1995 served to evaluate the effectiveness of medical and surgical treatments, a mention of an EAONO
   working group preparing European guidelines for diagnosis and treatment, and the title of reference
   [5]. This is a PURELY DIAGNOSTIC document and no management may be cited from it. (Unlike 3.1.1/
   3.1.2 in [H48] von Brevern 2015, response to treatment is not used here as a discriminator either.)
 · THIS PAPER NEVER USES THE WORD "CENTRAL" IN ITS OWN TEXT, and never uses "peripheral" anywhere
   (0 hits). "central" has 2 hits: one is a hospital name in the TITLE of a bibliographic entry, the
   other is Appendix 1 — the reprinted Japanese criteria of 1974 ("Exclusion of central nervous system
   involvement"). THE CNS-EXCLUSION SENTENCE MUST NOT BE ATTRIBUTED TO THE 2015 PAPER. Note the exact
   force of this: the paper does not use the central/peripheral DICHOTOMY and does not call Ménière's
   disease "peripheral" — but IN SUBSTANCE it demands exclusion of a vascular cause, and demands it
   broadly, in note (9) and in comment 4.6 (TIA, stroke, AICA infarction, MRI with MRA where
   clinically important doubt exists). What is missing is the WORD and the AXIS, not the caution.
 · VESTIBULAR MIGRAINE IS NOT A COMORBID CONDITION IN THIS PAPER — AND THE DISTINCTION IS WORD FOR
   WORD. The comorbidity sentence names MIGRAINE (no adjective), BPPV and some forms of systemic
   autoimmune conditions: these "are considered co-morbidities and DO NOT EXPLAIN the diagnosis" of
   MD. VESTIBULAR MIGRAINE stands instead in the ADJACENT sentence, on the DIFFERENTIAL list beside
   TIA and vestibular paroxysmia, and appears in Table 1 of mimics. The paper therefore puts migraine
   and vestibular migraine on TWO DIFFERENT SIDES: the first does not invalidate the diagnosis, the
   second is a COMPETING diagnosis. Collapse the two sentences into one and you get a claim the source
   does not make.
 · THIS PAPER ESTABLISHES NO RULE OF PRECEDENCE OVER VESTIBULAR MIGRAINE. Measured: "two different
   types" 0 hits, "even if" 0, "should be diagnosed" 0, "precedence" 0. The rule "Ménière's disease
   should be diagnosed even if migraine symptoms occur during the attacks; BOTH diagnoses only in
   patients with two different types of attacks" belongs to [H46] Lempert 2022 (its section on overlap
   with Ménière's disease) — where [H20] Lopez-Escamez 2015 is the paper Lempert points to FOR THE
   CRITERIA THEMSELVES. The correct citation therefore looks the way it does in chapter V28 above:
   the number [H20] attached to the word "CRITERIA", not to the rule.
 · WHAT THE PAPER DOES GIVE ABOUT MIGRAINE (comment 4.4) — and this is card-worthy content:
   fluctuating hearing loss, tinnitus and aural pressure MAY occur in vestibular migraine, but
   sensorineural loss in migraine does NOT TYPICALLY PROGRESS over the years to severe loss; when
   hearing loss does develop in vestibular migraine it is OFTEN BILATERAL, whereas involvement of BOTH
   ears FROM THE ONSET is RARE in Ménière's disease. Migraine headache and phonophobia MAY be present
   during Ménière attacks, and the pathophysiological relationship between the two remains UNCLEAR.
 · THE PAPER GIVES NO CLASSIFICATION CODES: "ICD" 0 hits, "ICHD" 0, "H81" 0. The only classification
   acronym is ICVD.
 · THE PAPER SAYS NOTHING ABOUT CHILDREN ("child" 0, "pediatric" 0). The only age references are the
   third to seventh decade as age of onset and older age as a vascular risk factor.
 · HYDROPS IS NOT A CRITERION (comment 4.2). 3 T MRI with a gadolinium chelate visualises hydrops in
   93% of symptomatic ears BUT ALSO IN 65% of asymptomatic contralateral ears — the paper offers this
   pair as a LIMIT OF SPECIFICITY, not as an argument for imaging. Electrocochleography correlates
   with cochlear hydrops, but the fluctuating symptoms limit its usefulness early in the course. The
   introduction already states that hydrops per se DOES NOT EXPLAIN all clinical features.

--- A CONFLICT LEFT OPEN: 12 HOURS vs 24 HOURS ---
WE DO NOT SETTLE IT — we describe it, because it concerns the structure of the classification, not an
engine parameter. DEFINITE MD demands episodes of 20 min - 12 h; PROBABLE MD allows 20 min - 24 h but
REQUIRES NO AUDIOGRAM. The consequence is paradoxical in the direction that matters clinically: a
patient with 18-hour episodes, with AUDIOMETRICALLY DOCUMENTED unilateral low-frequency hearing loss
and fluctuating aural symptoms, CANNOT be definite — he fails criterion A — and so lands in probable,
exactly where a patient with no audiogram at all lands. BETTER DOCUMENTATION BUYS HIM NO HIGHER
DEGREE OF CERTAINTY, and in that category his audiogram is read by nothing. The classification has no
intermediate category. Separately: an episode lasting 30 hours falls OUTSIDE BOTH forms. The paper
does not comment on this tension; it is for the user to decide whether a card should name it outright
or merely reproduce the windows.

--- WHAT THIS ABUTS (without prejudging any decision) ---
 · THE TIME WINDOWS DO NOT DISCRIMINATE AGAINST VESTIBULAR MIGRAINE, contrary to intuition. The
   interval 20 min - 12 h lies ENTIRELY INSIDE the 5 min - 72 h of [H46] Lempert 2022. What
   discriminates is the AUDIOGRAM (criterion B) and the fluctuation of aural symptoms (criterion C),
   not the length of the attack. The only measured QUANTITATIVE difference lies elsewhere: Ménière's
   disease requires ≥ 2 episodes, vestibular migraine ≥ 5. THAT IS NOT A DISCRIMINATOR TO BE USED
   WITHOUT THE USER'S DECISION: the review filed this pair as HARD CONFLICT K9 (thresholds of
   2 / 3 / 2-4 / 5 / 10 across the nineteen ICVD documents), whose point is the reverse of the
   intuition — at the 2nd episode PROBABLE Ménière's disease is already reachable (with no
   audiogram), vestibular migraine only at the 5th, so the order in which diagnoses "unlock" is a
   function of the THRESHOLD, not of pathophysiology. The decision belongs to the user (§E).
 · VESTIBULAR PAROXYSMIA [H49] Strupp 2016 is excluded STRUCTURALLY by the lower bound of 20 minutes
   (its attacks last seconds) — yet note (2) of the probable form does not list it anyway.
 · "RECURRENT UNILATERAL VESTIBULOPATHY" in note (9) HAS NO DEFINITION AND NO REFERENCE IN THIS PAPER,
   and in 2015 no ICVD document existed for it. It is not the same as acute unilateral vestibulopathy
   and the names must not be merged.
 · Comment 4.6 (TIA and stroke) is an INDEPENDENT confirmation of the axis OTOREPO already carries via
   [H24] Edlow 2023 — unsurprisingly, since David E. Newman-Toker co-authored BOTH documents. New
   relative to today's card are its numbers: up to 42% of AICA infarctions are preceded by a
   premonitory TIA; most strokes after TIA fall within 90 days; in 15-20% of individuals the internal
   auditory artery arises directly from the basilar, so mixed symptoms may herald basilar occlusion.
   The sentence about vigilance is SUBORDINATE in the source, not coordinate: its TRUNK is a very
   short TOTAL illness duration (< 3-6 months, especially < 30 days), while "fewer than 5 episodes"
   and "a crescendo profile" are its subordinate clause joined by OR — they are not standalone red
   flags. Vascular risk factors and presentation to acute care are a separate, parallel sentence.

A LIMIT OF THE PROOF, WORTH RECORDING. This chapter CHANGES NOT ONE LINE OF PHYSICS and can be
neither confirmed nor refuted by the golden set — the snapshot guards engine values, and here no value
arises. The only gate that sees this chapter at all is `zrodla:check`, and it sees only that the
number [H20] agrees with its label, not that the content agrees with the paper. The substantive
verification was therefore performed DIRECTLY on the full text, by string searches with counted hits
(the numbers stand beside every claim above) — and will have to be repeated the same way if anyone
ever extends this chapter. This is the place for a criteria card wired into the `sEVS` leaf — exactly
where V28c wired the vestibular migraine card — and not a place for trust in memory.

[H17] Della Santina CC, Potyagaylo V, Migliaccio AA, Minor LB, Carey JP (2005). "Orientation of human
      semicircular canals measured by three-dimensional multiplanar CT reconstruction." J Assoc Res
      Otolaryngol 6(3):191–206. doi:10.1007/s10162-005-0003-x. PMID 16088383.
      → MEASURED (3D CT, 22 subjects / 44 labyrinths): the direction angles of the LATERAL canal's normal
        relative to Reid's stereotactic axes — X (nasal) 108.7°±7.5, Y (left) 92.2°±5.7,
        Z (superior) 19.9°±7.0. A Z angle of ≈ 20° means that the PLANE of the lateral canal is inclined
        ~20° relative to Reid's horizontal. The canals are closer to orthogonal than previously reported.
      → SIGNIFICANCE FOR THE MODEL (R3/R9): this is the MEASUREMENT against which the textbook "30° of
        inclination" is an overstatement; our IE-Map atlas gives 10.4° (~1.3 SD below that mean — a low
        normal, not an error). Because the clinical "raise the head by 30°" realizes the goal "the canal
        vertical" EXACTLY for a 30° anatomy, at 20° (this paper) ~20° suffices, and at 10.4° (our atlas) ~10°.
      → WHAT WAS NOT VERIFIED: the abstract does NOT contain an explicit comparison with the 30° canon (the
        sentence in R9 about "an explicitly noted difference" requires the full text).
      → in the NeuroVOR module: RA∥LP, LA∥RP coplanarity and canal orientations → COPLANAR / PLANE_CANALS (RALP/LARP).
      → NUMBERING NOTE: until 2026-08-16 the same work also carried a second number, [H29], on the
        main branch; the entries were merged here, and slot 29 now holds the Cochrane 2014 review.

[H19] Strupp M, Kim JS, Murofushi T, Straumann D, Jen JC, Rosengren SM, Della Santina CC, Kingma H (2017).

      Res 27(4):177–189.

      It corrects ONE line — the rotatory-chair threshold in criterion C (p. 179, section 3):
      gain ≤ 0.1 (was < 0.1), phase lead ≥ 15 degrees (was > 68), time constant ≤ 6 s
      (was < 5 s). The stimulus 0.1 Hz and Vmax 50°/s is UNCHANGED, and so is the conjunction.
      WHAT THE ERRATUM DOES NOT TOUCH: the abstract (which joins the same quantities with
      "and/or") nor the note on rotational testing (gain < 0.15 at 0.05–0.1 Hz and 60°/s).
      The conjunction divergence and the third set of figures REMAIN open. The full text of the
      erratum is in the corpus: icvd-korpus/zrodla-pelny-tekst/07b-strupp2023-bvp-ERRATA.txt

      → BVH: a bilateral loss → oscillopsia/instability, NO spontaneous nystagmus, a BILATERAL vHIT → bilateralLoss.
